# Changelog

## Unreleased

### Follow-up `windows-ci-races`: races the Windows leg lost under load
- **`tools/test-bundle-compare.mjs` C6: a multipart request to the child server leaves no tail unread** (a test fix; no runtime, `tools/lib`, golden or output change). C6 failed the `windows (node 22)` job of run 38737724941 (`not ok 810 - C6 retrofeed in the shim`, `fetch failed` at the `serverFetch` call in `same()`) on the tree that had passed at 7950b03. Its `multipart/form-data is not parsed` case POSTs a `FormData`, which fetch streams in parts: the headers and the first part in one write, the closing boundary in a later one. The server parses no multipart, so retrofeed answers its 404 from the headers alone, and with `connection: close` (which `serverFetch` sets since the C3 keep-alive fix) Node's server closes the socket right after the answer, the closing boundary unread: its TCP stack sends an RST behind the 404. Linux still hands the client the 404; Windows discards bytes the client had not read when the RST lands (`WSAECONNRESET`), and fetch, with no status line yet, fails. `serverFetch`, in this file and in `tools/test-bundle-compare-pairs.mjs` (the same text, C3 pins it), now builds the request through `new Request()` and hands fetch its body as one `ArrayBuffer`, so the body leaves in the headers' write and the server reads all of it before it closes; the bytes and the Content-Type, boundary included, are the ones fetch would send, the shim still receives the original `FormData`, and a GET sends no body. On Linux (node 22.22.2), 40 multipart requests to a real child server made the server's kernel send 26–29 RSTs before and 0 after; through a relay that discards unread data on an RST as Winsock does, `TypeError: fetch failed` (cause `ECONNRESET`) came back 1–5 times in 40 before and 0 after, and C6's other request kinds 0 either way. Under four busy loops, ten runs of the 40: 6–16 RSTs and 3–10 failures in each before, 0 and 0 in each after. C3 now also pins it: a `FormData` goes to fetch as one buffer, with the bytes fetch serializes it to, `connection: close`, and a GET with a null body — an assertion in an existing test (it fails against the previous `serverFetch`), so Suite total 1519 → 1519.

## 0.6.0 — 2026-10-15

**The store-and-seams release.** The store now holds the studio's state, not only its identities: the pack registry moved into the database; services, their environments and an org's MCP endpoints are records managed over HTTP; every change a principal makes through the server is an audit row that can be read; the home is the org's services table with a page per service; and Settings is the org's administration as a studio mode (STORE_PLAN slices 4 to 6). A downstream can vendor and rebadge Observogram without forking it: a manifest of the vendorable modules, the MCP transport hook, the artefact taxonomy classifier, reverse-proxy identity passthrough, one brand config and an embeddable static bundle, which now runs Compare and loads no font over the network. The rebadge batches built on those seams: a reviewer's verdicts, waivers and an exportable service audit report; canonical pack production, where a fresh crawl is a valid pack; a true-snapshot live pack behind a connection test; comparison identity modes; the MCP server's settings in the page. Spec 1.4 keeps a repository's operational alert rules and pairs them with the live ones. Windows has a CI leg, and a path there is compared by what it names, not by how it is spelled: a journey's org scope holds, and `packc store purge-org` decides the users file by what deleting the org's root takes. Also: a malformed JSON body is answered as JSON on every route, a server-run journey's notification reads only its org's hooks, the settings-policy deadline is per rule, and the id clocks time the pattern, not the machine.

### Follow-up `paths-volume-through-handle`: on Windows the store's path comparison reads a path's volume through a handle

- **On Node 22.16.x the store's offline checks place a path through a share again** (a fix; `server/store/paths.mjs`; Linux and macOS compare exactly as before). `comparedPath` placed a path reached through a share by a file id it shares with a directory of the path it is checked against, and judged one nothing places by its volume (the serial's low 32 bits), both read with `statSync`. libuv answers a stat by the path's name (`GetFileInformationByName`, Windows 11 24H2 and Server 2025) unless the path is reached through a share or its last name is a reparse point, which it opens and reads the volume's serial from (`FILE_FS_VOLUME_INFORMATION`); libuv 1.49.0 to 1.50.x (Node 22.16.x ships 1.49.2) declared `FILE_STAT_BASIC_INFORMATION` with `FileId128` before `VolumeSerialNumber`, swapped against Windows' layout (fixed in 1.51.0), so a stat by name reported the upper half of the file's 128-bit id as the device, 0 on NTFS. There a local path and a share of it had no file id in common, and a local volume never matched a share's: with the workspace at `C:\ws`, a directory export to `\\myhost\ws\exports\new` (which is `C:\ws\exports\new`), or to `C:\ws\exports\new` with the workspace named `\\myhost\ws`, compared as outside the workspace and was written into it, and so was one through a share of a directory under it (`\\myhost\exports`, which no file id places and which was refused as lying on the workspace's volume); the in-place detection and backup's own-file check missed the workspace and the database's `-wal` through a share alike, and purge-org refused a users file a share placed outside the root (a false refusal). On Windows a file id and a volume are now read through a handle opened on the path (`openSync`, `fstatSync`, `closeSync`; libuv opens a directory too), which reports the serial on every libuv, every handle closed before the comparison goes on; a path that does not open is passed over as one that did not stat was. `comparedPath` takes `openSync`, `fstatSync` and `closeSync` in place of `statSync`. `server/fixtures/ntfs.mjs` models both libuvs (`fastDev`: the device a stat by name reports — `null`, all 64 bits of the serial as 1.51.0 reads it, or `0n` as 1.49.2 does; a path through a share, or whose last name is a link or another reparse point, is opened, with the serial's low 32 bits) and returns `openSync`, `fstatSync`, `closeSync` and `opened()`; its header no longer says a local stat may report all 64 bits on every libuv. One more test in `server/test-store-paths.mjs` (1519 → 1520 tests): on libuv 1.51.0 and 1.49.2, a directory export through `\\myhost\ws` into `C:\ws`, and one on the drive into the workspace named through it, refused as inside it, the workspace through its share refused as the in-place export, a backup over the database's `-wal` through `\\myhost\dbdir` refused, purge-org refusing a users file placed under the root and purging with one placed beside it, a share nothing places on the workspace's volume refused, one on another volume written, and every handle closed; at the parent commit it fails on 1.49.2 (the export through `\\myhost\ws` was written). Every comparison the suite builds injects the fixture's handles.

### Follow-up `purge-org-users-file-identity`: purge-org decides the users file by what deleting the root takes
- **`packc store purge-org` decides whether it would delete the users file by what deleting the org's root takes, not by how the users file's path is spelled** (**a declared default change**, on every platform; `server/store/users-file-place.mjs`, new; `server/store/ops.mjs`). The users file a pre-store build reads — the one recorded at the import when `OBSERVOGRAM_USERS_FILE` was set then, else `<base>/users.json` — may have its bytes under the root while its path lies outside it, and the check compared the path alone (realpath, which never crosses a mount back to its source). So with org beta's root at `/srv/ws/orgs/beta` and the users file recorded as `/mnt/b/users.json`, `/mnt/b` a bind mount of `/srv/ws/orgs/beta/sub`, `packc store purge-org beta` deleted `/srv/ws/orgs/beta/sub/users.json` — named directly, through a file link (`/cfg/users.json` → `/mnt/b/users.json`) or through a directory link (`/cfg/d` → `/mnt/b`); an NFS or SMB loopback mount of such a directory is the same hole on another filesystem; and with nothing recorded, a `<base>/users.json` that is a link into the root was deleted alike. purge-org now walks the root as `rmSync` takes it — `lstatSync` each entry, every directory listed, never through a link (a symlink, or a junction on Windows) — and follows the users file's path through every link (`statSync`). The users file lies under the root when an entry is the same file: the same device and inode (on Windows the volume serial's low 32 bits, all a share reports, as `server/store/paths.mjs` places a path), or, on another device, the same size and modification time, compared at the coarser of the two clocks' precision — a view of it through another filesystem: an NFS or SMB mount reports the server's inode under another device (SMB keeps 100 ns), an overlay mount another device and, with `xino` or once the file is copied up into an upper directory under the root, another inode too, and a FUSE mount without inode numbers (sshfs without `-o use_ino`) another inode, in whole seconds. The refusal names every such entry, hard links included, so one move clears them: `the recorded users file <path> lies under <root>: it is <entry> there — nothing was deleted. Move <entry> out of <root>, then run purge-org again. If <path> then no longer leads to the users file, make it lead there again before a pre-store build or the in-place packc store export uses it` (`the users file <base>/users.json (the one a pre-store build reads: OBSERVOGRAM_USERS_FILE was not set at the import) …` when none was recorded; for a view, `<entry> under it has its size and modification time on another filesystem (the users file seen through a network or overlay mount, or a copy that kept them)`, its way out with a second step after `then run purge-org again`: `; if it then refuses because <path> does not exist (a mount whose directories show another inode or modification time than theirs under <root>, as an overlay mount with an upper directory does, cannot be traced back there), delete <root> by hand, then run purge-org again: it drops org <id>'s records`). The way out holds followed literally: once no entry under the root is the users file, the purge goes through when the recorded path still leads to the users file (a hard link or a copy moved out) or leads under the root, where nothing of it is left (below); through a view whose directories show another inode or modification time — an overlay mount with an upper directory shows that directory's, not those of the lower one under the root (reproduced with a real overlay, `xino` on or off) — nothing traces the path back, so purge-org refuses it as a users file that does not exist, and deleting the root by hand, the second step the view's refusal names, lets purge-org drop the org's records. Declared false refusals, each with that way out: a hard link under the root to a users file outside it (`rmSync` removes only the link), and a copy of it under the root on another device that kept its size and modification time (`cp -p`, `rsync -t`, `tar`).
- **A recorded users file that does not exist is decided by where its path leads, so `move it out first` works** (**a declared default change**). `the recorded users file <path> lies under <root> — move it out first; nothing was deleted` came back word for word after the move: the record still names the path under the root, realpath falls back to the path as written, and the check refused again. The comparison by path now holds only while the recorded users file exists. One that does not is followed as the kernel resolves a path — every link that exists followed, a dangling one by its target — to the nearest directory that exists there: when that is a directory under the root (the same device and inode, or the view rule above) and no entry under the root has the users file's name (below), nothing of the users file is there to delete and the purge goes on — a users file recorded under the root and moved out, or reached through a bind mount of a directory under the root, a view that shows that directory's own inode and modification time, or a link into it (on Windows, not a dangling link: a path through a link whose target cannot be opened is refused before this check, by `purge-org-unresolvable-links`' check in `server/store/paths.mjs` below, whose way out — deleting the root by hand — holds). Any other is refused, failing closed (but one whose path runs only through directories the root, or its path, lies in, below), since a filesystem not mounted now may lead it under the root: with the bind mount above down, its mount point is an empty directory, and purge-org deleted `/srv/ws/orgs/beta/sub/users.json` behind it. The refusal: `the recorded users file <path> does not exist, and nothing on this host tells where its path leads[ (to <place>)]: the nearest part of it that exists, <dir>, is no directory under <root>, and a filesystem not mounted now may lead it under <root> — nothing was deleted. If it lies on a filesystem that is not mounted now, mount it, then run purge-org again; if it does not lie under <root>, delete <root> by hand, then run purge-org again: it drops org <id>'s records`. A declared false refusal: a recorded users file outside the root that no longer exists (deleted on purpose after the upgrade) is refused — unless its path runs only through directories the root, or its path, lies in (below) — and its way out holds — with no root left, purge-org checks no users file and drops the records. With nothing recorded, an absent `<base>/users.json` — nothing at its path, not even a link (a store that keeps no local users may have none) — is nothing to keep: the purge goes on, as before. A `<base>/users.json` that is a link whose users file does not exist is followed as a recorded one is: with `<base>/users.json` → `/mnt/b/users.json` and the bind mount above down, it was taken for absent and purge-org deleted `/srv/ws/orgs/beta/sub/users.json`; now it is refused, as `the users file <base>/users.json (the one a pre-store build reads: OBSERVOGRAM_USERS_FILE was not set at the import) does not exist, …`, with the same ways out.
- **A users file purge-org cannot read is refused** (**a declared default change**: the comparison fell back to the path as written, and the purge went through). `statSync` failing otherwise than that it does not exist — `EACCES` on a directory of its path for the user purge-org runs as, a link loop or a chain of more than 40 links — or a part of its path that cannot be read: `the recorded users file <path> cannot be read on this host (EACCES[ at <part>]), so nothing tells whether it lies under <root> — nothing was deleted. Run purge-org as a user who can read <part>; or, if it does not lie under <root>, delete <root> by hand, then run purge-org again: it drops org <id>'s records` (for a loop, `ELOOP: a link loop, or a chain of more than 40 links`, with the second way out alone).
- **purge-org reads the whole root before it deletes it, and refuses a mount point under it or a directory it cannot read** (**declared default changes**). On Node 22 `rmSync` stops part-way at a mount point (`EBUSY`); on Node 23 and later it first deletes the files of the filesystem mounted there; and a directory mounted under the root from outside it may hold the users file (purge-org deleted a users file outside the root whose directory was mounted under it, on Node 23 and later). A mount point is a directory on another filesystem than the directory that holds it, on Linux an entry `/proc/self/mountinfo` lists (a bind mount of a directory of the same filesystem, which `lstatSync` cannot tell from a directory: with `/srv/x` bind-mounted at `<root>/m`, `rmSync` deleted `/srv/x`'s files on Node 23 and later and then failed with `EBUSY` (Node 22 failed there with `EBUSY`), and when `/srv/x` held the users file, the `lies under` refusal named `<root>/m/users.json` to move out, and after the move the purge deleted the rest of `/srv/x` the same way; or a file mounted on a file; matched by the root's real path, as the kernel lists mount points; not one a later mount covers — mounted in the same parent mount over a directory above it, or covering that parent so — which the kernel lists too while its path shows a directory of the filesystem mounted above, where nothing is mounted: with a tmpfs mounted on `<base>` over a bind mount at `<root>/m`, the workspace made on the tmpfs purges, where `umount <root>/m` says `not mounted` — unless that path leads through a mount of the filesystem the covered mount lies in (its parent mount's device, as `/proc/self/mountinfo` lists it, or, where it does not list that mount — a chroot into a directory that is no mount point — the filesystem `/` shows, compared with the directory at the covered mount's path now), where it may show the very directory the covered mount is mounted on, which `rmdir` refuses while it is a mount point: with `mount --bind <base> <base>` over a bind mount at `<root>/m` (an fstab line mounted out of order), or `mount --rbind <base> <base>` and its copy at `<root>/m` unmounted as the refusal said, `rmSync` deleted part of the root, then failed there with `EBUSY` and the org's records stayed, and every later run failed the same way; such a mount is refused, `<root>/m (the same filesystem as <root>, listed in /proc/self/mountinfo, covered by a later mount through which its path may show what it is mounted on: first unmount <base>)`, naming the mounts in a covering mount before it (a declared false refusal where that mount shows another directory there — the workspace's, with the deployment bind-mounted over the directory that holds the covered mount: followed literally, `umount <base>` took the workspace away and purge-org, run again, said `no database at <base>/observogram.db — nothing to purge`, so the refusal goes on `; if the workspace goes with it (a mount that showed another directory there than the one it is mounted on — the workspace's), mount it there again once <root>/m is unmounted, before you run purge-org again`, and followed so — `umount <base>`, `umount <root>/m`, the workspace mounted on `<base>` again — the purge passes, reproduced with real mounts in a private mount namespace); a mount on `/` itself covers nothing, since a path is looked up from the process's root and never crosses a mount stacked on it: with a tmpfs mounted on `/` in a chroot, or `mount --bind / /`, a bind mount at `<root>/m` is refused still, where `rmSync` failed with `EBUSY`), a directory the walk reached already (one directory mounted at a second place under the root, a loop among them), or on Windows a folder another volume is mounted in; any other entry on another filesystem than its directory is one only as `/proc/self/mountinfo` lists it, since an overlay mount whose layers lie on different filesystems (`xino=off`) shows its directories on its own device and a lower layer's files on that layer's, with nothing mounted there (a workspace in one, its lower layer on tmpfs and its upper on ext4, purges); the root itself mounted (a volume per org) is refused alike: `<root> holds a mount point, <entry> (another filesystem than <dir>; or the same filesystem as <dir>, listed in /proc/self/mountinfo): deleting <root> would take the files of the filesystem mounted there (Node 23 and later) or stop part-way at it (Node 22), and a directory mounted from outside <root> may hold the users file — nothing was deleted. Unmount what is mounted there, then run purge-org again` (with the users file under the root as well, it names that too: below), and for the root, `… is a mount point: … Unmount it, then run purge-org again: it deletes what is left at <root> and drops org <id>'s records, and the unmounted filesystem's files stay`. A directory under the root it cannot read was where `rmSync` failed part-way; now nothing is deleted (an unreadable `/proc/self/mountinfo` is refused alike): `<dir> under <root> cannot be read (EACCES), so purge-org cannot tell what deleting <root> takes — nothing was deleted. Run purge-org as a user who can read it; or, if the recorded users file <path> does not lie under <root>, delete <root> by hand as such a user, then run purge-org again: it drops org <id>'s records` (for another error, such as `ENOTCONN` from a FUSE mount whose server stopped answering: `If it is a filesystem mounted there that stopped answering, unmount it, then run purge-org again; or, if …, delete <root> by hand, then …`).
- **purge-org reads each directory under the root again before it goes on, and refuses one that changed while it read the root** (**a declared default change**; `server/store/users-file-place.mjs`). The walk read each directory once, at its own moment, so an entry another process renamed while it ran, from a directory not read yet into one read already, was in neither listing: with the users file recorded as `/mnt/b/users.json`, `/mnt/b` a bind mount of `<root>/z/sub`, `<root>/z/sub` renamed into `<root>/a` once `<root>/a` was read hid the users file, and `rmSync` deleted it (reproduced on ext4 with a real bind mount, the rename made at that step of the walk). Now, before a purge whose users file exists goes on, each directory the walk read is read again — the same directory (device and inode), the same change time, which moves with every entry added, removed or renamed in it, and the same names — and one that changed is refused: `<dir> under <root> changed while purge-org read <root> (an entry added, removed or renamed in it, or a mount over it), so purge-org cannot tell what deleting <root> takes — nothing was deleted. Stop what changes files under <root>, then run purge-org again` (the root itself: `<root> changed while purge-org read it …`). Run again with nothing changing it, the walk finds the users file and refuses it as lying under the root. `server/test-store-ops.mjs` (one more, over `server/fixtures/mount-fs.mjs`, whose directories now carry a change time that moves with each entry added, removed or renamed in them, and whose `coarse` clock keeps every change time the same): the rename into a directory read already, or into the root, refused by the change time and the names, and on a coarse clock by the names; a rename on, before the second reading, that leaves the names as the walk found them, refused by the change time; on a coarse clock, a mount over a directory read already holding the same names, refused by its inode. Suite total 1510 → 1511 tests.
- **purge-org reads a name under the root that is not valid UTF-8** (**a declared default change**: a refusal where it purged; `server/store/users-file-place.mjs`). The walk listed each directory as strings, and `node:fs` gives a name that is not valid UTF-8 with U+FFFD in place of each such byte (`c\xffd` as `c�d`), a name no lookup finds: `lstatSync` of it failed with `ENOENT`, which the walk took for an entry gone while it ran, so it read nothing under it, while `rmSync`, which lists names as bytes, deleted all of it. With the users file recorded as `/cfg/users.json`, a link to `<root>/c\xffd/users.json` (or `<root>/run\xe9/inner/users.json`), purge-org deleted it, as `develop` does (reproduced on ext4); and a mount point below such a name went unseen, and Node 22's `rmSync` failed there with `EBUSY` (reproduced with a bind mount from the root's own filesystem and with a tmpfs). The walk now lists each name as the bytes the filesystem holds and follows one that is not valid UTF-8, and every path under it, as bytes; a refusal names such a path as a POSIX shell's `$'…'` spells it, each such byte `\xNN`, so it can be typed: `the recorded users file /cfg/users.json lies under <root>: it is $'<root>/c\xffd/users.json' there — nothing was deleted. Move $'<root>/c\xffd/users.json' out of <root>, then run purge-org again. …`. Followed literally in a shell (`mv $'<root>/c\xffd/users.json' /keep/`), the purge passes and the moved file stays; a mount point below such a name is refused as one, `$'<root>/c\xffd/inner/m' (another filesystem than $'<root>/c\xffd/inner')` or `(the same filesystem as …, listed in /proc/self/mountinfo)`, and passes once unmounted. A name that is valid UTF-8 is read and named as before. `server/test-store-ops.mjs` (one more, over `server/fixtures/mount-fs.mjs`, which now spells a byte that is not part of a UTF-8 character as U+DCNN, takes a path as a Buffer, and lists names as `node:fs` does — as strings, each such byte U+FFFD, or with `{ encoding: 'buffer' }` as bytes): the users file in such a directory reached through a link, refused, and the way out followed, also with a quote and a backslash in the path, escaped (`$'<root>/it\'s\\c\xffd/users.json'`; the fixture keeps a backslash in a name on POSIX paths); with the users file outside, a tree of such names, purged; below such a name a directory from another filesystem mounted there, a same-filesystem bind mount `/proc/self/mountinfo` lists (also under a root whose path is not ASCII, `/srv/données/…`), and a directory it cannot read, refused. Suite total 1511 → 1512 tests.
- **purge-org refuses an entry under the root with the users file's name, which a mount that is down leads to once it is back** (**a declared default change**: a refusal where it purged; `server/store/users-file-place.mjs`). Failing closed through a mount that is down held only while the users file did not exist, and a mount that is down keeps what was written in its mount point meanwhile: with the users file recorded as `/mnt/b/users.json`, `/mnt/b` a bind mount of `<root>/sub` that is down, and a `users.json` written in `/mnt/b` meanwhile, the path led to that file, outside the root, and purge-org deleted `<root>/sub/users.json`, the users file the path leads to once the mount is back (reproduced on ext4 with a real bind mount, as `develop` does). Now, when the users file exists and no entry is it, an entry under the root with its name — the name its path ends in, as written or at each link it leads through, compared ignoring case and Unicode normalization (as a case-insensitive filesystem may look it up) — is refused, on every platform, recorded or `<base>/users.json` alike: `the recorded users file <path> is no file under <root>, but <entry> under it has its name: a mount that is down keeps what was written in its mount point meanwhile, and mounted again it may lead the path there — nothing was deleted. Move <entry> out of <root>, then run purge-org again; if it then refuses because <path> does not exist (the users file seen there through a mount that cannot be traced back), delete <root> by hand, then run purge-org again: it drops org <id>'s records. …`, ending, as the refusal of a users file under the root does, with making the path lead to a moved users file again. An entry of that name that is a link names what it leads to under the root too, which `rmSync` deletes once the link is moved. Followed literally — the entry moved out, the mount down or up — the purge passes and the moved file stays; mounted again first, the refusal of a users file under the root comes, with the same way out. A declared false refusal: an entry under the root with the users file's name that is not it (moving it out is the way out). A view of a directory under the root that reports neither the inode nor the size and modification time (some FUSE filesystems) shows its names, so the users file seen through it, which passed, is refused by its name. `server/test-store-ops.mjs` (one more, over `server/fixtures/mount-fs.mjs`): through a mount that is down whose mount point holds a file of its name, refused — named directly, through a file link, through a link of another name, in another case or Unicode normalization, and, none recorded, `<base>/users.json` a link there — and a link of that name under the root naming what it leads to; mounted again, refused as lying under the root; moved out, passing with the mount up or down; and an entry of that name below a name that is not valid UTF-8, refused naming it as `$'…'` spells it and passing once moved out. The place suite's users file outside the root now has a name no entry under the root has (`/srv/accounts.json` and the like), a fixture whose names match is refused by the name, and the FUSE view's case expects that refusal. Suite total 1512 → 1513 tests.
- **A users file moved out of an org's root as its refusal said no longer refuses every later purge of another org** (**a declared default change**: a purge where it refused; `server/store/users-file-place.mjs`). Once that org was purged, the record still named the path in its root, which no longer exists, and every purge of another org was refused as a recorded users file that does not exist — `… the nearest part of it that exists, <base>/orgs, is no directory under <base>/orgs/beta …` — until the path led to the moved file again, which meant re-creating directories in the purged org's root; neither the refusal nor README said so. Now a users file that does not exist, whose path is looked up only in directories the root lies in — its parent and theirs, on the root's real path — or that the root's own path, `<base>/orgs/<id>`, is looked up in (with a workspace, or its `orgs/`, reached through a link in a directory the root does not lie in, that directory and those above it — `/home/u` and `/home` with `OBSERVOGRAM_WORKSPACE=/home/u/ws`, a link to `/data/ws`), each by its path (case folded on Windows) and its device and inode, while no entry under the root has the name its path ends in (as written or at each link it leads through, ignoring case and Unicode normalization), is nothing to keep, and the purge goes on: a filesystem mounted again on one of those directories covers the root too, or hides the link the root's own path is reached through, so through it the path leads under the root by a directory under the root mounted in it, which shows the users file by its name, or by a link purge-org does not see (below). A recorded users file deleted in one of those directories passes alike. With an entry of that name under the root it is refused as before — `<base>/orgs` a bind mount of `<root>/x` that is down, the users file written through it at `<root>/x/gamma/sub/users.json` (with a real bind mount, this rule without the name check purged it) — and so is a path looked up in any other directory (a link in `/cfg` into the purged directory), with the same ways out. `server/test-store-ops.mjs` (one more, on the real filesystem through `purgeOrg`): the users file recorded under acme's root refused, moved out as the refusal says, acme purged, then beta removed and purged, the moved file kept, with the workspace named by its real path, through a directory link to it in a directory the roots do not lie in (a junction on Windows), and with its `orgs/` such a link — refused without this rule, and through either link with only the directories on the root's real path. Unchanged: the Windows refusal of a path it cannot place runs first.

### Follow-up `compare-in-bundle`: Compare in the static bundle
- **One collation for every `tools/lib` sort** (**a declared default change**, on a server too): each of the 23 `localeCompare` calls in `artefact-model.mjs`, `crawler.mjs`, `diff.mjs`, `library.mjs`, `pack-conformance.mjs`, `promql-canon.mjs`, `promql.mjs`, `traceability-graph.mjs` and `waivers.mjs` passes `'en'` — the CLDR root order, which English does not tailor — where it passed nothing and so followed the runtime's default collation (Node's follows `LC_ALL`; a browser's, its language). The diff, the traceability graph, canonical PromQL and the identity keys and behaviour hashes built from it, the crawl, the library build, the waivers list and the placeholder report now order text the same under every process and browser locale. Nothing else in the nine modules changes; their exports are unchanged; `VENDOR-MANIFEST.json` is regenerated (nine hashes). A process under `C`, `C.UTF-8` or `en_*` answers byte for byte what it did (every golden unchanged); one whose locale tailors Latin letters (Czech, Danish, Estonian, Lithuanian, Hungarian, Slovak and the like) answered differently before (under `LC_ALL=cs_CZ.UTF-8` eight diff goldens failed and the traceability graph moved) and now answers the same. **On such a server**, an artefact whose identity key holds canonical PromQL, or a sorted list that now sorts differently, gets a new key, so a verdict recorded on it before the upgrade does not carry at the pack's next re-registration. Migration: check the collation where the server runs (`node -p "new Intl.Collator().resolvedOptions().locale"` in its environment); `en`, `en-US` or `und` — nothing moves. Otherwise, after each pack's first re-registration on this version, read its `verdict.carry` audit row (`dropped`, `droppedCount`) and record those verdicts again. Answers that are not stored only re-order. Guarded: `tools/test-vendor-manifest.mjs` refuses a `localeCompare(` or `Intl.Collator(` call anywhere under `tools/lib` that does not pass `'en'`, naming `file:line` (one more test in `tools/test-vendor-manifest.mjs`; 1476 → 1477 tests); `tools/test-golden-diff.mjs` hashes the `/api/diff` body shape (the diff and the graph, which no golden covers) of three pairs, and `diffPacks` of the `payment-service.vs.production-curated` golden pair, in a child under each of `C`, `cs_CZ.UTF-8`, `da_DK.UTF-8`, `et_EE.UTF-8` and `lt_LT.UTF-8`, and requires every hash equal (a harness check, no new test; where Node ignores `LC_ALL`, as on Windows, it passes trivially and the static guard still holds every call).

The default is unchanged, so the server (at start) and the bundle builder (at bake: `--taxonomy`, or `OBSERVOGRAM_TAXONOMY` on the build machine) time every pattern against the four adversarial ids as before. `studio/taxonomy.mjs` `bindTaxonomy` compiles the document it is served with `{ timed: false }` — the server timed it at start, the builder at bake — and every static check (anchoring, length, flags, nested quantifiers) still runs in the browser. A browser that ran the patterns over the 50 ms budget (a slow phone, a throttled tab) bound the default families instead, saying so only through `state.taxonomyBindError`; it now binds the served override. `VENDOR-MANIFEST.json` regenerated (exports unchanged by name). One more test in `tools/test-artefact-classify.mjs` (1477 → 1478 tests): with every clock a runtime offers (`Date.now`, `performance.now`, the process's CPU time) stepping past the budget on each read, `compileTaxonomy` and `validateTaxonomy` refuse both taxonomy fixtures as too slow by default and accept them with `{ timed: false }` (an unanchored pattern still refused), `bindTaxonomy` binds the served override, and the builder's `loadTaxonomyFile` still refuses.
- **Deploy in the static bundle is explained, and every deploy control in Compare, Diagnose and Remediate is drawn unavailable with the reason** (**a declared default change**, the static posture only; the live studio is unchanged). A static bundle has no server to deploy through, and its Deploy opened the deploy modal, whose every read answered 501. `openDeployModal` (`studio/app.mjs`) now explains instead, from every entry point, prototype space included: `Deploy needs the <product> server; this studio is a static bundle built without one.` — the shim's 501 sentence — as a toast and to the screen reader. Every deploy control outside prototype space is also drawn unavailable with that sentence (`aria-disabled`, not faded — the reason line is what marks it, and at reduced opacity it reads below AA —, a filled button without its fill — under the pointer too —, the reason inside the control, the click explaining — each zone's rule in its own sheet: `ux-diagnose.css`, `ux-remediate.css`, the Compare zone's in `app.css`): in Diagnose and its Compare, the drift action `⇪ Deploy repository changes to live`, the chain cards' deploy and the pack cards' deploy; in Remediate, the decision header's `Deploy repository artefacts to live` and `Review and deploy … to live`, the active strategy's `Review and deploy … to live ↗` beside its proposed changes, the response path's per-SLO `Deploy <identity> (N rows)` and the compiler's `Review and deploy to live ↗`. Until Compare is answered in the bundle (its entry below), a bundle reaches two of them, both Remediate's with no Pack B: the decision header's `Deploy repository artefacts to live` and the active strategy's `Review and deploy repository artefacts to live ↗`; the compiler's button is drawn only where the server's deploy matrix says the target deploys, and the others only beside a comparison. In a bundle no target deploys (the shim answers the matrix with a `501`), so the compiler's Deploy to live card gives that sentence as its reason, where it said `No deploy path is configured for this target.` — a configuration its reader cannot change; a generated board the pack does not declare, which said only `declare it in spec.dashboards[] to deploy and verify it` — a step its reader cannot take here, and one that deploys nothing without a server —, now gives that sentence too, then that on that server it deploys once the pack declares it in `spec.dashboards[]` (a format the studio never deploys keeps its own reason, true in both postures: hand the file to its platform), and the board's subtitle in the compiler's list, in the list item's title and over its preview, which ended with the same words, now ends `deploying it needs the <product> server, and the pack declaring it in spec.dashboards[]` (`staticRemedy`'s `compiler-generated-board`, row 29; S3 holds the compile catalogue's subtitle to ending with that way out, S4's pattern also finds `deploy and verify` — the ledger gives the card's live reason row 29 —, C8's copy follows, and C8 reads the three on the generated board). `studio/static-remedies.mjs` (new; a pure view model, zero imports; the live studio loads it at boot, imported by `app.mjs`, `compare-view.mjs` and `compile-view.mjs`): `serverNeeded(feature, product)` and `DEPLOY_CONTROLS`. `tools/test-static-remedies.mjs` (new, 2; `npm run test:static-remedies`; 1478 → 1480 tests): S1 holds `serverNeeded` to the shim's `denialText` for every feature the shim names, unbranded and branded; S2 holds the seven non-prototype `openDeployModal(` call sites to their controls, `DEPLOY_CONTROLS` to the id, class or `data-*` attribute each source writes, each drawing view to marking them under an `isStatic()` test once it has rendered, and `openDeployModal` to explaining before it touches the modal — a new deploy entry point fails until it is classified.
- **Every bundle carries Observogram's MIT licence** (**a declared default change**, the bundle's bytes): `tools/build-studio-bundle.mjs` writes one `<script type="text/plain" id="observogram-licenses">` element at the end of `<head>` — never executed or rendered, kept by a minifier that strips comments, read as its `textContent` — holding a preamble that says what the file contains, `==== Observogram <version> — MIT ====` with `LICENSE` verbatim, and the spec's provenance: the schema every bundle embeds (and any pack built in from the spec's examples) comes from the repository and commit `vendor/observability-pack-spec/VERSIONS.json` records, which publishes no licence file — the block says so and claims none for it. One comment line right after the doctype points a human opening the file at the element, worded for what the file holds (until Compare's entry below brings the first package, `<!-- Licence: this file contains Observogram (MIT) and no third-party package; its full text is in the … element in the head. -->`). The block is a pure function of the tree — two builds with one `builtAt` stay byte-identical — and no path of the build machine enters it. The build refuses a tree without `LICENSE` (`LICENSE is missing from <root> — every bundle carries Observogram's licence; copy LICENSE with the tree`), a licence text holding `</script` or `<!--` (it would end the element or change how it is parsed; a licence is reproduced as written, never escaped) and a `VERSIONS.json` that names no upstream repository and commit. `verifyBundleNotices(html)` (exported by the builder) → `{ ok, packages, problems }` checks a finished file — the build's own, or one a pipeline rewrote after it: exactly one element, `text/plain`, in `<head>`; Observogram's section with the MIT grant and warranty disclaimer; for every import-map key that is not a studio or `tools/lib` module (a bundled package), one section with its name, version and licence id, a `File:` line naming that key and a licence text, and no section for a package the map does not carry; the pointer comment, where the file still has it, worded for what the map carries. Comments stripped and attributes reordered are not damage. It does not compare the texts with an installed tree. The human summary line and `--json` are unchanged. `tools/test-bundle-compare.mjs` (new, 1; `npm run test:bundle-compare`; 1480 → 1481 tests): C2 builds a two-pack bundle through the CLI and checks the element's exact text and place, the pointer comment, that no build-machine path is in the file, `verifyBundleNotices`'s acceptance, each problem it names on a damaged file and its acceptance of a minified one, the package-section format from a synthetic package, and the tree's refusals; `tools/test-studio-bundle.mjs` T3 checks the element and the comment in a built bundle and T4b that the block carries no clock.
- **npm packages from the lockfile** (`tools/build-studio-bundle.mjs`; `--json` gains a last key, **a declared change**; no bundle's bytes move at this entry): a `tools/lib` module may import an npm package by its bare name when `VENDOR-MANIFEST.json` lists that name in the module's `npm` field — `collectModuleGraph(root, entries, { read, npmOf })` records the import as `{ spec, key: spec, package: spec }` and gathers `graph.packages`, reading the manifest at the first bare import it meets. The bundle then inlines that package and every package it imports (`collectBundledPackages(root, roots)`, breadth-first through `dependencies` and `peerDependencies`), each as one import-map entry under its own name: the bytes of its ES module entry (`exports.import`, or `exports["."].import`; never `main`, `module` or a `require` file) as `npm ci` installed them from `package-lock.json`, base64 of the bytes read, nothing rewritten. The licence block names each, in code-point order — `==== <name> <version> — <SPDX id> ====`, a `File:` line, the `Source:` repository its `package.json` publishes (https, no userinfo; omitted otherwise), the distinct copyright lines its entry file carries, then each licence file (`LICENSE`, `LICENCE*`, `COPYING*`) and each `NOTICE` file at the published package's root, verbatim — and the preamble and the pointer comment take their wording for a file with packages. The build reads the lockfile's entries and the files they name: it resolves no module, consults no package's source repository and compares no installed file with the registry's, so a patched install is embedded as installed and unmarked. It refuses, with a fix for whoever runs it: a bare import the module's npm field does not list (`… a package is bundled only when VENDOR-MANIFEST.json lists it in this module's npm field (a package.json dependency; npm run vendor-manifest)`), or a file inside a package (`… a package is bundled by its own name (<name>), never a file inside it`); a missing or unparsable `VENDOR-MANIFEST.json`; a missing or unparsable `package-lock.json`, or one older than lockfileVersion 2; a package the lockfile has no entry for; a second copy npm installed where an importing package loads it instead of the hoisted one (`… add "overrides": { "<name>": "<hoisted version>" } to package.json (npm 8.3 or newer), run npm install, and commit the lockfile` — a nested copy anywhere else changes nothing the bundle loads and builds); a package not installed, installed at another version or declaring another licence than the lockfile pins (`… run npm ci there (npm ci --omit=dev on a build machine that needs no dev tools)`); no ES module entry, or an entry file missing; an import its `package.json` does not declare, an import of a file, or of `node:*`; no licence file, or no licence id; a licence, NOTICE or copyright text holding `</script` or `<!--`; a package named `lib` or `studio`. `buildStudioBundle` returns `thirdParty: [{ name, version, license, entry, bytes }]` and `--json` prints it as its last key (names, versions, SPDX ids, entry files, bytes — no path); the human summary line is unchanged. A build whose module graph imports a package therefore needs, in the tree it builds from, `package-lock.json`, `VENDOR-MANIFEST.json` and an `npm ci`; no module in the bundle's graph imports one at this entry, so every bundle's bytes, and what its build reads, are unchanged. The live studio stays package-free — it loads `tools/lib` through the server's `/lib` mount, which has no import map — and `tools/test-studio-bundle.mjs` T1 now holds its graph (from `studio/app.mjs`) to no package, naming the import chain of any it reaches (a view importing the traceability graph would reach the PromQL parser's package: checked), beside synthetic cases for an accepted, an unlisted and a subpath import; T3 holds the import map to the graph's modules and the bundled packages, `thirdParty` and `--json`'s last key, and a two-pack bundle to under 6,000,000 bytes (a dependency that enters the graph unexpectedly is caught where it lands). One more test in `tools/test-bundle-compare.mjs` (1481 → 1482 tests): C1b, over synthetic roots in a tmpdir, a closure that builds — each entry file's bytes as installed, licence and NOTICE files, copyright lines, the source repository — with two nested copies that change nothing (under an unrelated package, under a dev-only one), and every refusal's exact text.
- **Retrofeed in the bundle** (**a declared default change**, the static bundle only): `POST /api/packs/:id/retrofeed` — Compare's repository update from live — is answered by `studio/static-backend.mjs`, the one non-GET it answers (it was 501 `Compare needs the <product> server; …`). `server/index.mjs`'s handler, ported: A's 404, the body as the server's parsers left it (`req.body || {}`, so a string, an array or no body reach it as on the server), B's 404, the diff without the traceability graph and without the scopeMode annotation fallback, the `keys` filter by base identity, `retrofeedShadowSignals` and the schema tripwire against the bundle's schema — computed in the browser over two packs the bundle carries, written nowhere. The engines (`tools/lib/diff.mjs`, `tools/lib/retrofeed.mjs`, already in the bundle's graph, so no byte of the bundle moves for them) load at the first call through literal dynamic imports; a failed import is not kept, so the next call retries; `createStaticBackend(config, { clock })` is the time that stamps `observogram.retrofeed.adoptedAt`. The body is read as the server's three parsers read it (`readRequestBody(request)`, exported): `application/json` strictly — an empty body is `{}`; blank, `null`, a number or a string 400 `the request body is not valid JSON`; a charset not starting `utf-` 415; over 16 MiB 413 —, `text/plain`, `text/yaml` and `application/x-yaml` as text (over 4 MiB 413), a form by qs's depth-0 rules in `utf-8` or `iso-8859-1` (another charset 415; over 64 KiB, or over 1000 parameters, 413), any other type, or none, as no body; each refusal with the server's status, text and headers — `Cache-Control: no-store` and `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, but on a 413, which keeps the server's crawler hint and sets neither, as the server's `bodyParserError` answers it. Not ported, and none of it is what the studio sends (`application/json`, UTF-8): a `Content-Encoding` (the server inflates gzip, deflate and br), a JSON body in a UTF charset other than UTF-8 (read as UTF-8), and a text body's charset (the server refuses one its decoder does not know). Any other method on `…/retrofeed`, a GET included, is `This action` (`PACK_FEATURES` loses `retrofeed`), as the server has no other route there. A computing route answers 500 naming it when the bundle bakes a taxonomy the page's classifier does not hold (the studio's bind failed in that browser): an answer classified by the default families would not be the one a server with that taxonomy gives. **Each answer of the shim's JSON helper carries the reason phrase a server sends** (**a declared default change**: `studio/api.mjs` reads a shim error as `404 Not Found on …` where it read `404  on …`; a `denied` 501 reads as before). The studio's retrofeed entry points appear only beside a comparison, which the bundle does not answer at this entry; in the static posture the result's note says how a reader of a bundle learns whether the gap closed — `staticRemedy('retrofeed-result', { product })` in `studio/static-remedies.mjs`, the brand's product named — where it said to re-scan (the live studio's note is unchanged). DOWNSTREAM §10's table names retrofeed among what the bundle answers, and its denial cell reads `every non-GET but retrofeed` and drops retrofeed from Compare's routes. One more test in `tools/test-bundle-compare.mjs` and one more in `tools/test-static-remedies.mjs` (1482 → 1484 tests): C6 holds the shim to a child server — status, reason phrase, content type, `Cache-Control`, `nosniff` and raw text — for three computed pairs with and without `keys` (the shim's clock pinned to the instant the server stamped), a pack that annotates its scopeMode as A (no fallback), the two 404s and every body rule above, then meets what only the shim can (a `--pack-url` pack that failed to load, an engine import that fails once and is retried, a baked taxonomy not bound); S3 holds the note's static text to its words for two products and to `runRetrofeed`'s `isStatic()` branch. `tools/test-studio-bundle.mjs`: T1 holds the shim's literal `import()` of the two engines, T3c the inverse lists, T6 the answered POST, `This action` for any other method and the reason phrases.
- **Compare in the static bundle** (**a declared default change**, the static bundle — its bytes, its build and its answers): `GET /api/diff` is answered by `studio/static-backend.mjs` (it was 501 `Compare needs the <product> server; …`). `server/index.mjs`'s handler, ported line for line: `a` and `b` each one non-empty value (absent, empty or repeated: the 400), both packs looked up and A's 404 answered before B's, `aEnv`/`bEnv` and `service` a non-empty value or none, `scopeMode` the query's when non-empty, else Pack A's `observogram.diff.scopeMode` annotation (then the legacy `tomograph.diff.scopeMode`), no schema validation (the server's route has none), and `{ ...diffPacks(…), traceabilityGraph: comparePackBranches(…) }` — computed in the browser over two packs the bundle carries. The engines (`tools/lib/diff.mjs` and `tools/lib/traceability-graph.mjs`) load at Compare's first call through literal dynamic imports (`createStaticBackend(config, { importDiffEngines })` is the seam); a failed import answers 500 with its message and is retried at the next call; the computing-route taxonomy guard applies. A non-GET to `/api/diff` is `This action` (`FEATURES` loses Compare). The notice reads `Static studio — no <product> server behind this page. Discover, Diagnose, Compare, Compile and conformance read the <n> packs built in; Scan a repo, Draft from MCP, Deploy, Journeys, Build and sign-in need the server.` Pair by (name, id) works in a bundle with no client change: the studio re-keys the shim's bodies with the same engines. **Every bundle now carries the traceability graph, the blast radius and the PromQL parser with the packages it imports**: the bundle's module graph reaches one package root, `@prometheus-io/lezer-promql` 0.312.0-rc.0 (Apache-2.0; `VENDOR-MANIFEST.json`'s `npm` field for `tools/lib/promql-lezer.mjs`), and its closure `@lezer/lr` 1.4.10, `@lezer/highlight` 1.2.3 and `@lezer/common` 1.5.2 (MIT), each embedded as `npm ci` installed it under its own name in the import map; the licence block names the four — the Apache-2.0 text in full for lezer-promql, its file's `Copyright The Prometheus Authors` line, each package's `Source:` repository — the pointer comment takes its with-packages wording, and `--json`'s `thirdParty` lists them. An empty bundle measures 5,346,173 bytes, up from 4,939,627 (+8.2 %; 114 modules, up from 111). **Every build now needs `package-lock.json`, `VENDOR-MANIFEST.json` and an `npm ci`** in the tree it builds from (it read the tree only). Migration for a downstream: (1) snapshot the whole tree as before — `package.json`, `package-lock.json`, `LICENSE` and `VENDOR-MANIFEST.json` are part of it; keep the four from the same upstream commit; (2) run `npm ci` at the tree's root (Node ≥ 22.16) before `node tools/build-studio-bundle.mjs` — on a build machine that needs no dev tools, `npm ci --omit=dev`; either installs the server's production dependencies too (74 packages: express, openid-client, …), of which the bundle embeds four, so an offline build machine needs them in npm's cache or a registry mirror (prime once with `npm ci` online, then `npm ci --offline`); (3) build — `--json`'s `thirdParty` lists the packages embedded, the human line is unchanged; (4) a package you patch after install (patch-package, a hand edit) is embedded as patched and the block does not mark it modified — that is yours to do where its licence asks; (5) a pipeline that rewrites the HTML after the build keeps the `#observogram-licenses` element, and `verifyBundleNotices` (exported by the builder) checks a finished file; (6) nothing else — no flag, config key, CSP or host change (`data:` in `script-src` was already needed). The build refuses, naming the fix, a tree without them (the refusals of the entry above). In the static posture the texts a working Compare newly reaches say what a reader of the bundle can do (`studio/static-remedies.mjs` `staticRemedy`, the brand's product named; the live studio's texts are unchanged): the drift drill's partial-live-evidence banner (`Before acting on this drift, get a complete live draft: drafting needs the Observogram server, and whoever builds this bundle builds the new draft in (--pack).`, where it said to redraft from MCP); Compare with no second pack (`This bundle carries no other pack that loads, so there is nothing to compare this one with; whoever builds the bundle can add another with --pack (a live draft, or a curated repository pack).`, where it said to load or scan one from Discover); the Assessment's drift-free and live-missing fixes (`Review the differences, then update the repository from live; deploying what is missing needs the Observogram server.`, and `Review them, …`), through an explicit `remedy` input of `buildAssessmentDecision`. **Both postures** (**a declared change**): Pair by's reasons read `the artefact taxonomy the comparison used did not bind in this page: …` (was `… did not bind as the server's: …`) and `the packs on screen are not the ones the comparison was computed for` (was `… the server compared`), and the bind error `the served taxonomy override does not compile here` (was `the server's override …`) — the comparison may be the page's own, the override the builder's. `tools/lib/promql-lezer.mjs` and `tools/lib/library.mjs` change comments only (the parser is an npm import served to a browser through the bundle's import map alone; `VENDOR-MANIFEST.json` regenerated, exports unchanged); `docs/BUILD_JOURNEY.md` follows. The README 501 list and DOWNSTREAM §10's denial cell drop Compare, its answered cell names `GET /api/diff`, and §10's Compare paragraph says it is answered. Five more tests in `tools/test-bundle-compare.mjs`, `tools/test-bundle-compare-pairs.mjs` (new, 1; `npm run test:bundle-compare-pairs`) and two more in `tools/test-static-remedies.mjs` (1484 → 1492 tests): C1 holds the real closure — one root, the four at the lockfile's versions, each entry file and import-map address byte for byte the installed file, each source — and the licence pin, `THIRD_PARTY`, each package's SPDX id and the sha256 of its licence and NOTICE files (a change fails until reviewed; a synthetic root with one LICENSE byte changed shows it bites); C2 now checks the four sections against the installed files; C3 holds the diff to a child server's, status, reason phrase, headers and raw text, from the source — every scopeMode spelling × four service values on four pairs (the annotation fallback in both namespaces), the environments, every refusal, krystaline and a live pack both ways — then a failed `--pack-url` pack, one that fails the schema but adapts, and an engine import retried; C3b a server started with `OBSERVOGRAM_TAXONOMY` against the bound document, and the guard's 500 on the diff and retrofeed; C4 Pair by over the shim's bodies, 5/15/5, 4/16/4, 3/17/3; C9 the CLI-built file itself, its import map applied by a `module.register` resolve hook and the shim imported from its own `data:` address, twelve diffs and a retrofeed equal to the server's; M1 every ordered pair of ten packs in each scope mode the studio sends (300 queries); S4 the phrase ledger — every studio line naming a server action listed with its disposition, so a new one fails until it has a static text or a reason — and S5 the wiring of each static text behind `isStatic()`. `tools/test-studio-bundle.mjs`: T1 the shim's literal import of the graph and the one package root, T3 the four in the notices, T3c Compare in neither 501 list, T6 the answered diff, its 400 and `This action`, the notice, T7 the notice exactly; `tools/test-identity-modes.mjs` the new reason.
- **The service audit report in the static bundle** (**a declared default change**, the static bundle — its bytes and its answers): `GET /api/packs/:id/audit-report` is answered by `studio/static-backend.mjs` (it was 501 `Audit report needs the <product> server; …`), `server/routes/audit-report.mjs`'s handler ported: the 404, `format` one of `json`, `html` (absent or repeated: `json`), else the 400, `top` absent → 10, else one value of digits from 1 to 100 (repeated: the 400), the taxonomy guard of the computing routes, then the report — its conformance section the bundle's own `/conformance` body for the environment (one function now answers both, so the two cannot grade one pack differently), its goes-blind section `tools/lib/blast-radius.mjs` over the traceability graph's shape, computed in the browser with the parser Compare inlines (`tools/lib/audit-report.mjs` and `tools/lib/traceability-graph.mjs` load at the first call through literal dynamic imports; `createStaticBackend(config, { importAuditEngines })` is the seam; a failed import answers 500 with its message and is retried). `Cache-Control: no-store` on the report only, as the server's code sets it — its refusals carry none —, `download=1` the server's `Content-Disposition` (`<id>.audit-report.json|html`), and the HTML is `renderAuditReportHtml` with the brand the shell was built with (`createStaticBackend` gains `brand`; `product` defaults to its name) over the bundle's own `design-tokens.css` and `design-kit.css`: `bundleStyles(textOf)` (exported) takes each inlined sheet's text less the one newline the builder wraps it in on each side, byte for byte the server's stylesheet, and a sheet the page lacks answers 500 `the bundle's design kit is missing (<sheet>)`, never an unstyled report (`styles` is the seam, a string or a function read at the first HTML request). The Conformance view's two download anchors now save the report as a Blob under the server's file name. **The bundle's report differs from a server's in five declared fields**: `generator` (`<product> static bundle`), `pack.source` (`bundle` for a pack built in, `url` for a `--pack-url` one), `assessments` and `waivers` (`available: false` — "not recorded by this build": a bundle has no store; its `GET …/verdicts` keeps answering the empty document a server gives a never-registered pack), the clock's `generatedAt`, and for a pack a server has registered `tier.service`/`tier.environment` (`null`: no registry, as the bundle's `/conformance` has answered since W6). `PACK_FEATURES` loses the Audit report; the README 501 list and DOWNSTREAM §10's denial cell drop it, its answered cell names the route, §10's Compare paragraph and §14 close `B3.5-bundle-audit-report`. An empty bundle measures 5,407,911 bytes, up from 5,346,173 (115 modules, up from 114: `tools/lib/audit-report.mjs` joins the graph). `tools/lib/audit-report.mjs`'s HTML for a report given no graph reads `Not computed by this build: no traceability graph was given to it.` (was `… the traceability graph needs the PromQL parser the server and the CLI have; the static bundle does not.` — true of no caller now; `VENDOR-MANIFEST.json` regenerated, exports unchanged); `docs/ADAPTER.md` follows. `server/routes/audit-report.mjs`'s header comment now says what its code does (`no-store` on the report; the refusals carry none) — a comment, no answer moves. One more test in `tools/test-bundle-compare.mjs` (1492 → 1493 tests): C5 holds the report to a child server's — payment-service under no env, `prod` and `staging`, `download=1`, `top=3`, a registered pack, a repeated `format` — JSON and HTML, status, reason phrase and headers exactly, the text equal once the declared fields are the shim's and its clock the server's `generatedAt`, and a branded server against a shim given the same brand; every refusal equal, headers included; then the design kit read from the page, a missing sheet's 500, an engine import retried and a failed `--pack-url` pack. C3b adds the report under the bound taxonomy (equal, and its coverage section moved by it) and the guard's 500 on the report; C9 the report in JSON and HTML from the CLI-built file, its design kit extracted from the file's own stylesheets. `tools/test-studio-bundle.mjs`: T1 the shim's literal import of the report, T3c the Audit report in neither 501 list, T6 the answered report and its 400, T7 the anchor's download, the goes-blind section computed in the page; `tools/test-audit-report.mjs` the new sentence exactly.
- **The static posture's remedies** (**a declared default change**, the static posture only; the live studio's texts are unchanged): every remedy a reader of a bundle could already reach in Diagnose, Traceability and Remediate that named a server action — connect MCP, scan live, refresh or redraft the live draft, deploy the repository's artefacts, crawl or upload — now says, addressed to the reader (who did not build the file), how a static bundle gets a live pack: what is built in, what the reader can do here, and what needs a server or whoever builds the bundle (`studio/static-remedies.mjs` `staticRemedy`, the brand's product named). What is built in is read from the catalogue (`staticInputOf(product, catalog, selectedPackId)`: a live pack — a scaffold or a snapshot — other than Pack A, whether Pack A is itself one, and any pack that loads), so with a live pack built in the text points at it (`Choose the live pack built into this bundle as the baseline (Pack B) to compare.`) and without one it says so (`Nothing live is built into this bundle; whoever builds it can add a live draft made on the Observogram server (--pack), to choose as the baseline (Pack B).`); with the live pack as Pack A and no other, a text that says the bundle carries no live pack says none besides Pack A (`A recommendation needs a live pack: this bundle carries no live pack besides Pack A; whoever builds it can add one (a live draft made on the Observogram server, built in with --pack); deploying needs the Observogram server.`). The texts: Traceability's empty state with no requirement and no Pack B; Diagnose's banner over a pack nothing live backs (`Nothing live backs this pack. “Drift-free” and “Fresh” need a live pack: …`); the Assessment's fresh, nothing-live and partial-evidence fixes (`The live pack in this bundle is as fresh as when it was drafted; a fresh look needs a new live draft, made on the Observogram server and built into the bundle by whoever builds it (--pack).`, …), through `buildAssessmentDecision`'s `remedy` input; the Live measure's note; the drift-free check's detail (`declared-only - no live signal to verify against (no live pack is built into this bundle)`); Traceability's scrape-gap fix and next actions (`Capture scrape evidence with a live draft (made on the Observogram server)`, `Deploy the dashboard on the Observogram server; a new live draft confirms it`, …), on the screen and in the drawer's trace panel; the response path's note before a comparison on Diagnose and on Remediate (`Not compared with live: every alert reads as declared; deploying needs the Observogram server.`); Remediate's decision note with no Pack B, and its strategy cards that need one, drawn disabled — not faded: the reason line is what marks them, and at the reskin's opacity for a disabled button it read below AA — (`To enable, pick a live pack as Pack B: this bundle carries no live pack; …`, or with one built in `Choose the live pack built into this bundle as Pack B to enable`, where they said `Load a live pack (Pack B) to enable`); Remediate's primary action with no Pack B where no live pack is built in besides Pack A (`Review the assessment`, which names the Assessment it opens, where it said `Compare with live first` — a comparison with live for which that Assessment's Pack B picker offers no live pack; with one built in it still says so, and the picker offers it); and Diagnose's and Remediate's prompt before a pack is chosen (`Head to Discover and open one of the packs built into this bundle — then come back here.`, or, in a bundle with no pack, `This bundle carries no pack; whoever builds it can add one with --pack (…).` — where it named the crawl, the live MCP draft and the upload, each a 501 in a bundle); and, in a bundle of one pack, the Assessment's comparison with no baseline, which says what Compare says there (`This bundle carries no other pack that loads, so there is nothing to compare this one with; …`, where it said to choose a baseline in the header's Pack B picker, which offers none). The shared modules take the text as an option and keep today's words by default, so the live studio, every server reader and the journey (`tools/lib/journey.mjs` grades through it) read what they read: `studio/diagnostic-grade.mjs` `computeDiagnosticGrade(…, { noLiveHint })`; `studio/trace-chain.mjs` exports a frozen `LIVE_ACTION_TEXT` (the live-evidence texts by name) and `readTraceability(pack, { actions })`, `readChain(chain, index, { actions })` and `traceIssue(code, { actions })` take a set with its keys (`staticTraceActions(product)`); `studio/remediation-flow-view.mjs` `remediationFlowHtml` and `renderRemediationFlow` take `{ notComparedNote }` (its imports unchanged). `tools/test-static-remedies.mjs`: S3 holds every static text exactly for five products and each input, the static live-evidence texts, `staticInputOf`, and the three options with their defaults (a probe set shows the Traceability reading moves its live-evidence texts and nothing else); S4's phrase ledger gives each of those lines its row (the design's rows 1, 3, 5–8 and 13–18, and rows 19 and 28 for the prompt before a pack and the disabled strategy cards, which the design did not list — S4's pattern also finds `load a live pack`, C8's copy follows, and C8 reads the cards, and measures each text on them against AA in both themes, in a bundle with the live pack built in, over a live Pack A and in a bundle of one pack) and no longer accepts a deferral; S5 holds each text's wiring behind `isStatic()` (row 4's on the Assessment too, where no other pack loads: a bundle's Pack B picker offers the other packs that load and no example), the one way views read what is built in, and every Traceability reading taking the static texts. `tools/test-remediation-flow-view.mjs`'s pin on Diagnose's call admits the option. No test is added (the suite total is unchanged).

- **Compare in Chromium** (a test; nothing a reader sees changes): one more test in `tools/test-bundle-compare.mjs` (1493 → 1494 tests). C8 builds a bundle through the CLI — payment-service, the live snapshot as `payment-live`, the compare-modes pair, production-curated, and payment-service with one remediation linked to its burn-rate alert (no example pack links one, and an unlinked response path draws no deploy button) — and serves it from a loopback host to headless Chromium. The in-page `fetch('/api/diff?…')` text equals a child server's for six queries under the browser locales `en-US`, `cs-CZ` and `et-EE` (each page shown to collate by its locale, the two tailoring ones differently from English);
