Remediate's decision-header deploy and the response path's per-SLO button are drawn and explained the same way, and its decision's filled buttons (`Review and deploy …`, and in a bundle of one pack `Review and deploy repository artefacts to live`) without their fill, at rest and under the pointer; the deploy controls Remediate draws in that bundle of one pack, with no Pack B, are the ones the entry for Deploy in the static bundle counts and names as what a bundle without a comparison reaches; the artefact compiler, opened on payment-service, draws no `Review and deploy to live ↗`, its Deploy to live card giving the reason, and on the generated board the server before the declaration, in the card and in the board's subtitle — the list's, its title and the preview's; every deploy control each of those views draws (the pack cards' in Compare, the drift action and the chain cards' in Diagnose, Remediate's) is measured in the light and dark themes: at full opacity through its ancestors, and each text in it — the label, a sub line, the reason — clearing AA on what it is drawn on (a glyph alone, an icon, 3:1), colours composited through every ancestor's background and opacity; the design's rows 4 (in a bundle of one pack, on Compare and on the Assessment Remediate's `Review the assessment` lands on, whose Pack B picker offers none; with other packs built in the picker offers each and the Assessment points at it), 6, 11, 12 and 18 (over the live pack as Pack A) render as written, as does Remediate's primary with no Pack B (`Compare with live first` with the live pack built in; `Review the assessment`, landing on the Assessment, over a live Pack A and in a bundle of one pack, where no live pack is built in besides Pack A), and the rendered Compare, Diagnose, Traceability and Remediate hold no phrase S4 holds the studio's source to; the Conformance view's HTML anchor downloads `payment-service.audit-report.html` with its goes-blind section, and the in-page report, JSON and HTML, is the server's but for the declared fields; the notice, exactly; no page error, no `console.error`, nothing off the loopback, and each static host asked for `/` only. Eight of the design's mutations fail it (a dropped `'en'` in `diff.mjs` under `cs-CZ` only, the graph omitted, the report's stylesheets keeping their newlines, the modal opening, `.rflow-deploy` left out of `DEPLOY_CONTROLS`, row 6's static branch deleted, a deploy control hidden instead of drawn unavailable, the notice still listing Compare among what needs the server). It skips like T7 where Playwright or its browser is missing — CI's jobs have neither — unless `OBSERVOGRAM_BUNDLE_SMOKE=require` (its loader imports an absolute `OBSERVOGRAM_PLAYWRIGHT` as a file URL, as the `windows-ci` follow-up's seven loaders do, so the knob takes a Windows path too); before it looks for one, so in CI's jobs too, it holds `README.md`, `docs/UPDATE_JOURNEY.md` and `docs/DOWNSTREAM.md` §10 to that limit — where each claims the diff's parity in Chromium, the sentence says the check runs only where Playwright and its browser are installed, which CI's jobs are not — and `.github/workflows/ci.yml` to naming no Playwright outside a full-line comment (the Windows leg's comment says the browser tests skip there, as on every leg; a step that installed it would fail here until the documents said so). `tools/test-bundle-compare.mjs` is the eighth suite that loads Playwright, so `tools/tap-summary.mjs`'s probe for the `cannot import` skip reason, `tools/test-tap-summary.mjs` S11 (extended, not added: eight suites' reasons, POSIX and Windows, one line) and `server/fixtures/serve-child.mjs`'s list of the browser suites count eight where they counted seven; no knob is added — C8 reads `OBSERVOGRAM_PLAYWRIGHT` and `OBSERVOGRAM_BUNDLE_SMOKE`, both already stripped from every child.
- **The Docker image carries `LICENSE`** (**a declared change**, the image's files): `Dockerfile` copies Observogram's MIT licence into `/app` beside `package.json`, so the licence travels with the code the image runs. The image's `node_modules` already carries each installed package's own licence file (npm installs them), and `npm pack` already includes `LICENSE`. The image does not gain `VENDOR-MANIFEST.json`: the server does not read it, and building a static bundle is not something the image is for (the builder's refusal names the fix where a build runs). `tools/test-deploy-manifests.mjs` asserts the copy — an assertion in a harness, so the suite total is unchanged.
- **The bundle loads no font over the network by default** (**a declared default change**, the bundle's bytes and the builder's flags; the live studio, `studio/index.html` as the server serves it, is unchanged): `tools/build-studio-bundle.mjs` drops the shell's three Google Fonts lines — the preconnects to `fonts.googleapis.com` and `fonts.gstatic.com` and the `css2` stylesheet — unless asked to keep them, so a bundle opened online no longer asks Google's CDN for that stylesheet (the maintainer's ruling of 2026-10-08: the finished bundle self-contained and offline, no CDN); the stylesheets' font stacks apply, a family the reader's system has, else the system fonts after it. `--remote-fonts` (new) and `buildStudioBundle({ remoteFonts: true })` keep the three lines, byte for byte as a build without a flag kept them before; `buildStudioBundle`'s `remoteFonts` now defaults to `false`; `--no-remote-fonts`, which dropped them, is now the default and still accepted, so a script that passes it builds the default's bytes; both together are a usage error, exit 2 (`--remote-fonts and --no-remote-fonts given together` and the usage — this CLI refuses a seam given twice the same way), and one repeated is that one. `--json`'s `remoteFonts` reports the effective value: `false` unless `--remote-fonts`. An empty bundle measures 5,454,277 bytes, down from 5,454,649 (the three lines). Migration: a build that wants the fonts for online readers adds `--remote-fonts`; one that passed `--no-remote-fonts` needs no change (`docs/DOWNSTREAM.md` §10). One more test in `tools/test-studio-bundle.mjs` (1494 → 1495 tests): T3d — the default names neither host, `remoteFonts: false` and `--no-remote-fonts` build its bytes, `remoteFonts: true` and `--remote-fonts` build the default with the shell's three lines back after the theme script and nothing else moved, `--json`'s key in each case, both flags refused in either order. T3's build, which passed `remoteFonts: true`, now takes the default, and its assertion that the fonts were kept by default now asserts none; T7 and C8 (`tools/test-bundle-compare.mjs`) build with no font flag, so their check that no request leaves the loopback in headless Chromium (where Playwright is installed) holds the default.

### Follow-up `smoke-stale-adopt-id`: the stale-scan adopt test holds `/api/validate` to the YAML's own id, which may be the scan's
- **`server/test-smoke.mjs`: adopting a scan whose entry is gone no longer has to register under another id** (a test fix; no runtime, `tools/lib`, golden or output change — `studio/api.mjs` changes in a comment only). The assertion failed both Linux jobs, `validate` and `node-floor`, of PR #158's run 38556085157 (`✗ adopting a scan whose entry is gone registers it through /api/validate`, `got: {"id":"uploaded-smoke-crawl-2ba480cc","source":null}`), and could fail on `develop` since #134 (*Fixed: one scan or one live draft is one pack in the catalogue*, below). Once a re-scan has replaced the first scan's entry, adopting the first result goes through `POST /api/validate`, which registers the YAML under its content id (`packIdOf`: the pack's name and a hash of the parsed object); the test asserted that this id differs from the one `POST /api/crawl` registered. That route crawls twice — `crawlToYaml` for the YAML it answers, `crawlFiles` for the pack it registers — and the two differ only in `crawler.discoveredAt`, each stamped `new Date().toISOString()`; the YAML parses back to its pack exactly, so when both crawls fall in the same millisecond (a fast runner) `/api/validate` registers under the scan's own id. The code was right; the test's assumption was wrong, and so was the same sentence in `registeredOrValidated`'s comment (*the YAML is not the registered object byte for byte, so its content hash differs*), which now says the id is the YAML's content id, not necessarily another. The assertion holds the id to `packIdOf(parse(canonicalYaml))` and keeps `adapted` in the answer as the proof that `/api/validate` ran (selecting a catalogue entry answers `{ id }` alone). Under a preload whose wall clock moves only between event-loop turns (a route's synchronous work sees one millisecond), the previous assertion fails as on CI and this one passes; with the real clock both pass. The assertion is replaced, not added: the suite total stays at 1472 tests.

### Follow-up `id-clock-tolerance`: the id clocks time the pattern, not the machine
- **`tools/lib/artefact-classify.mjs`** (**a declared default change**; `compileBoundedPattern` takes a `clock` option and `compileTaxonomy` a `{ timed }` option — no export added or removed): the id clocks — one pass of a taxonomy or settings-policy pattern over the four adversarial 256-character ids, held to 50 ms (a settings policy where the static bundle's build compiles it: a server loads one untimed since `policy-worker`, below) — charge the pass the **smaller** of the wall clock (now the monotonic `performance.now()`, was `Date.now()`) and the process's CPU time (`process.cpuUsage()`, user + system, read guarded — a browser has no `process`) plus its slack. The wall clock alone counted the time the thread was descheduled, so on a loaded machine a fast pattern was refused: on 4 cores with 12 busy loops and 4 copies of each suite (load 21–47), `tools/test-artefact-classify.mjs` failed 7 runs in 100 and `tools/test-mcp-server-settings.mjs` 28 in 100 (`pattern too slow against a 256-character id`), and a server started with a taxonomy whose one pattern costs ~10 ms of CPU refused to start 70 times in 100 (`[studio] failed to start: OBSERVOGRAM_TAXONOMY: <path>: taxonomy: ids[0]: pattern too slow against a 256-character id`, nothing served). The CPU time never counts a deschedule but is process-wide — V8's compiler and GC helpers, the libuv pool and a worker count too — and reads short of the time spent by less than its slack: 0 on Linux and macOS, whose process times are exact; on Windows (`process.platform`, read guarded) 15.625 + 1 ms — `GetProcessTimes` charges whole 15.625 ms clock ticks and libuv truncates each reading to the millisecond, so a pass of 62 ms can read 46. The smaller of the wall reading and the CPU reading plus its slack bounds the pattern's own work from above: a pattern over the budget is over on both on every pass and refused, on every platform. One reading can still overstate a pass whose own work fits — the process's other threads busy in the same window, or a stall Windows' coarse clock charges three ticks (46.875 ms, which a pass over the budget can read too) — so a first pass over the budget but within twice it (100 ms) is re-timed, up to two more passes, and the pattern is refused only when none fits. On a loaded Windows machine a pattern within about one tick of the budget can still be refused, as the wall clock alone refused it: a pass of 44–50 ms of its own work that the clock charges three ticks reads like a pass over the budget, on every pass (simulated as below with 24 busy loops: 19 refused in 200). Each re-time runs a fresh compilation, the source wrapped in non-capturing groups (`(?:…)` once, then twice — they match exactly what it matches): V8 keeps one compilation per source and runs its second pass warm (`^.*a{0,19}a{0,19}a{0,19}b$`: ~55 ms on its first pass, ~8 ms on the next), so re-running the same RegExp would admit what the first pass refused. (Simulated Windows — `process.platform` faked, `process.cpuUsage()` cut into 15.625 ms ticks at a random phase and truncated to the millisecond — under 24 busy loops and 4 probes at a time, a pattern of 27–41 ms of its own work: refused 12 times in 200 with the one pass, among them passes of 29 and 30 ms whose process was charged three ticks; 0 in 200 with the re-time.) (Without the slack, simulated on Linux through the clock seam — `process.platform` faked, `process.cpuUsage()` quantized to 15.625 ms ticks at a random phase and truncated to the millisecond, 40 cold processes a pattern — `^.*a{0,19}a{0,19}a{0,19}b$`, 53–63 ms of its own work, compiled 18 times in 40; with it, 0 in 40, while a fast pattern and one of ~11 ms compiled 40 in 40 under 12 busy loops.) A first pass over twice the budget is refused on it, never re-timed: a catastrophic pattern (`^.*a{0,60}a{0,60}a{0,60}a{0,60}b$`, seconds on one id) costs the one pass it always did, and a re-time adds at most two passes of a pattern whose own work is within 100 ms (the 53–63 ms pattern above is refused 40 times in 40 with the re-time too, on three cold passes). The 50 ms budget, the adversarial ids and every refusal text are unchanged; without a CPU clock the wall clock decides alone, as before. `clock` — `() => ({ wall, cpu, slack })` in milliseconds, `cpu` null where there is none, `slack` 0 if absent, read once before each pass and once after — is the suites' seam. Under the same load after the change: 100 runs in 100 for each of those suites and `tools/test-glossary.mjs`, and 100 boots in 100 with that taxonomy.
- **The studio compiles the server's taxonomy untimed** (`studio/taxonomy.mjs` `bindTaxonomy`; **a declared default change**): `compileTaxonomy(json, { timed: false })` — the document `GET /api/taxonomy` serves is one the server timed at start, and a static bundle's one the builder compiled — so a slow phone or a throttled tab no longer refuses what the server accepted. Before, in headless Chromium under CPU throttling (`Emulation.setCPUThrottlingRate`, 5 loads a rate), a valid taxonomy whose slowest pattern costs ~11 ms at full speed did not bind in 4 loads of 5 at 6× and at 20× from a child server (1 and 4 of 5 from a static bundle that baked it), and one whose pattern costs ~3.6 ms in 3 of 5 at 20× (2 of 5 from the bundle): `[taxonomy] the server's override does not compile — classifying with the default families: taxonomy: ids[1]: pattern too slow against a 256-character id` on the console, and every typed artefact classified `unknown`. After, both bind in every load at 1×, 6× and 20×, from the server and from the bundle. Every static check still runs, as for the settings policy (`studio/app.mjs`, `timed: false` since rebadge batch 4); a vendored classifier without the option ignores it and times as before.
- **The vendoring contract declares the read** (`tools/gen-vendor-manifest.mjs`, `docs/DOWNSTREAM.md` §2, `docs/VENDORING.md`): the purity rule's ``read `process` `` did not see optional chaining — `globalThis.process?.env?.SECRET` passed it — which is how the module above stayed listed while §2 said a listed module "reads no `process`" and its VENDORING.md row said "no Node APIs". The rule now catches `process?.` (and `process?.[…]`) and allows exactly the two guarded forms the id clocks use, `globalThis.process?.cpuUsage?.()` and `globalThis.process?.platform` (`PROCESS_READS_ALLOWED`, a new export of the generator); §2 and the module's row name them. The vendorable set and `VENDOR-MANIFEST.json` are unchanged. `tools/test-vendor-manifest.mjs` pins the two forms, the reads the rule now refuses (`process?.env`, `globalThis.process?.argv`, a longer name after `platform`), the docs that name them and the one module that makes them.
- **Beside `policy-worker`** (below, which loads a server's settings policy untimed): the comments on `compileSettingsPolicy` (`tools/lib/mcp-server-settings.mjs`; comments only, no export changed, `VENDOR-MANIFEST.json` regenerated) and `server/mcp-settings-policy.mjs` no longer say a loaded machine's clock would refuse a valid policy — the wall clock alone did; the id clocks charge a pattern's own work now, and a loaded Windows machine can still refuse one near the budget. Its two jumping-clock pins — `server/test-mcp-settings.mjs` (the server's load refuses no valid policy) and `tools/test-studio-bundle.mjs` T8d (the bundle build keeps the clocks) — jump `performance.now()` and the CPU time, the readings the id clocks take, a second per read, where they jumped `Date.now()`, which no id clock reads now: T8d failed, and the server's test held against a timed load too. No test added.
- **Tests**: 1468 → 1472 tests — three in `tools/test-artefact-classify.mjs` (with an injected clock: a stalled pass of a fast pattern compiles, a slow pattern is refused, the budget is inclusive, a coarse clock's three ticks on every pass are refused and two compile, a first pass within twice the budget (inclusive) is re-timed up to twice — three ticks then two compile, as does a first pass the process's other threads overstated, no pass of three fitting is refused without a fourth, a first pass over twice the budget is refused on it — and the wall clock decides alone without a CPU time; a catastrophic pattern is run once on each of the four adversarial ids, timed once and refused, and each re-time runs its own compilation of the pattern with its flags, wrapped in groups the suite checks match what the source matches; the default clock is pinned — `performance.now()` and `process.cpuUsage()`, never `Date.now()`, through `compileTaxonomy` and `validateTaxonomy` too, and the slack it reads with `process.platform` faked: 15.625 + 1 ms on `win32` to the microsecond, where a pass re-timed at two ticks compiles, 0 on `linux` and `darwin` — the Windows name from `server/fixtures/platform.mjs`'s new `WIN32_PLATFORM`, since `tools/test-platform.mjs` P5 keeps every other suite from spelling it) and one in `tools/test-discover-rows.mjs` (`bindTaxonomy` binds under clocks that read every pass as too slow; a static refusal still refuses). `timed: false`'s test injects the clock instead of stubbing `Date.now`, and the purity test pins the two guarded `process` reads (the CPU time and the platform its slack depends on).

### Follow-up `purge-org-unresolvable-links`: on Windows purge-org refuses a users file whose recorded path cannot be fully resolved
- **On Windows `packc store purge-org` refuses a users file whose recorded path this host cannot fully resolve** (**a declared default change**: such a path was compared as written and the purge went through; `server/store/paths.mjs`, `server/store/ops.mjs`; Linux and macOS compare exactly as before). The comparison is the native realpath (`realpathSync.native`) of a path's longest prefix it can read, the rest as written; it stops at a part that exists but that it cannot read through, and the rest was read as written beside that part, wherever the part leads. So with org beta's root at `C:\ws\orgs\beta` a users file recorded through such a part compared as outside the root, and `packc store purge-org beta` went through: it deleted `C:\ws\orgs\beta\sub\users.json` recorded as `C:\links\betasub\users.json`, with `C:\links\betasub` a directory symlink to `\\myhost\betasub` (which is `C:\ws\orgs\beta\sub`), while that share was down (served, it was refused as a share no file id places), or as `C:\cfg\users.json`, a file symlink to `\\myhost\betasub\users.json`. Such a part is a link whose target cannot be opened — those two, a chain of links ending at one, a link to a path that does not exist — or any other reparse point whose target cannot be opened — a cloud placeholder, a deduplicated file, a tag no filter on this host handles — which `lstatSync` answers as no link (`isSymbolicLink()` false) or cannot read either; or the path lies on a volume whose paths the native realpath cannot name at all (an ImDisk RAM disk, which bypasses the Volume Mount Manager: `EISDIR`, its root among them), where a users file under the root named by a short name (`R:\WS~1\orgs\beta\users.json`) or through a link on it, or through a link on `C:` to it, was deleted alike. With `{ deleting: true }` — purge-org's users-file check, the only caller that passes it — a path now answers null when a part the walk that spells it could not read exists: `lstatSync` answers it, a link or not, or fails for another reason than `ENOENT` (libuv's `lstat` opens through a reparse point it does not take for a link, as `stat` does) — or when that walk could not read even its root: a drive letter not mapped when it runs, or mapped to a share that cannot be reached, where the path stays spelled as written on that letter, as on a local drive (before, with `Z:` mapped to `\\myhost\betasub`, `Z:\users.json` then compared as outside the root and the purge went through when a later read of its volume found the share back, or `Z:` mapped). Those parts are the ones that walk could not read, not a second walk's, so a share that comes back or goes down, or a drive letter mapped or unmapped, while the check runs is refused alike. purge-org refuses a null answer naming its way out, and its description now says what the check knows (`the recorded users file <path> is not a path on a local drive, or its path cannot be resolved on this host (a link or another reparse point on it whose target cannot be opened, a part of it this host cannot read, or a volume whose paths do not resolve), and such a path, like a network share no file id places beside <root> (one on any volume, a DFS link, one that cannot be reached), may lead under it — nothing was deleted. If it does not lie under <root>, delete <root> by hand, then run purge-org again: it drops org <id>'s records. If it does, move it out of <root> first, then do the same`; the last sentence is new, the way out for a users file that does lie under the root). A declared false refusal: a users file outside the root recorded through such a part, or anywhere on such a volume (`R:\cfg\users.json`, beside a root on `R:` or on `C:`), is refused alike, since nothing on this host tells it apart, and the way out holds; one named as on disk under the root on such a volume (`R:\ws\orgs\beta\users.json`) is refused with this description, no longer as lying under the root, and the new last sentence is its way out. A share's server follows a junction on its volume itself, and SMB before Windows 10/Server 1709 (at either end — Windows Server 2016 among them) has no normalized-name query, so there the native realpath names a path through a share as the client opened it, the junction by its own name: with `C:\ws\links\j` a junction to `C:\ws\orgs\beta\sub`, a users file recorded as `\\myhost\ws\links\j\users.json` (or `\\localhost\C$\ws\links\j\users.json`, or `Z:\links\j\users.json` with `Z:` mapped to `\\myhost\ws`) was placed by the share's directory as `C:\ws\links\j\users.json`, outside the root — with the workspace named through the share, it compared as outside the root by its name — and the purge deleted `C:\ws\orgs\beta\sub\users.json`. With `{ deleting: true }` a path now also answers null when a part the walk read is a link to `lstatSync` (realpath named it without following it), or one `lstatSync` cannot read now; one through a junction to a path outside the root is refused alike (a declared false refusal, whose way out holds). Unchanged: a link whose target opens is followed to it (to `C:\out`, outside the root: purged; to `C:\ws\orgs\beta\sub`: refused as lying under the root) on the drive, and through a share when the native realpath follows it (SMB from Windows 10/Server 1709 on, at both ends), a path whose native realpath stops only at parts that do not exist yet, below a root it reads, is judged as before, and the export, in-place, no-marker, scan and own-file checks, which never pass `deleting`, compare as before. `comparedPath` takes `lstatSync` beside `statSync`; `server/fixtures/ntfs.mjs` models symbolic links (`links`: the native realpath and `statSync` follow them, `lstatSync` reports a link itself), reparse points that are no link whose target cannot be opened (`reparse`: nothing opens through one, and `lstatSync` answers the point, as no link, or fails with the open's error) drives the native realpath cannot name (`unmanaged`: `EISDIR`, while `statSync` and `lstatSync` answer) and SMB with no normalized-name query (`unnormalized`: the native realpath names a path through a share as the client opened it, a junction by its own name), and returns `lstatSync`. `server/test-store-paths.mjs` (four more, 18): a directory link, a file link and a chain to `\\myhost\betasub` refused by purge-org while the share is served, as before, and while it is down (the purge went through before), and with the share coming back or going down at any host call of the check, through those links or through `X:` mapped to it, and with `X:` mapped or unmapped then (through `X:`, the share coming back or `X:` mapped after the walk let the purge through before); links to paths that do not exist refused; links to `C:\out` purged — a users file there, or one not created yet — and to `C:\ws\orgs\beta\sub` refused as under the root; reparse points that are no link, which `lstatSync` answers or cannot read, refused (the purge went through before), and paths not created yet beside them purged; on a RAM disk a users file under the root by a short name or through a link, on it or on `C:`, refused (the purge deleted it before), and one named as on disk, under the root or outside it, refused alike, while a path on `C:` is purged; a users file through a junction to `C:\ws\orgs\beta\sub` on `\\myhost\ws`'s volume, by any name of the share, refused before 1709 beside a root on the drive or named through the share (the purge deleted it before) and refused as lying under the root from 1709 on, one through a junction to `C:\out` refused alike before 1709 and purged from 1709 on, and one whose parts the walk read `lstatSync` cannot read then refused, while named on the drive both junctions are followed and a path through the share that passes no link is placed and judged as before; the export's comparison unchanged in each; every comparison the suite builds injects the fixture's `lstatSync`; its pin of the refusals' ways out holds purge-org's new last sentence and its description as it reads now (the one before, naming a share alone, read beside the same way out). Suite total 1472 → 1476 tests.

### Follow-up `journey-notify-env-ownership`: a server-run journey's notification reads only its org's notify hooks
- **`server/notify-target-policy.mjs`** (new, SQL-free, the twin of `server/mcp-target-policy.mjs`): a journey run by the server in org `<org>` reads at most two variables for its notification — the org's hook URL `OBSERVOGRAM_ORG_<KEY>_NOTIFY_<HOOK>_URL` and, when the journey names it, that same hook's `OBSERVOGRAM_ORG_<KEY>_NOTIFY_<HOOK>_TOKEN` (`<HOOK>`: words of A–Z and 0–9 joined by single `_`; the whole name upper case exactly and at most 200 characters) — where the org is the **sole** owner of the name by the longest-prefix rule of `server/store/mcp-endpoints.mjs` (removed orgs count; a KEY tie such as `a-b`/`a_b`, or an org whose id extends this one's such as `acme-notify-eu`, shadows the name for everyone, and no text names the other org). `HOOK_RE`, `hookVarsOf`, `notifyHookOf` and `notifyNamesRefusal` (the NAME, SHADOW and PAIR texts, at capture or at a run — for an org whose every hook name another org's id takes, a KEY tie or an `acme-notify` beside `acme`, one TAKEN text for any name, `cannot be a notify hook of org '<org>' — another org's id also maps onto every <P>NOTIFY_<HOOK>_URL by the longest-prefix rule, so the server posts no notification for this org's journeys`, whose way out is to leave `notify` out, never the listing, which can hold none; it reads no environment, so a refused name gets the same sentence whether its variable is set or not), `notifyValueRefusal` (an unset hook or token; a URL that is not http(s), carries `user:password` or exceeds 2000 characters; a token that is not printable ASCII without spaces once its surrounding HTTP whitespace is trimmed; the origin), `resolveJourneyNotify` (→ `{ refused: null, url, auth, urlEnv }` or `{ refused }`) and `captureNotifyRefusal`; `notifyOriginList` — the **notify origin allowlist**, `OBSERVOGRAM_NOTIFY_ORIGINS` (`TOMOGRAPH_NOTIFY_ORIGINS` honoured) ∪ `OBSERVOGRAM_ORG_<KEY>_NOTIFY_ORIGINS` (read only for the name's sole owner), read per call: loopback always passes, a set list admits its origins (or every origin with `*`) and nothing else, no list admits only loopback; a rejected entry is noted once per process on stderr by its position only (`entry 1 is a URL, not an origin — list only its scheme://host[:port]`), never the entry; `isOrgHookShaped` (the URL or TOKEN shape of any org's hook, live or removed), `hooksAllTaken` (whether another org's id, live or removed, takes every one of an org's hook names; it reads orgs, never the environment), `notifyHooksOf` (the org's set hooks: names, `usable` and the capture `problem`, never a value or an origin), `notifyBootReport` (the start's `[notify-hooks]` lines) and `notifyPostureRefusal` (`authzDecision`'s posture texts for `running a journey that notifies`). Every text names variables, never a value and never an origin. `server/fixtures/serve-child.mjs`'s `STRIP` gains `NOTIFY_ORIGINS` (both spellings). `server/test-notify-target-policy.mjs` (new, 7, in `tools/test-doc-test-totals.mjs`'s LEDGER): every deny-list row (the server's own secrets, deployment secrets, another org's variables, a longer org's, shadowed names, the tie and an `<org>-notify` beside its org (TAKEN, for any name), the org's own non-hook variables, the shape's edges, bare names) refused before any env read, the pairing, the values, the origin list and its owner, the resolver, the hook shape, the listing and the boot report. 1439 → 1446 tests.
- **`tools/lib/journey.mjs`** (excluded from the vendorable set; it now imports `node:util` for `isDeepStrictEqual`, so the manifest's exclusion reason reads `node:fs/path/url/util imports` and `tools/test-vendor-manifest.mjs` pins the fourth built-in) gains `notifyTargetOf(notify, { url, auth, rawUrl, rawAuth })` — the notify target from values already resolved, which `resolveNotifyTarget` (the CLI's: it still reads the names from its invoker's environment, its texts unchanged) now builds through — and `scrubNotifyError(text)`; **`runJourney` takes `resolveNotify`**, the twin of `resolveMcp`: `(def.notify) → target`, so a host that resolves the hook itself has the engine read no variable a definition names (the server's run route passes one). **A delivery error never carries any part of the hook's URL or token** (**a declared default change**, on the CLI too — fleet CronJob records sit in the shared workspace the studio serves): `record.notify.error` is redacted by value — the URL as read and trimmed, its `href`, origin, host, hostname (an IPv6 literal's also without its brackets, as Node's `connect E… <address>:<port>` quotes it), host:port with the scheme's port when the URL names none, and path with query; the token as read, trimmed of HTTP whitespace and per line; each of at least 4 characters — and by pattern, undici's two texts that quote a value becoming `Headers.append: "<redacted>" is an invalid header value.` and `Request cannot be constructed from a URL that includes credentials: <redacted>` (a token undici trims before it quotes it, a token too short to redact by value, a URL spelling `new URL()` would normalise), and Node's and undici's four that quote the hook's host, or the address it resolved to with its port, becoming `getaddrinfo E… <redacted>`, `connect E… <redacted>`, `Connect Timeout Error (attempted address(es): <redacted>, timeout: …)` and `Hostname/IP does not match certificate's altnames: <redacted>` (a host under 4 characters, an address no value list holds); a text without a value — `HTTP 500`, `timeout after 1000ms` — is unchanged. The vantage-lost path leaves the record's delivery on the error it rethrows (`e.notify`). **`saveJourneyDef` writes only what reads back** (**a declared default change**): every banner line is one comment line — a CR, LF, U+2028 or U+2029 in it becomes a space, so a crawl `label` with line breaks can no longer write a top-level `notify:` (or any key) into a captured file that capture never checked — and the text is parsed before it is written and must read back deep-equal to the definition, else `journey <name>: the definition does not read back as written (<the first top-level key that differs, never a value>) — nothing was saved`, nothing written and a previous file of that name untouched; `POST /api/journeys/capture` answers that 400, so a body whose `env`, `service`, `scopeMode` or `gate` holds a line break (or a gate key `mini-yaml` would write unloadable) no longer saves. Cases in `tools/test-journey.mjs` (the seam, the vantage-lost path, the redaction) and `tools/test-schedule.mjs` (the banner and the read-back); both are harness suites, so the count is unchanged. `tools/test-platform.mjs` pins the one `.pathname` the module now reads (a hook URL's path, for the redaction — a URL path, never a filesystem path).
- **`GET /api/journeys/notify-hooks`** (new; `viewer`, like `GET /api/journeys`; no row; `server/index.mjs`, `server/route-table.mjs`): the request's org's notify hooks as `notifyHooksOf` answers them — `{ ok, org, pattern, hooks: [{ hook, urlEnv, tokenEnv, usable, problem }], none }`: every set `OBSERVOGRAM_ORG_<KEY>_NOTIFY_<HOOK>_URL` the org solely owns, sorted by hook (a name another org's id also maps onto is left out, so no other org is revealed), `tokenEnv` the hook's `_TOKEN` variable when that is set (else `null`), `usable` or the `problem` capture would answer (a URL that is not http(s) or carries `user:password`, a token it cannot send, an origin not on the notify origin list — the variable named, never its value), `pattern` the org's exact prefix and `none`, when there is no hook, who sets one (or, for an org whose every hook name another org's id takes, that none can be set and its journeys leave `notify` out). Never a value, an origin, an origin-list entry or another org's name. The list a journey's `notify` chooses from (R4's registered target, chosen by name). The static bundle answers it with its 501 "Journeys" (the `/api/journeys` prefix), unchanged. **The start reports the hooks** (`start()`, after the store opens; with the store's own warnings, so a silent start prints none): `[notify-hooks] org '<org>': <urlEnv> — <problem>` for each set hook a run would refuse, `[notify-hooks] <name> has the shape of org '<org>''s notify hook, but org '<other>' also maps onto it by the longest-prefix rule — no journey can use it`, and `[notify-hooks] <name> is no org's notify hook (no org's id maps onto it)` (`only a removed org's id` when that is so) — variables and org ids only; nothing at all when no such variable is set. Rows: `server/test-authz.mjs` (the class and the probe), `server/test-tenancy.mjs` (each org's listing holds its own hook and never the other org's name, in the sweep and for alice and bob), the README API Surface. `server/test-notify-target-policy.mjs` gains H14 (the listing as a viewer, an operator, the bearer and the anonymous `local` caller of an `OBSERVOGRAM_AUTH=off` child, which acts in the default org; bravo's `none`; no value, origin or other org in any body or child output) and H15 (a verbose child prints the three kinds of line and no value). 1446 → 1448 tests.
- **A server-run journey notifies only through its org's notify hook** (`server/index.mjs`; the open security item `journey-notify-env-ownership`). Before, a server run read whatever variables a journey's `notify` named and posted them: any operator — or, under `OBSERVOGRAM_AUTH=off`, anyone who could reach the studio — could have the server post another org's MCP token, or one of its own secrets, to a URL of their choosing. Declared changes: **(a)** `POST /api/journeys/:name/run` of a journey with `notify` resolves the notification before anything runs, after the Pack B target (`resolveJourneyNotify`): it runs only when `notify` names the org's hook pair — `OBSERVOGRAM_ORG_<KEY>_NOTIFY_<HOOK>_URL` and, when named, that hook's `_TOKEN`, solely owned by the org — set, with sound values and an allowed origin, and hands the engine the resolved target (`runJourney`'s `resolveNotify`), so the engine reads no variable a definition names; otherwise 400 `{ ok: false, error }` with the run text (`journey <name> is not run: …`, naming what to fix and who fixes it: the server's operator for the environment, whoever holds the workspace for the journey file — the file the run loaded, relative to the workspace in its org's root, `orgs/<id>/journeys/<file>.journey.yaml` for any org whose root is not `.`) — no record, no row, nothing sent, so a refused run never becomes the next run's baseline. **(b)** `POST /api/journeys/capture` checks a `notify` with `captureNotifyRefusal` after `validateNotify`: a name that is not one of the org's hooks, an unset hook, or a set hook whose value or origin a run would refuse is 400 with the capture text, no file and no row (a literal `url` stays refused for every role, admins included). **(c)** An unset hook on a server run is 400 naming the variable and who sets it (it was a 502 `notify.urlEnv names …, but that env var is not set`, which told any operator whether any variable was set); a refused name gets one sentence whether its variable is set or not. The CLI keeps exit 2. **(d)** The notify origin allowlist applies to server runs and captures: a webhook off this machine needs its origin in `OBSERVOGRAM_NOTIFY_ORIGINS` or `OBSERVOGRAM_ORG_<KEY>_NOTIFY_ORIGINS`. **(e)** On the server a hook URL that is not http(s) or carries `user:password`, and a token that holds a space, a control character or a non-ASCII character once its surrounding whitespace is trimmed, are refused (before, an inner control character put undici's echo of the whole token on the record, and an inner space or non-ASCII character was sent). **(f)** A server run's delivery error carries no part of the hook URL or token (the engine's redaction, above). **(g)** The audit rows name the hook, only for a journey with `notify`: `journey.capture` adds `notify: { urlEnv, authEnv }` (the variables' names, `authEnv` `null` without a token) and `journey.run` adds `notify: { urlEnv, status, attempts }` — `sent`, `skipped` or `failed` and how many requests left the server; on the vantage-lost 502 the delivery its record kept (`runJourney` sets `e.notify`), and `{ status: null, attempts: 0 }` when no record exists — never any part of the URL; a refused run writes none. The README's audit table says so. **(j)** `GET /api/journeys` adds `notify.problem` — the run's refusal text — only for a journey a server run would refuse. **(l)** `GET /api/journeys/:name/runs` serves a record's `notify.error` through `scrubNotifyError`: a record written before this change, or by an older CLI on the shared workspace, never serves undici's echo of a bearer token or of a URL with credentials, nor the hook's host or address in a failed lookup or connection. **(o)** Without sign-in a journey that notifies runs only from a request sent straight to a loopback address, and never on a server bound beyond loopback (403 `denied: 'posture'`, `notifyPostureRefusal` with the bind named — the live MCP API's rule and texts); a journey without `notify` keeps its posture. `server/test-notify-target-policy.mjs` gains H1–H13 and H17 over an identity child (a users file armed; the API token's marker doubling as the bearer) and two `OBSERVOGRAM_AUTH=off` children (loopback; `OBSERVOGRAM_INSECURE_NO_AUTH=1` on 0.0.0.0), every deny-listed variable — the server's own secrets, another org's, bare names — set to a marker: capture's refusals for an operator, an admin, an owner acting in the org, the bearer and the anonymous `local` caller; capture accepted, its file and row; legacy definitions refused before they run, with `notify.problem` on the listing; the sent POST with the hook's own token and the rows of the 200, the vantage-lost 502 and the record-less 502; the pairing; the origin list (no lookup on a refusal; a listed non-loopback address of this host posted to); a 302 never followed; another org's hook and a shadowed name; `packB.mcp.authEnv` never read with its variable set; an unset hook; the posture; the read-back (a pack label with line breaks stays one banner comment line, a gate key or an `env` that would not read back is refused with the earlier file intact); pre-change records served scrubbed; and H17, a scan of every response body, child output, audit row, store file, workspace file and webhook request for the markers. `server/test-smoke.mjs` moves its notify cases to `OBSERVOGRAM_ORG_DEFAULT_NOTIFY_SMOKE_URL`/`_TOKEN` (a bare `SMOKE_HOOK_URL` capture is now its 400); `server/test-tenancy.mjs`, `server/test-authz.mjs` and `server/test-audit-api.mjs` pin the rows' `notify` key, and tenancy pins that acme's origin list never admits a bravo hook (assertions in existing tests). 1448 → 1462 tests.
- **The schedule snippets never bind a notify name the server refuses** (`server/index.mjs`, `tools/lib/schedule-snippets.mjs` — manifest regenerated). Declared change **(k)**: `GET /api/journeys/:name/schedule` splits a journey's notify names with `notifyNamesRefusal` (which reads no environment): `envNames` keeps `packB.mcp.authEnv` and the notify names the server accepts, and a name it refuses — not one of the org's notify hooks, or not the token of the hook the journey's `urlEnv` names — goes to a new body key `refusedEnvNames`, present only when non-empty. `tools/lib/schedule-snippets.mjs`'s input takes `refusedEnvNames` (validated, de-duplicated, minus `envNames`; `normaliseSnippetInput` answers it): each form binds none of them and carries, where the binding would have been, one comment — `# <NAME> is not a notify hook of this org: the studio never reads it — rename it to OBSERVOGRAM_ORG_<KEY>_NOTIFY_<HOOK>_URL/_TOKEN before scheduling (docs/DOWNSTREAM.md §17.2)` in the cron line, the Actions step's `env:` and the CronJob container's `env:`, the same after `REM ` in the schtasks command. A token refused beside the org's own hook (`notify.authEnv` any name but that hook's `_TOKEN`, another of its hooks' included) is told that hook's token: the input's `refusedPairs` (`[{ authEnv, urlEnv, tokenEnv }]`, the route's from `notifyHookOf`; a pair whose names are not all valid, or whose `authEnv` is not refused, is dropped) gives it `# <NAME> is not the token of the hook <urlEnv> names: a hook's token goes only to its own URL — set the journey's notify.authEnv to <tokenEnv>, or remove it, before scheduling (docs/DOWNSTREAM.md §17.2)` instead — the run's own way out, never a rename to a hook's shape (which another hook's token already has). One of the org's hook tokens refused beside a refused `urlEnv` is told its own hook's URL, for the same reason: the input's `refusedHookTokens` (`[{ authEnv, urlEnv }]`, `urlEnv` the hook whose token `authEnv` is — the route's from `notifyHookOf(db, authEnv, org, 'TOKEN')`, which with that suffix reads a name as a hook's token; an entry whose names are not both valid, or whose `authEnv` is not refused, is dropped) gives it `# <NAME> is the token of the hook <urlEnv> names, not of the journey's notify.urlEnv: a hook's token goes only to its own URL — set the journey's notify.urlEnv to <urlEnv>, or remove notify.authEnv, before scheduling (docs/DOWNSTREAM.md §17.2)` instead. One of the org's hook tokens named as `notify.urlEnv` itself — alone, or as its `authEnv` too (one refused name) — is told it is refused as `notify.urlEnv` and its hook's URL, never only to remove `notify.authEnv` (which leaves the urlEnv naming the token, refused): the input's `refusedUrlTokens` (`[{ tokenEnv, urlEnv }]`, `tokenEnv` the refused urlEnv and `urlEnv` the hook whose token it is — the route's from `notifyHookOf(db, urlEnv, org, 'TOKEN')`; an entry whose names are not both valid, or whose `tokenEnv` is not refused, is dropped; it wins over `refusedHookTokens`) gives it `# <NAME> is the token of the hook <urlEnv> names, refused as the journey's notify.urlEnv: the studio posts only to a hook's URL — set the journey's notify.urlEnv to <urlEnv>, and its notify.authEnv to <NAME> or leave it out, before scheduling (docs/DOWNSTREAM.md §17.2)` instead. Every name refused for an org whose every hook name another org's id takes (a KEY tie, an `acme-notify` beside `acme`) is told to leave `notify` out, as that org's run refusal and listing say: the input's `refusedTaken` (`true` only; the route's from `hooksAllTaken`) gives it `# <NAME> cannot be a notify hook of this org: another org's id also maps onto every name of its hook shape (OBSERVOGRAM_ORG_<KEY>_NOTIFY_<HOOK>_URL/_TOKEN) by the longest-prefix rule, so the studio posts no notification for its journeys — remove the journey's notify before scheduling (docs/DOWNSTREAM.md §17.1)` instead — never a rename no name can satisfy (its hook-shaped names already have the shape, and each is another org's too). Otherwise a name of the org's hook shape, URL or token, that another org's id also maps onto (an `acme-notify-eu` beside `acme` takes `OBSERVOGRAM_ORG_ACME_NOTIFY_EU_*`) is told so, as the run's SHADOW refusal says, never a rename to the shape it already has: the input's `refusedShadows` (`[NAME]`, the route's from `notifyHookOf` with either suffix; a name not refused, or not valid, is dropped; `refusedPairs`, `refusedHookTokens`, `refusedUrlTokens` and `refusedTaken` win) gives it `# <NAME> has the shape of a notify hook of this org, but another org's id also maps onto it by the longest-prefix rule, so the studio never reads it — set the journey's notify.urlEnv to one that GET /api/journeys/notify-hooks lists, and its notify.authEnv to that hook's _TOKEN or leave it out, before scheduling (docs/DOWNSTREAM.md §17.2)` instead. Before, the route bound every name the definition held — a file written before the rule could have the k8s snippet bind `OBSERVOGRAM_API_TOKEN` from a Secret for the CronJob. A journey without `notify`, or whose `notify` names its org's hook pair, has the same body and the same snippets as before; `packc journey schedule` passes no `refusedEnvNames` and is unchanged. `server/test-notify-target-policy.mjs` gains H16 (a legacy journey's names refused in every snippet and bound in none; a stranger token beside the org's hook the only name refused, its comment naming that hook's own token; another of the org's hook tokens beside a refused `urlEnv` told its own hook's URL, never a rename, and that way out, followed, binding both names; the org's hook token named as the `urlEnv`, alone or as the `authEnv` too, told it is refused as `notify.urlEnv` and its hook's URL, never only to remove `notify.authEnv`, and that way out, followed with `authEnv` left out, binding the hook; a name another org's id shadows, as the `urlEnv` or as the `authEnv` beside a refused `urlEnv`, told so and a hook the listing lists, as the run says, never a rename; a hook-named journey's body exactly what `scheduleSnippets` makes from the route's inputs; over a child whose orgs file holds `pay` beside `pay-notify`, a hook-shaped and a bare name each told to leave notify out, as the listing's run refusal says, and never to rename it); `tools/test-schedule-snippets.mjs` pins each emitter with `refusedEnvNames`, `refusedPairs`, `refusedHookTokens`, `refusedUrlTokens`, `refusedTaken` and `refusedShadows` (a harness suite). 1462 → 1463 tests.
- **An MCP endpoint's read token never names a notify hook's variable** (`server/store/mcp-endpoints.mjs`, `server/service-admin.mjs`). Declared change **(h)**: `readTokenEnv` — on `POST /api/mcp-endpoints`, on a `PATCH` and in the store's `createMcpEndpoint`/`updateMcpEndpoint` — is refused when it has the URL or TOKEN shape of **any** org's notify hook, live or removed, whoever owns the name by the longest-prefix rule: `observogram store: <name> has the shape of a notify hook's variable (OBSERVOGRAM_ORG_<KEY>_NOTIFY_<HOOK>_URL or _TOKEN), which the server sends only to its own webhook — name another variable of this org (for example <P>MCP_TOKEN)` (400, no row), the example `<P>MCP_READ_KEY` when the org's own `<P>MCP_TOKEN` is itself a hook's token (an org `pay-notify` beside `pay`). Before, an org's admin could register the org's hook token as an MCP read token and have the server send it to the MCP, and an org whose id extends another's (`acme-notify` owns every `OBSERVOGRAM_ORG_ACME_NOTIFY_*` name by the longest prefix) could read that org's hook token as its own. The check is repeated where the token is read (`resolveMcpTarget`, for `POST /api/refresh-live` and `POST /api/draft-from-mcp`): an endpoint registered before this change with such a name answers 400 with that text and `— PATCH /api/mcp-endpoints/<id> names another variable`, before the variable is read, a sent `mcpAuth` included. The hook's shape — `HOOK_RE`, `hookOfShape`, `isOrgHookShaped` — now lives in `server/store/mcp-endpoints.mjs` beside the per-org variable rule it narrows (with `HOOK_NAME_MAX` and `envNameHookText`); `server/notify-target-policy.mjs` imports it and re-exports `HOOK_RE` and `isOrgHookShaped` unchanged. One test each in `server/test-store.mjs` (the shape for every org, live or removed; create and update; the `acme-notify` case and its example; an origin list or a `_KEY` name is no hook) and `server/test-services-api.mjs` (the API's refusals and their rows; a pre-change endpoint refused at use with nothing sent and the value in no response or log line, then repaired by the PATCH; an `acme-notify` planted as an orgs-file import would plant it). 1463 → 1465 tests.
- **Org creation refuses an id that would take another org's notify hooks, whose own another org already takes, or that shares another org's KEY** (`server/identity-admin.mjs` `createOrgFromAdmin` — `POST /api/admin/orgs` and `npm run orgs -- create`). Declared change **(p)**: an org's variables are `OBSERVOGRAM_ORG_<KEY>_<NAME>`, each owned by the org whose prefix is the longest prefix of the name, removed orgs counting — so an org `acme-notify` (or `acme_notify`, `acme-notify-eu`) would own `OBSERVOGRAM_ORG_ACME_NOTIFY_*`, acme's notify hooks, for good (ids are never reused), and an org `a_b` beside `a-b` ties every variable, which refuses a hook for both. After the existing reuse check, counting removed orgs: `org "<id>" would take the notify hook variables of org '<other>' (OBSERVOGRAM_ORG_<OTHER>_NOTIFY_…) by the longest-prefix rule — choose another id`, `org "<id>"'s notify hook variables (OBSERVOGRAM_ORG_<KEY>_NOTIFY_…) are already taken by org '<other>' by the longest-prefix rule — choose another id` and `org "<id>" maps onto the same variables as org '<other>' (OBSERVOGRAM_ORG_<KEY>_) — choose an id that differs in more than - and _` (409 on the API, the same texts from a shell; nothing written). An id that only starts alike — `acme-notifications`, `acme-eu` — is created as before. The orgs-file import is not guarded (it reproduces what exists); the start's `[notify-hooks]` lines name each set hook variable such a pair shadows. `server/test-identity-api.mjs` +1 (the three refusals on the API, a removed org's hooks, an id that only starts alike, the shell's texts, no row and no directory). 1465 → 1466 tests.
- **The studio's notify hints name the org's hook variables, and a journey the server would refuse is not "Set up"** (`studio/journeys-view.mjs`, `studio/neuron-view.mjs`). Declared change **(i)**: `journeys-view.mjs` exports `notifyHookExample(org)` — `OBSERVOGRAM_ORG_<KEY>_NOTIFY_<HOOK>_URL` with `<KEY>` the org id in capitals and `-` as `_` (it mirrors `orgEnvPrefix`), or the literal `<KEY>` when the studio does not know its org (no memberships: `OBSERVOGRAM_AUTH=off`, the bearer — the default org's id is not always `default`, so it is never guessed), `notifyKeyNote(org)` (` <KEY> is this org's id in capitals, with - as _.` in that case) and `notifySetupLine(notify)` — for a declared `notify` before any delivery, `The server will not run this journey as it stands: <problem>` when `GET /api/journeys` carries `notify.problem` (the run's own refusal text, without its `journey <name> is not run: ` lead, which the line's own says), else `Set up: posts <on> to the webhook in $<urlEnv>.`. The Neuron view's Notification row uses it (a legacy journey naming a bare variable read "Set up: posts … to the webhook in $MY_HOOK_URL" though every server run refused it), and adds the refusal after the newest record's delivery too, so a journey that notified before — before the rule, or through the CronJob's CLI — is flagged as well; and the evidence block's hint and the `not-configured` remedy (now read when shown, from the studio's active org) say `Add notify: { urlEnv: OBSERVOGRAM_ORG_<KEY>_NOTIFY_<HOOK>_URL } …` — one of this org's notify hooks, which the server's operator sets — where they said `MY_WEBHOOK_URL`; for an org whose every hook name another org's id takes (`pay` beside `pay-notify`), where no name could satisfy that, they say the server's sentence that none can be set instead: `GET /api/journeys` adds `notifyUnavailable` — the listing's `none` — only for such an org, the two journey listing loaders note it per org (`fetchJourneyListing`) and `notifyUnavailableOf(org)` answers it, capitalised with its full stop. No control, state key or storage key is added (the note is kept in memory); the static bundle is unchanged. `tools/test-neuron-model.mjs` +1 (the example for a known and an unknown org, the remedy as the active org changes, the setup line with and without `problem`, the Notification row under every delivery state — the refusal said for a refused journey, never for one through its org's hook — and the server's sentence for an org that can have no hook, noted by either loader and never borrowed by another org; `server/test-notify-target-policy.mjs` H16 pins `notifyUnavailable` for `pay` beside `pay-notify`, and its absence for `default`). 1466 → 1467 tests. The focus header's row (`.journey-card-meta`, `studio/app.css`) breaks a word that does not fit (`overflow-wrap: anywhere`): a hook's names are single words wider than a phone, and `PLATFORM_ALERTS` — one of `docs/DOWNSTREAM.md` §17's examples — with its token scrolled the page 102 px sideways at 390 px. `server/test-live-studio.mjs` +1 (the Neuron view at 390 px, sign-in off, a journey through that hook: both names whole, no horizontal page scroll). 1467 → 1468 tests.

README "Saved Journeys" (the example names the default org's hook `OBSERVOGRAM_ORG_DEFAULT_NOTIFY_ONCALL_URL`/`_TOKEN`, with how to read the default org's `<KEY>`; the notify paragraph; the four variables), "Roles", "Services, Environments And MCP Endpoints" (a hook-shaped read-token name is refused) and "The Identity API" (org ids that would share variables); `docs/MCP_INTEGRATION.md` "Notify on transitions" (the hook pair in the example, which names the CLI and the server read, the origin list, the request, the schedule snippets); `.env.example` (the CLI's literal names and the server's hook pair, `OBSERVOGRAM_NOTIFY_ORIGINS`); `deploy/k8s/deployment-studio.yaml` (a commented `OBSERVOGRAM_NOTIFY_ORIGINS` and a hook bound from a Secret, with the egress note), `deploy/k8s/components/journeys/cronjob-journeys.yaml` and `deploy/k8s/README.md` (a journey the studio also runs names its org's hook, bound on the studio too); `docs/DELIVERY-REBADGE-BATCH3.md`'s open security item, closed.
