### Follow-up `windows-ci`: a Windows CI leg
- **`tools/tap-summary.mjs`** (new; a `tools/` CLI, not vendored, `node:*` only): the end of a CI log for a `node --test` run. A CI log is read from its tail, so the summary prints there one bounded block read from the run's TAP — the host (platform, OS release, CPUs, cwd, temp directory, whether a symlink can be created), the totals, the skip lines by reason (the browser suites' importer-specific reasons as one line), the `SKIP win32:` count, every failure with its place (the first stack frame in the test's own file, repo-relative, from a POSIX or Windows path or a `file:` URL) and its error (a harness suite's `✗` lines with their got/want; a crash's last 12 lines; each line cut at 500 characters), the timing bounds the suites print (`bound:` lines, nearest their limit first, flagged past half), the slowest tests, and the block's own length — then up to 10 `::error` annotations, the run-level ones first (the count, an unfinished run, an unknown skip reason, a todo), then one per failure with its first 12 error lines, cut alike, and a `::notice` with the totals and the `tail_lines` that reaches the block. An unfinished run (no totals, or a cut inside them: a step timeout, a crash, a cancel) shows the TAP's last 40 lines, each cut at 500 characters, and names the totals it lacks. Every line of the run's own text the block shows — a failure's error, an unfinished run's or a not-started capture's lines — stands behind `| `, `--compare` names a harness file in quotes, as it names a skip reason (`'<file>': …`), a CR in the run's text prints as `\r`, and a `##[` in the run's text the summary prints — a name, a skip reason, an error, an unfinished run's or a not-started capture's lines, a timing bound's text, a failure's place, the host line, `--compare`'s lines, an annotation's `file=`, title and message, the working directory `--win32-skips readme`'s refusals name, and the path of the TAP file or a shape the block and the refusals name (CI's capture sits in `$RUNNER_TEMP`, beside the checkout) — prints as `#\#[` (an annotation's `file=` then names the path as the block shows it, not the file: the runner splits a `::` line with a culture-sensitive search for its second `::`, which finds none before a combining mark or after a prepended character, and reads a line it cannot split for a `##[` in it): the runner executes a log line that starts with `::` once its leading whitespace is trimmed, and one that holds its older form, `##[`, anywhere, and reads a lone CR as a line break, so a failure that prints `::stop-commands::` or `##[stop-commands]` cannot leave the block's own annotations and notice unprocessed, nor a test named `::stop-commands::x.mjs` (a harness file to `--shape`) that ran on one leg only, or printed more assertions or fewer (an early exit) on the run, leave `--compare`'s `::error` annotations unprocessed. `node tools/tap-summary.mjs <tap-file> [--win32-skips readme|<n>] [--max-failures <n>] [--shape <out.json>]` exits 0 only when the totals are all present, nothing failed, was cancelled or is a todo, every skip reason is a `win32: …` one or on `KNOWN_SKIPS` (the browser suites without Playwright or without a browser, the PID 1 test without `unshare`, the unprivileged-user probe run as root — each with its probe), and the run printed the number of `SKIP win32:` lines README "Platforms" states (`readme`) or the number given; 1 otherwise; 2 for a usage error or a file that is not TAP. Spec-reporter output (Node 23 and later print spec even into a file) is refused naming the capture command that works on every Node `engines` admits, `NODE_OPTIONS=--test-reporter=tap npm test > <file> 2>&1` (`cmd /c "set NODE_OPTIONS=--test-reporter=tap&& npm test > <file> 2>&1"` on Windows), the file one word to the shell that runs it: single-quoted for sh when it holds a space or another character sh reads (a `##[` in it as `#''#[`, which sh reads as the same path), and on Windows, where no one quoting carries such a path through both cmd and PowerShell, captured into `test.tap` instead, the command saying so; a capture in which `npm test` never started prints a block of its own, an `::error` and the notice: npm's, node's or a shell's refusal to start the tests names no reporter, which would start nothing — the block shows what the capture holds (its last 40 lines, the cause); an empty capture holds no cause, so its line names the capture command whole, `<file> is empty: npm test printed nothing into it — capture the run again: <cmd>`, `<cmd>` that command; a UTF-16LE capture (Windows PowerShell 5.1's `>`) is read, and one that went through a console code page (`✗` arriving as `Γ£ù`) is warned about with the same command. `--shape` writes the run's shape (totals, skips by reason, per harness suite its ✓ assertions and `- SKIP win32:` lines); `--compare <reference.json> <run.json>…` holds each run to the reference: the same tests, the same harness assertions per file (fewer only under a counted win32 skip, never more), the same non-win32 skip reasons. `tools/test-platform.mjs` P6 also requires README "Platforms" to state its "<N> `SKIP win32:` lines" sentence, at least one line per site. `tools/test-tap-summary.mjs` (new, 13). Suite total 1425 → 1438 tests.
- **CI: a Windows leg** (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`). `windows (node 22.16.0)` and `windows (node 22)` run `npm ci` and `npm test` on `windows-latest` (`fail-fast: false`; every run step under Git Bash's `bash -eo pipefail`), first running the capture command `tap-summary` names on Windows, `cmd /c "set NODE_OPTIONS=--test-reporter=tap&& npm run test:platform > test.tap 2>&1"`, from Windows PowerShell on one suite. The suites' TAP goes through `tee` to a file and the job ends with `tap-summary --win32-skips readme --shape`: a run that prints another number of `SKIP win32:` lines than README "Platforms" states, an unknown skip reason, a todo or any failure is red, and the step timeout (15 minutes: twice the slowest `npm test` measured on the runner, 3m39s, rounded up to 5 minutes and at least 15) sits under the job's (25) so the summary still runs on a hang. `windows-vs-linux` (after node-floor and both Windows legs) holds each Windows run's shape to node-floor's (`--compare`; the shapes travel as artifacts, kept 7 days). **node-floor's test step changes shape** (a declared change): through `tee` under `bash -eo pipefail` with a 7-minute step timeout, then the summary (`--win32-skips 0`) and the shape upload; `validate`'s suites step likewise (8 minutes: twice its slowest c8 run measured on the runner, 3m48s, rounded up; **its job's timeout 10 → 13 minutes**, adding twice the slowest steps before the suites, 2m26s), the summary its last step. A Linux run now also fails on any `SKIP win32:` line, unknown skip reason or todo. The header comment names the triggers the workflow has. `tools/test-tap-summary.mjs` gains S10, the wiring — every trigger and no job condition, the report-command step and each suites, summary and compare step whole (no shell but the one pinned, no `if:` but the summary's, nothing after its command), nothing after the summary but the shape upload in `windows` and `node-floor` and nothing after it in `validate`, each measured step timeout at least twice the slowest run its comment names and the number the comment's rounding gives from it, as each timeout this file derives from a measured run must be — and holds DOWNSTREAM §13's report commands to the one `tap-summary` names. Suite total 1438 → 1439 tests.
- **Test and fixture fixes for Windows** (tests and fixtures only; no runtime, `tools/lib` or `studio/` change; on Linux every edited assertion accepts what it accepted). Found on the runner or predicted: an environment path compared through `resolve()` (`server/test-store-import.mjs`); the tenancy line's literal `orgs/default/<entry>` (`server/test-store-ops.mjs`); `server/test-store.mjs`'s `child()` and `packc()` resolve on `close`, once the child's output is read to its end — on Windows a child's last write can arrive after its `exit`. Pre-emptive: the cron line's workspace may be a quoted path holding a space (`tools/test-journey.mjs`); the shallow clone's URL from `pathToFileURL` (`tools/test-build-info.mjs`); the fetch-live CLI child inherits the environment minus every variable the CLI reads, in any case, so it keeps `SystemRoot` (`tools/test-fetch-live.mjs`); `childEnv` drops a STRIP or per-org name whatever its case, and `dropInheritedOrgVars()` (`server/fixtures/serve-child.mjs`, new) replaces the per-org loop in 16 suites — `server/test-hermetic-suites.mjs` plants lower- and mixed-case names and refuses a suite that spells the loop by hand; a refused connection goes to `127.0.0.1:2`, never to a port just closed, which Windows can hand out again (port 1 is on the Fetch standard's bad-port list and is refused before any connect; `tools/test-journey.mjs`, `tools/test-mcp-ping.mjs`, `server/test-mcp-ping.mjs`); the secret scans of `server/test-mcp-settings.mjs` and `server/test-mcp-settings-studio.mjs` skip `-shm`, the WAL index the win32 VFS locks (it holds no row's bytes); the seven browser loaders import an absolute `OBSERVOGRAM_PLAYWRIGHT` as a file URL, so the knob takes a Windows path too. The count of tests is unchanged by them.
- **`bound:` lines** (a declared change): every wall-clock bound the runner can miss prints what it measured, `bound: <what>: <ms> ms < <limit> ms` (or `>= <floor> ms of a <hold> ms hold`), at 11 sites in `server/test-store.mjs`, `server/test-mcp-settings.mjs`, `tools/test-journey.mjs`, `tools/test-live-snapshot.mjs`, `tools/test-mcp-server-settings.mjs` (one per pattern) and `tools/test-mcp-transport.mjs`; the summary lists them nearest the limit first. In a terminal's spec output they show as `ℹ bound: …` lines. The hung journey receiver's line prints the time past its two 1000 ms timeouts against 1500 ms (the same assertion as 3500 ms in all). Every assertion is unchanged.
- **Windows is tested in CI** (docs). README "Platforms" moves from "expected, not verified" to tested on `windows-latest` (GitHub-hosted, elevated), with what the runner's summary printed (Windows 10.0.26100, Node v22.16.0 and v22.23.3, the checkout on `D:`, `TEMP` an 8.3 short name on `C:`, a symlink created, `npm test` in 138–219 s); symlink creation is skipped on every Windows host, elevated or not (the "fewer on an elevated runner" sentence and the "4 symlink skips" were wrong), and the unchecked 18 + 1 split is gone. "On Windows, today" states what holds there now: the directory's ACL protects the database, its backups and `users.json` (Windows does not enforce `0600`; also README's backup and restore paragraphs and STORE_PLAN), the network-filesystem refusal is Linux-only, journey names that differ only in case share a definition and a runs folder, and journey listings follow the directory's order. DOWNSTREAM §13: the CI leg is the acceptance for `windows-latest`, and a Windows box of one's own reports with `cmd /c "set NODE_OPTIONS=--test-reporter=tap&& npm test > test.tap 2>&1"` and `node tools/tap-summary.mjs test.tap --win32-skips readme` (Git Bash: `NODE_OPTIONS=--test-reporter=tap npm test > test.tap 2>&1`) — **the report command names the reporter**, because Node 23 and later print spec even into a file; a test that fails only on Windows is first made OS-neutral, a skip only for a fact Windows cannot express. §8 follows; HANDOVER's CI list names the new jobs; `docs/BRANCHING.md` and `.github/copilot-instructions.md`: a red CI leg on develop, Linux or Windows, is fixed forward or reverted within a working day. The PR's all-green time was 5m31s on the green run (validate under c8 the slowest; a Windows job at most 4m38s), up from validate's ~4 minutes.

### Follow-up `windows-path-scope`: on Windows a path is compared by what it names, not by how it is spelled
- **`tools/lib/journey.mjs`** — a studio-run journey's org scope (A-24) holds on Windows (a fix there; Linux and macOS compare exactly as before). On NTFS one file has many spellings — another case, another drive-letter case, `/` for `\`, a short (8.3) name — and JS `realpathSync` keeps the caller's, so with the workspace at `C:\ws` an acme journey's `packA: { file: C:/WS/orgs/beta/packs/b1.pack.yaml }` (or `c:/ws/…`, or `C:\WS~1\…`) read as a file outside the workspace, nobody's part, and was loaded into acme's run; the default org's `C:\ws\Orgs\beta\…` likewise. On Windows the scope now compares both sides — the workspace, the org's root, and every `file:` source, crawl root, walked directory and file, and inventory site — as the native realpath (`realpathSync.native`: long names, the case on disk) of the longest prefix that exists, the rest appended as written, with `\` separators and case folded. A network share is one more name that realpath keeps: a path reached through one (`\\localhost\C$\ws\orgs\beta\…`, `//127.0.0.1/c$/…`, `\\?\UNC\…`, a mapped drive, another name of the share's server) resolves to `\\host\share\…`, never to the volume behind it, so it too read as outside the workspace; on Windows a path outside the workspace is now nobody's part only on a local drive, and any other is refused (`… is not a path on a local drive, and a network share or a device path may lead into another org's part of the workspace — refused; name it by its path on a local drive (copy it there if it has none)`). The mirror: a workspace named through a share this host serves (`OBSERVOGRAM_WORKSPACE=\\myhost\ws`, or a drive mapped to `\\localhost\C$\ws`) is stored on a local drive, and its path there is one more name for it, so beta's `C:\ws\orgs\beta\…` read as outside the workspace on a local drive; while the workspace is named through a share, a path on a local drive outside it is now placed by its nearest directory that is the workspace's own — the same file id on the same volume (`statSync`, with the volume serial's low 32 bits, all a share reports) — and judged there. That form only compares: a refusal names the path as written, and nothing stored or returned changes. `crawlScopeRefusal(scope, { windows, path, realpathSync, statSync })` (new: `''` when the org may read a path, else the refusal's reason) and `crawlScopeTest` (new: the same as true or false) are exported so the rule is proven with an injected host. `tools/test-journey-scope.mjs` (new, 10) drives it with Windows' path rules and an NTFS volume's realpath (`server/fixtures/ntfs.mjs`, new: case, short names, `\\?\` and `\\.\` paths, shares and mapped drives as libuv resolves them, a file id the same through every name) on any host: for the workspace `C:\ws`, beta's file in every spelling — on the drive, through any name of a share, through a mapped drive — is refused to acme and to the default org, acme's own on the drive in every spelling is read, an org whose directory is not created yet follows the case fold, and `C:\ws2` and `orgs\acme2` are not inside; for a workspace named through a share this host serves (`\\myhost\ws`, `Z:`), beta's file named by its local path in every spelling is refused and acme's own is read; for a workspace on a share (`\\nas01\obs`), its own share name decides as a drive path does, another name of its server is refused, and where this host reaches the share's disk, its local path is the workspace too; the same volume compared by spelling lets each cross-org read through; and the default host, on a real workspace, refuses beta's file with the workspace spelled in another case exactly when the suite runs on Windows, so a Windows run fails if the platform's rule is not the one chosen (the expected verdict reads the platform fixture's `isWin32`, a reviewed entry in `tools/test-platform.mjs`'s `ISWIN32_ALLOWED`). `VENDOR-MANIFEST.json` is unchanged: `journey.mjs` is an excluded module. Suite total 1401 → 1411 tests.
- **The store's offline operations compare paths the same way on Windows** (`server/store/paths.mjs`, new: `comparedPath(own, { windows, path, realpathSync, statSync })`, which answers `compared(p, beside, { deleting })`; `server/store/ops.mjs`, `server/store/backup.mjs`; a fix there, Linux and macOS compare exactly as before). By spelling, with the workspace at `C:\ws`: `packc store export C:\WS\exports` wrote a directory export inside the workspace, without the in-place steps, instead of refusing it; `packc store export C:\WS` was not taken for the in-place export (it was refused as a directory that is not empty); a database at `C:\WS~1\observogram.db` was refused as living outside a workspace with no marker; `packc store purge-org beta` deleted the recorded users file when it was spelled `C:\WS\orgs\beta\users.json`; and `packc store backup c:/ws/Observogram.db-wal` wrote the backup over the database's own `-wal`, which the next open deletes (a restore from the database itself, named another way, was not refused either). Each check — a directory export's refusal, the in-place detection, the no-marker database check and its scan for another store's database, purge-org's root and users-file checks, the backup's and the restore's own-file check — compares through `comparedPath`: off Windows the module's own realpath form, unchanged; on Windows the journey scope's comparison (above). A network share is one more name for a directory this host serves, so with the workspace and the database named through one (`OBSERVOGRAM_WORKSPACE=\\myhost\ws`, or a drive mapped to it) a target named by its local path compared as outside them — `packc store purge-org beta` deleted a recorded `C:\ws\orgs\beta\users.json`, a directory export was written into `C:\ws\exports`, a backup was written over `C:\ws\observogram.db-wal` — and with them at `C:\ws`, so did a share name the admin gave for them (`\\localhost\C$\ws\exports`, `\\localhost\C$\ws\observogram.db-wal`). On Windows, when a path and the one it is checked against resolve under different roots (a drive, a share), the path is now placed by its nearest directory that is the other one or one of its ancestors — the same file id on the same volume (`statSync`, with the volume serial's low 32 bits, as the journey scope places one) — and compared from there. A path through a share that no file id places may still lie inside them — a share of a directory inside the workspace or under the purged root (`\\myhost\exports` for `C:\ws\exports`, or a drive mapped to it) names none of their directories, so with the workspace at `C:\ws` a directory export to `\\myhost\exports\new` was written into it, and `packc store purge-org beta` deleted `C:\ws\orgs\beta\sub\users.json` recorded as `\\myhost\betasub\users.json` — so on their volume, or through a share (or a drive mapped to one) that cannot be reached when the check runs, it is now refused, naming the way out: a directory export there (`… is not a path on a local drive, and a network share on the volume that holds the workspace … (or on another machine's volume that has its serial, …), or one that cannot be reached, may lead inside it … Name the directory by its path on a local drive; if it has none on this host, export to an empty or new directory on a local drive outside the workspace, then copy that directory to <target>`), purge-org with the users file recorded through one (`… nothing was deleted. If it does not lie under <root>, delete <root> by hand, then run purge-org again: it drops org <id>'s records`), and the no-marker check of an in-place export, which refused such a database as living outside the workspace and now says it may or may not lie in it (`… Set OBSERVOGRAM_DB to the database's path under <workspace>, then run the export again …`; refused before and after). A declared false refusal: a share of a directory outside them on the same volume (`\\myhost\out` for `C:\out`) is refused alike, since no file id tells it apart; named on the drive, it is judged as before. A share that cannot be reached (its server stopped, its name not resolving), like a drive that does not exist, has no volume to read, so nothing tells where it leads; a share that can be reached on a volume whose serial differs from theirs — another volume of this host, or another host's — stays an ordinary export target. To purge-org, whose "outside" answer deletes the root, no share is: its users-file check passes `{ deleting: true }`, and a users file recorded through any share that no file id places — by its name or a drive mapped to one — is refused with the way out above, whatever volume it reports (a declared false refusal for a share outside the root). A DFS link sits below a namespace root another server answers: with the users file recorded as `\\corp\dfs\betasub\users.json` (a link to `\\myhost\betasub`, which is `C:\ws\orgs\beta\sub`), or through a drive mapped to the namespace, while the link's target is down the nearest path that answers is the namespace root, whose volume is not the one the link leads to, so no serial tells where it leads; by spelling, `packc store purge-org beta` deleted it. One whose volume serial equals the workspace volume's (or the purged root's) is refused alike when no file id places it, whether that volume is this host's or one of another machine cloned from the same image — a cloned disk keeps its serial, and the low 32 bits a share reports do not tell hosts apart (a declared false refusal too) — with those ways out: another host's share has no path on this host's drives, so there the export's way out is a directory on a local drive outside the workspace, copied to the share afterwards, and purge-org's is deleting the root by hand. Placement only ever makes a check more conservative, since a file id names a file on one volume, not on one host: a sector copy keeps the MFT, so another machine cloned from this host's image (`\\machineb\C$\ws`), or this drive's disk image attached as `E:`, keeps the serial and every copied file's id — the root directory's among them, MFT record 5 on every NTFS volume — and is placed on this host's same path. So placement decides a containment refusal (a directory export inside the workspace, purge-org's users file under the root), where a false "inside" refuses with the ways out named; the checks whose "same" or "inside" answer writes, skips a file or takes it for the database's own — the in-place detection, the scan for another store, the no-marker check, the backup's and the restore's own-file check — decide by path alone, and a path only its file id equates is refused naming the way out (the scan reads that database instead of skipping it): with the workspace at `C:\ws`, `packc store export \\localhost\C$\ws` (or `\\machineb\C$\ws`, or `E:\ws`) is not exported in place (`… matches the workspace C:\ws only by its file id, not by its path … To export the workspace in place, stop the server and run packc store export C:\ws; otherwise choose an empty or new directory`); a backup to `\\localhost\C$\ws\observogram.db-wal` and a restore from `E:\ws\observogram.db` are refused, never called the database itself (`… only by its file id, not by its path … Back up to a new path under C:\`, and `… If it is a backup, copy it to a new path under C:\, then restore from the copy`); and the no-marker check refuses a database only its file id puts in the workspace with the refusal above, whose way out names it under the workspace as `OBSERVOGRAM_WORKSPACE` does. A workspace at a share's root (`\\myhost\ws`) now contains the paths under it to these checks: its compared form ended in a separator, so nothing compared as inside it. Every message, record and return value names the path as given. `server/test-store-paths.mjs` (new, 14): each check's predicate over the injected Windows host for the workspace `C:\ws`, the spelling comparison failing each, `C:\ws2` and `orgs\beta2` kept apart; the containment refusals for the workspace and the database named through a share this host serves (`\\myhost\ws`, `Z:`) with targets named on the drive, and for them at `C:\ws` with targets named through a share of it, the comparison by name alone failing each, the in-place, scan, no-marker and own-file checks refusing each such target only its file id equates (the scan reading it) and passing the workspace or the database by its own path, a share on another volume and one of another host written as ordinary targets; a share of the workspace's exports (by its name and a mapped drive), orgs, a directory under beta's root and the db directory refused by the export, purge-org and the no-marker check (the export and the purge went through before), each path named on the drive judged as the way out, a same-volume share outside the workspace refused alike, and one on another volume or of another host with its own serial written; the users file recorded through `\\myhost\betasub` (by its name, a drive mapped to it or a device path) refused by purge-org while that share cannot be reached, as while it is served, and an export through a share that cannot be reached refused, neither the workspace, the database nor its own file; the users file recorded through a DFS link whose target is down (`\\corp\dfs\betasub\users.json`, by its name, a device path or a drive mapped to the namespace), its namespace root answering on another volume, and through any other share no file id places (on another volume, of another host, of a clone's `D:`) refused by purge-org (the purge went through before), one on a local drive outside the root or placed outside it by file id purged, and the export's comparison of such shares unchanged; a machine cloned from this host's image and this drive's disk image attached as `E:`, modelled as a sector copy keeps them (this `C:`'s serial and every copied file's id, the root directory's among them): the in-place export never taken on a copy, the scan reading the workspace's own database beside one, the no-marker check refusing one, a restore from `E:\ws\observogram.db` and a backup to a copy's `-wal` refused by file id alone, never as the database's own, each way out passing, the containment refusals holding, a share of a directory the clone made later refused by the export and purge-org (the export's way out to a local directory written), and a share of its `D:` written by the export as an ordinary target; the default host on a real directory (one spelled in another case is itself exactly when the suite runs on Windows; `isWin32` again, allowlisted likewise), and a pin that every comparison in `ops.mjs` and `backup.mjs` goes through it — by path alone where its answer writes or skips — and each refusal of a path it cannot place, or places by file id alone, names its way out. Suite total 1411 → 1425 tests.

### Follow-up `policy-worker`: the settings-policy deadline is per rule, a worker that cannot start fails closed, and the modal draws the acknowledgement a 409 names
- **Each settings-policy rule gets the pass-through's 100 ms deadline from its own start** (`server/mcp-settings-eval.mjs`; no route, variable, default, log line or text changes). One deadline counted from the worker's start covered every rule, so a worker thread that lost the CPU on a loaded machine — a busy server, a CI runner — could count fast rules as timed out: fail-closed (their acknowledgements required although they would not have matched), never a hole. The worker now posts each rule's start (a rule's answer and the next rule's start in one message) and the main thread restarts the deadline at each, first reading whatever the worker posted before a deadline it may have fallen behind; a rule that does not finish within 100 ms of its start ends the check, and it and the rules after it, never run, count as matched as before. A check takes at most the worker's start (10 s) plus 100 ms per rule — 3.2 s for the 32 rules a policy may hold. `POLICY_EVAL_DEADLINE_MS` stays 100. `server/test-mcp-settings.mjs`: the evaluator's case gives its fast rules a 1 s deadline (the slow pattern misses it all the same), adds a rule after the slow one (counted as matched, never run) and compiles without the id clocks it does not test; its wall-clock bound and the server case's — 2 s for the submit and 1.5 s for the request beside it, which flaked under load — become seconds, which still prove the server never waits for the pattern (minutes). The server case's approved-host submit runs on a child, whose route keeps the default 100 ms, and with one rule a deadline per rule is the one from the start: on a loaded machine that rule can still time out (fail closed), so that submit is sent again — at most five times in all — only while the answer is that 409 and the child has logged the rule's `did not finish within 100 ms` line; a 409 without the line fails at once. MCP_INTEGRATION, DOWNSTREAM §16.2 and the batch's delivery report (a dated note) say "per rule". The count is unchanged.
- **A server started with a flag a worker refuses starts the settings-policy worker without its flags, and a worker that cannot be constructed fails closed** (`server/mcp-settings-eval.mjs`; no route, variable, default or text changes). The worker was handed the server's own flags but `--input-type`, and the `Worker` constructor refuses a V8 or process-wide flag (`ERR_WORKER_INVALID_EXEC_ARGV`), so a server started as `node --max-old-space-size=512 server/index.mjs` with the pass-through on and a settings policy (`OBSERVOGRAM_MCP_SETTINGS_POLICY`) answered every submit, acknowledged or not, `502 the server-settings proxy failed (Error)` — nothing sent and nothing logged, but no way through (a server without a policy builds no worker and was unaffected) — although `evaluatePolicy` was documented never to reject. The worker is now constructed once more with an empty flag list (never the inherited one, which brings `--input-type` back), each attempt on a channel of its own; any other constructor failure, or a second refusal, fails closed like a worker that fails (which counts every rule it did not finish as matched): every rule counts as matched and one stderr line, `[mcp-settings] the policy worker failed (<class>) — rules[…] counted as matched`, names the error's class, never its message. The whole check is wrapped — the rules read once into plain records, an entry that is no rule with an index (`null`, `undefined`) none, and options that cannot be read failing closed like a worker that cannot be constructed —, and a policy without rules (none, no list of them, a list that cannot be read — its length throws, or a revoked `Proxy` —, or only such entries) answers as `null` does — no findings —, so `evaluatePolicy` never rejects, whatever it is given; its new `execArgv` and `createWorker` options are the suites' seams. Three more tests in `server/test-mcp-settings.mjs`: the refused flag in-process (the constructor given the server's flags but `--input-type`, then none; the findings `policyFindings`' own, the slow rule counted at its deadline) and in a child started with `--max-old-space-size`; a constructor that throws, a flag refused twice and a worker that fails once constructed (every rule matched — after a rule finished, every rule but that one, which keeps its findings —, one line naming the class and never the message; a log that throws costs only its line; a policy without rules: no findings, no worker, no line; a `null` or `undefined` entry beside the rules, which threw on the fail-closed path, is none, and options that cannot be read fail closed); and the per-rule deadline, pinned with a paced stand-in worker (two rules taking 0.6 of the deadline each both finish, where one deadline shared from the start counted the second as matched). MCP_INTEGRATION, DOWNSTREAM §16.2, the route's comment and the batch's delivery report (its dated note) say so. Suite total 1392 → 1395 tests.
- **The settings policy loads without the id clocks, so a loaded machine never refuses a valid policy at start** (`server/mcp-settings-policy.mjs`; no route, variable, default or text changes). `readSettingsPolicyConfig` compiled the policy with `compileBoundedPattern`'s id clocks (four runs against 256-character ids, refused past 50 ms of wall time), so a server, or a suite's child, that lost the CPU while loading refused to start with a valid policy (`OBSERVOGRAM_MCP_SETTINGS_POLICY: <path>: rules[0].when: pattern too slow against a 256-character id`, naming no way out), and a pattern with a shape fault could get that refusal in place of its own: of 300 fresh processes loading the suite's policy under twelve busy loops on four cores, 1 refused at the branch's head and none after. The load now compiles with `timed: false`, as the studio does, and checks each pattern's shape, never its speed — as MCP_INTEGRATION and DOWNSTREAM §16.2 always said; the pass-through's worker deadline per rule is the guard. The taxonomy's loader and the bundle build (`tools/build-studio-bundle.mjs`, no server) keep the clocks, so a pattern a server loads can fail the build as too slow; the bundle loader's comment, DOWNSTREAM §10 and the comments on `compileSettingsPolicy` (`tools/lib/mcp-server-settings.mjs`) and `compileBoundedPattern` (`tools/lib/artefact-classify.mjs`) say so (comments only, no export changed; `VENDOR-MANIFEST.json` regenerated), and `tools/test-studio-bundle.mjs` T8d pins it under the jumping clock below (no test added). One more test in `server/test-mcp-settings.mjs`: under a clock that jumps a second per read, the suite's policy and a generic one load, and a bad pattern keeps its shape refusal. Suite total 1395 → 1396 tests.
- **The pass-through's 409 for a missing acknowledgement says when the server could not check the rule it names** (`server/mcp-settings-eval.mjs` `policyAckRefusal`, `server/routes/mcp-settings.mjs`; additive — no route, variable, default or text changes). `409 { ok: false, denied: 'policy-ack', rule, error }` gains `unevaluated: true` when the server could not check that rule — it did not finish within its deadline, the worker could not start or failed (each counted as matched), the rule cannot run on the form, or a value it checks is over 512 characters (the pattern never runs on it) — and has no such key when the rule's pattern matched (the rule's first unticked finding decides, so a rule by type that matched one field and could not check another carries it or not by the fields' order); like the rest of the answer it is value-free (the rule's index, its warn and its ack, never a value or a pattern). A server started under the permission model without `--allow-worker` (`node --permission --allow-fs-read=* --allow-fs-write=* --allow-child-process server/index.mjs`) answers every submit with a settings policy so — the approved host included —, and a loaded one now and then (about 1 in 600 approved submits under twelve busy loops on four cores, as measured in review); an API caller gets through with `acks: [N]` as before, and the flag lets a caller say why the rule applies. One more test in `server/test-mcp-settings.mjs`: the refusal names the first unticked rule and carries the flag for a rule a worker that cannot start counted as matched, a rule past its deadline, a rule that cannot run on the form and a value too long to check, never for a match, and no value or pattern; the policy re-check case's 409s for a URL over 512 characters and on the generic form, and the slow-pattern case's 409, assert the flag end to end, and its 409 for a matched host asserts none (unless the child logged that rule past its deadline). README "API Surface", MCP_INTEGRATION and DOWNSTREAM §16.3 name it. Suite total 1396 → 1397 tests.
- **The Server settings modal draws the acknowledgement of a rule the pass-through's 409 names** (**a declared default change**: the modal drew a box only for a rule its own in-page check flagged; `studio/mcp-settings-model.mjs` `serverAckModel`, `secretsEmptiedLine`, `policyView`'s third argument and `actionBlock`'s `named`, `studio/app.mjs`, `studio/services-api.mjs`, `studio/mcp-settings-view.mjs`). A rule the studio server could not check — every rule on a server whose policy worker cannot be built, a rule past its deadline on a loaded one — or one its check matched where the page's did not answered `The studio server answered 409: <warn> — tick "<ack>" in the server settings.` with nothing to tick: a dead end for the modal's reader (an API caller sent `acks: [N]`). The modal now draws that rule's box — its warn and ack from the policy the page holds, else from the 409's sentence — with one line saying why: `The studio server could not check these settings against this rule, so it counts as matched — tick the box to send them with your approval.` for a 409 that says `unevaluated: true`, else `The studio server's check of these settings matched this rule, where the check in this page did not.` The send waits for the tick as for any other box, and so does a declared action that carries a field the page's copy of the rule checks (any field it sends when the page does not hold the rule) — the page's own box when its check found the rule on another field —, so nothing is sent meanwhile; ticked, the next submit carries the rule's index in `acks`, which attests exactly what an API caller's `acks: [N]` does, and the 409's sentence, followed literally, now works. A later 409 naming another rule adds that rule's box; an edit of any value but a secret (no rule reads one), the generic form's names, a fresh read or a reopen takes the boxes away and the page's check decides again. The send emptied the secrets it carried, so after such a 409 the notice adds `The secret you typed was emptied when the request left — type it again before you send.` (`The secrets you typed were …` for more than one), and that line stays — through the tick the 409 asks for, an untick or another edit — until each is typed again, so the 409's sentence followed in its order — tick, then type it again — sends the configure with it (the tick erased the line, and a send then went without the secret). `servicesRefusal` keeps a denial's `rule` and `unevaluated: true` on the Error it throws (value-free), and the view tells the controller whether an edit was a secret's. `server/fixtures/serve-child.mjs` `serve()` takes `execArgv`, node flags before the script. One more test in `tools/test-mcp-settings-model.mjs` (the refusal read through `servicesRefusal`; the box from the page's policy or from the 409's sentence; both lines; drawn only where the page's check found nothing, blocking until ticked — an action only when it carries a field the rule checks, by field or by type, or any field for a rule the page does not hold, and when the page found that rule by type on a field the action does not carry —, another rule's box added in rule order; any other refusal draws nothing; the emptied-secret line) and three more in `server/test-mcp-settings-studio.mjs` (BROWSER 14: a child started under the permission model without `--allow-worker` — the approved host, which the page's check passes, is a 409 naming rule 0 with `unevaluated: true`; the box with its line; no submit without the tick; ticked first, as the 409 says, the line asking for the emptied secret stays; typed again, it goes and the box stays ticked; the submit carries `acks: [0]` and the secret, the configure goes and the audit row records the index; an edit of the user field takes the box away; a declared action that carries the URL waits for the box like the send — its own 409 draws it, clicked unticked it sends nothing, ticked it carries `acks: [0]` — and one that carries nothing never waits; BROWSER 15: that child with a policy of two acknowledgements — each 409 names the first unticked rule and adds its box beside those drawn, rule 0's staying ticked when rule 1's appears, so ticking what each names reaches a submit with `acks: [0, 1]` that goes, where a box that replaced the last one looped between the two 409s; BROWSER 16: that child with a rule by type on two URL fields, the page's check flagging the first — a declared action that carries only the second is a 409 naming that rule, then waits for the page's box like the send, clicked unticked it sends nothing, and ticked it carries `acks: [0]` and goes, where it stayed enabled and sent again with `acks: []`). MCP_INTEGRATION's pass-through section, DOWNSTREAM §16.2 and §16.3 and the batch's delivery report (its dated note) say so. Suite total 1397 → 1401 tests.

### Follow-up: the SERVICE chip at phone width — the OBSERVA bar fits a phone in every mode
- **The OBSERVA bar is two rows at phone width** (**a declared default change** to the bar's layout at 720 px and below; deferred in 6b-ii, #153, whose `app.css` comment said the page still scrolled sideways in a pack's views). In a pack's views — Discover, Diagnose, Remediate and every Advanced view, the modes that show the SERVICE chip — the bar was 472–507 px wide at 320, 360 and 390 px (an operator, a viewer, an owner acting in an org), the action cluster past the page's edge; and in every mode with tabs the three tabs were squeezed to 28 px each, drawn on top of one another. At 720 px and below (`studio/ux.css`, its phone block) the bar wraps in the markup's order, so the Tab key reads it as the eye does (WCAG 1.3.2, 2.4.3; `order` places only the break, never one control before another): the brand, the ORG chip and the SERVICE chip on the first row; a zero-height break; then the tabs, which share the second, and the action cluster at its end (the home has no tabs — it names no journey — and the action cluster ends its one row). The chrome's side padding is 16 px there and its gaps 8 px. The SERVICE chip's name takes the ORG chip's 8ch phone cap (`studio/app.css`): the button's `aria-label` and `title` name the service in full, a label (a service no record covers) now carries `title="service: <name>"` (`studio/app.mjs` `updateObservaServiceChip`), and the context bar's SERVICE select shows it. Each tab is a size container: under 100 px of content it shows its number alone, centred — its `title` and `aria-label` name the step — and otherwise its number and its question, 13 px, in up to two lines. At 320, 360, 390 and 720 px, in both themes and every mode, the bar scrolls nothing sideways and every control in it — the brand, both chips, each tab, Advanced, the account menu — sits inside the viewport, none on top of another, each tab 40 px wide or more, each right of the one before it in the markup on its row or on a row below; in headless Chromium the chrome is 101–108 px tall in a journey (it was 109) and 59 px on the home. Above 720 px nothing moves. A pack's view draws its own content, which the bar no longer hides: at a phone width some views' tables and fact lists (Diagnose, OTLP Coverage, References, Schema, Traceability, Neuron; Discover and Conformance at 320 px) still scroll the page sideways by themselves.
- **Tests**: `server/test-settings-studio.mjs`'s long one-org label subtest sweeps every mode at 320, 360, 390 and 720 px in both themes — the home, a service page, Discover (the SERVICE chip capped, the button naming the service in full), the Diagnose and Remediate tabs, the seven Advanced views, Discover with the chip a label (the services table unread, the home's derived tile: a `<span>` whose `title` is `service: payment-service`, its name capped), Settings, Build as oscar, and Discover as olive (an owner in three orgs: the ORG switcher beside the SERVICE chip): the bar's own scroll width is the viewport's, every control inside it and none on top of another, each tab 40 px wide or more, the controls in the markup's order (the Tab key's: none takes a positive `tabindex`) each right of the one before on its row or on a row below, and no horizontal page scroll on the home, a service page, Settings and Build. Against the previous stylesheets it fails at the first screen with tabs (a service page at 320 px: the tabs 28 px wide, on top of each other and of the action cluster); in a pack's views the previous bar measured 472–507 px. The subtest is extended, not added: the suite total stays at 1392 tests. No vendored module changed.

### Follow-up `auth-off-way-in-studio`: without sign-in, the studio names the server's own way in
- **The MCP panel's Server settings button and the empty MCP picker's lines say the server's sentence** (**a declared default change** to both texts; deferred by name in 6b-ii, #153). In the token posture the button said `Configuring the MCP server needs a signed-in admin — this server has no sign-in: add the first user with npm run users -- add <login>, or configure OIDC.` and the picker hint `registering one needs a signed-in admin (npm run users -- add <login>)` — a way in `OBSERVOGRAM_AUTH=off` defeats (adding a user arms nothing there, and it beats OIDC). Both now take `GET /api/mcp-endpoints`' `policy.register.why`, the server's sentence for this reader, whose way in is `server/authz.mjs` `noSignInWay` (under `OBSERVOGRAM_AUTH=off`: restart without it, once a user exists or with OIDC configured), so the studio keeps no copy of the rule. `studio/mcp-settings-model.mjs` `settingsGateModel` reads the token posture as it reads the open one — `Configuring the MCP server is endpoint configuration: <the server's sentence>.`, `Checking whether you may configure the MCP server…` while unread, `Could not check whether you may configure the MCP server — close and reopen the panel to try again.` after a failed read, neither naming a way in — and its `NO_SIGN_IN` constant is gone. `studio/settings-model.mjs` `mcpTargetModel` (new input `registerWhy`) and `mcpTargetMissingText` (new inputs `posture`, `registerWhy`) say `No MCP endpoint is registered in <org> yet — <the server's sentence>.` wherever there is no sign-in: the token posture, and the open postures too, where `an admin registers them in Settings → MCP endpoints` was untrue bound off the loopback (closed while exposed) and to a request not sent straight to it (only from this machine); when the server named none, no way in is named (`registering one needs a signed-in admin`, `registering one is not open to you here`). When the policy's read failed and the list was then read without it (Settings, an environment's editor), the pickers drew that empty list beside the failed policy with the posture's line as develop says it — on an open loopback server `No MCP endpoint is registered in <org> yet — an admin registers them in Settings → MCP endpoints.` — and never said the policy could not be read. The pickers now draw no list while the policy is unread or its read failed (`studio/app.mjs` `mcpPickerEndpoints`; a list drawn there with no typed URL allowed would preselect its first endpoint over the person's own choice — the deploy modal's Type a URL… — and Go would deploy there): the picker says `<org>'s MCP endpoints could not be read just now — reopen this to try again.` and, with nothing to send, its status line the same (`mcpTargetMissingText`: new input `unreadable`; it said `choose one of <org>'s MCP endpoints`), never a refusal — and the deploy history's roll back toast, which appended ` in the target form first` to that line as it said it and so read `Choose one of <org>'s MCP endpoints in the target form first` after the failed read, never that the list could not be read (and beside an empty list `No MCP endpoint is registered in <org> yet — register one in Settings → MCP endpoints in the target form first`, two instructions run together), leads with what roll back needs and ends on the line as it reads, one sentence for every line (`studio/settings-model.mjs` new `rollbackTargetMissingText`, **a declared default change** to the toast): `Roll back needs an MCP target in the form above: <the line>.` — and a picker's next opening reads the list again whenever the policy's read failed, the home's next drawing too (`studio/app.mjs` `openMcpTarget`, `paintHomeMcpTarget`; its policy unread meanwhile, so the pickers draw no list until it answers, and the list kept for the view that drew it — a picker's read that fails, however many are in flight, never takes the list another read drew (`readMcpEndpointsForPickers`), so Settings → MCP endpoints, which read it, keeps every row's Edit… while the panel reads again, opened once or twice, and after those reads fail, and when the home's first read fails after Settings drew the list), so that retry, and the Server settings button's `close and reopen the panel`, work. Signed in, both read as before, that failed read aside (said the same way in every posture). In the token posture the panel's opening now reads `GET /api/mcp-endpoints` (`studio/app.mjs` `readMcpGatePolicy`; again at the next opening after a failed read, whose button says `Checking whether you may configure the MCP server…` until that read answers — never `close and reopen` while the panel reads again) for the button alone: the pickers there still draw no list and keep the typed URL, and the home reads nothing; the identity and open postures read as before — and there too the next opening after a failed read reads the list again (`studio/app.mjs` `openMcpTarget`) and, until it answers, the button says `Checking whether you may configure the MCP server…` and the picker names no failure (it said `close and reopen the panel to try again` and `could not be read just now — reopen this to try again` while it read again) — and the static bundle reads nothing new. The home's **Register and connect** refusal kept a copy of its own (`studio/settings-model.mjs` `mcpRegisterCheck`, a remote origin typed on an open loopback server, the one reader the server lets register without sign-in): `…; the server's operator lists it there, or a first user arms sign-in (npm run users -- add <login>)` — on the loopback a server without sign-in runs `OBSERVOGRAM_AUTH=off`, where adding a user arms nothing. It now names the operator's list alone (**a declared default change**): `<origin> cannot be registered on a server without sign-in — only a loopback MCP or an origin listed in OBSERVOGRAM_MCP_ORIGINS; the server's operator lists it there`, a way out that holds in every posture and that the server's own `400` to the same URL names beside its restart; `GET /api/mcp-endpoints` names no sign-in way to a reader it lets register, so the studio names none. Two server sentences changed (**a declared default change**), where each was itself short of true. ### Follow-up `malformed-json-app-wide`: a body the parsers refuse is answered as JSON, quoting none of it
- **A malformed JSON body is answered `400 { ok: false, error: 'the request body is not valid JSON' }` on every route** (**a declared default change**: it was Express's HTML error page, which quoted a fragment of the body — a `POST /api/mcp/ping` `mcpAuth`, a password — and printed the error's stack, fragment included, on stderr). `server/index.mjs`'s `payloadTooLarge` error handler becomes `bodyParserError` (`server/route-table.mjs` `MIDDLEWARE` follows): every error the app-wide parsers raise is answered on every path as JSON with a fixed text and the parser's own status — 400 not JSON, 413 too large (`/api/*` keeps its crawler text; elsewhere `the request body is too large`, which was the HTML page), 415 an unsupported charset or content encoding (was the HTML page) — with `Cache-Control: no-store`, no fragment of the body, and nothing logged. The parsers still run after the auth gate and before every route's `authorize()`: an anonymous caller, or a session without the CSRF header, is refused as before; a caller `authorize()` would refuse gets the 400 first, as it got the page. `POST /api/mcp-settings/describe` and `/submit` keep their own parser and answers (batch 4, M6 (b)); the static bundle reads no body and is unchanged. `server/test-malformed-json.mjs` (new, 2): a marker in a malformed body to every mutating route, in the token and identity postures — JSON, never the marker, the refusal unchanged — plus oversize and charset, and the marker in neither child's log; `server/test-mcp-settings.mjs`'s check of another route's answer flips from the page to the JSON 400. Suite total 1357 → 1359 tests.

### Rebadge batch 4, D1–D4: in-page MCP server settings
- **`tools/lib/artefact-classify.mjs`** gains `compileBoundedPattern(pattern, { flags, noun, timed })` → `{ re }` or `{ reason }`: the taxonomy's pattern rule (a non-empty string of at most `PATTERN_MAX_LENGTH` characters, anchored with `^`, flags `""` or `"i"`, no quantified group, the four adversarial ids within the 50 ms budget) as one export, so another file carrying a user-written regex applies the same rule. `noun` names the pattern in each reason (default `pattern`); `timed: false` skips only the wall-clock run, every static check still runs. The taxonomy compiles through it and every one of its texts is unchanged. `tools/test-artefact-classify.mjs` +3. Suite total 1250 → 1253 tests at this entry's commit.
- **`tools/lib/mcp-url-safety.mjs`** gains `isLoopbackOrigin(origin)` — moved unchanged from `server/mcp-target-policy.mjs`, which re-exports the same function, so the server's allowlist and loopback rules do not move — and `mayBeThisMachine(hostname)`: every host `isLoopbackOrigin` accepts plus `*.localhost`, the unspecified addresses `0.0.0.0`, `[::]` and `[::ffff:0:0]`, and one trailing dot. The wider reading is for a check that refuses more when a host may be the reader's own machine (the browser's same-machine rule for server settings), never for an exemption: the server keeps the strict `isLoopbackOrigin`. `server/test-mcp-url.mjs` +2 cases.
- **`tools/lib/mcp-server-settings.mjs`** (new; listed — `VENDOR-MANIFEST.json` 56 → 57 modules; pure, browser-safe, imports `artefact-classify.mjs` and `mcp-url-safety.mjs` only): the MCP server-settings contract, one module for the studio, the studio server and an MCP server author who vendors it to check their own description. **The descriptor, version 1** (`SETTINGS_DESCRIPTOR_VERSION`), served at `<MCP server root>/admin/schema` (`SETTINGS_DESCRIPTOR_PATH`): `{ version, endpoint, auth?: { field, scheme: 'bearer' }, fields: [{ name, label, type, required?, help?, placeholder? }] (1–24), actions?: [{ name, label, endpoint?, fields?, confirm? }] (0–4) }` within `SETTINGS_LIMITS` (16 KiB, label 80, help 240, placeholder 120, confirm 160, …); `FIELD_TYPES` `text`, `url`, `secret`, `boolean`, any other type read as text; unknown keys ignored and a `value` key never read. `parseSettingsDescriptor(text, { mcpUrl, contentType })` → `{ descriptor }` (normalised, frozen), `{ notDescriptor }` (a JSON-RPC answer, an object without `version` and `fields`, a type that is not JSON — the generic form's cue) or `{ reason }` naming the broken rule (`this server describes its settings in version 2; this studio reads version 1 — update the studio, or use the generic form`, `fields[2].name "__proto__" is reserved`, `duplicate field name "user"`, a control character, `auth.field` not a secret, an action naming an unknown field). **The root and the path rule**: `settingsRoot(mcpUrl)` drops the last path segment (one trailing slash ignored), the query, the fragment and any userinfo (`…:9000/mcp` and `…:9000/mcp/` → `…:9000/`, `https://gw/team-a/mcp` → `https://gw/team-a/`); `resolveSettingsPath(path, mcpUrl, { noun, fix })` → `{ url }` or `{ reason }` takes only `^/?[A-Za-z0-9._~-]+(/[A-Za-z0-9._~-]+)*$` within 128 characters with no segment `.` or starting `..` — no `%` escape, `;`, `:`, `@`, `\`, `?`, `#`, white space or empty segment —, a leading `/` relative to the root, then re-checks the result (same origin, under the root, no userinfo, search or hash, `mcpUrlPolicy` clean). `normaliseUrlValue(value)` → `{ href }` (http(s), scheme and host lower-cased) or `{ reason }`, refusing userinfo (`the backend URL carries a user or password before "@" — put them in their own fields, so they are treated as secrets`). **The generic form**: `genericDescriptor({ names, path, auth })` — `GENERIC_NAMES` `url`, `user`, `secret`, `apiKey` (any subset renamed), `/configure`, every field in the body unless `auth: 'bearer'`. **The request**: `settingsRequest(descriptor, values, { action })` → `{ path, body, payload, headers, sent, carries, secretValues, secretNames, user }` or `{ reason, missing? }` — a null-prototype body, a url normalised, a secret exactly as typed, an empty optional omitted, a missing required field, a bad url or a body over 64 KiB refused; the `auth` field as `Authorization: Bearer`; an action sends `{ action }` plus its declared `fields`, else the `auth` field, else every non-empty secret, and lists the candidates in `carries`. **The outcome**: `outcomeOf({ status, contentType, text, type, truncated }, { secretValues, secretNames, user })` → `{ tone, headline, success, ok, message, checks, shape, raw, json, redacted, capped }` — `The server reports the settings verified (HTTP <s>).` only on `ok: true`, `accepted … It reported no verification.`, `answered HTTP <s> but reports a failure.`, `refused the settings: HTTP <s>.`, the redirect line; check statuses as words (`pass`, `fail`, `skip`, else `unknown`); message ≤ 500, 24 checks, the body verbatim after redaction, 8 KiB shown of 64 KiB read. **Redaction**: `redactEchoes(text, secretValues, { user })` hides every submitted secret of 4+ characters raw, URI-encoded, JSON-escaped (with `\uXXXX` and `\/` variants), base64 and as base64 `<user>:<secret>`, longest first; `outcomeOf` runs it on the raw text, then on every parsed string and key, then `redactSecretKeys(json, secretNames)` — a submitted secret field's name or `/^(pass(word)?|secret|token|api[-_]?key|credential|authorization)$/i` becomes `"<redacted>"` whatever its length. **The settings policy**: `compileSettingsPolicy(json, { timed })` → `{ policy, errors }`, strict (an unknown key anywhere, a version other than 1, 0 or more than 32 rules, a rule on a secret); a pattern passes `compileBoundedPattern` and then the settings bound — at most one unbounded quantifier (`^https://.*.*.*\.internal$` is refused); these are checks of shape, and no load-time probe times a pattern against URL-shaped values (a probe cannot prove a pattern fast on every value — see the review fixes), so a caller that must not block runs `policyFindings` under a deadline; `timed: false` skips only `compileBoundedPattern`'s id clocks; the optional `generic` block (`path`, `names`, `auth`) is validated as the generic form. `policyFindings(policy, descriptor, values)` → `[{ rule, field, warn, ack, unevaluated, note }]`: a non-empty value only, a url as its normalised href, a value over 512 characters itself a finding, a rule whose field the form lacks (or holds as a secret) unevaluated with its ack still required. Nothing calls it yet: inert. `tools/test-mcp-server-settings.mjs` (46 tests at this entry's commit, 48 at the head of the branch — the review fixes' two timing-run cases, counted in their own entry; new, in `npm test`: the descriptor, the root table, the path table against a loopback and a path-prefixed https root, the request, the outcome and every redaction form, the policy and its findings) and `tools/test-vendor-manifest.mjs` (`EXPECTED_MODULES` +1). Suite total 1253 → 1299 tests at this entry's commit.
- **`OBSERVOGRAM_MCP_SETTINGS_POLICY=<path.json>`** (new; `TOMOGRAPH_MCP_SETTINGS_POLICY` honoured, the modern name in every message) — `server/mcp-settings-policy.mjs` (new, the taxonomy loader's twin) reads the settings-policy file once in `start()`, right after the taxonomy and before the store: one leading byte-order mark is stripped (Windows PowerShell 5 writes one), the JSON is parsed and compiled by `compileSettingsPolicy` (strict, every pattern's shape checked), and an unreadable or invalid file **refuses the start** with `OBSERVOGRAM_MCP_SETTINGS_POLICY: <path>: <the ENOENT text | invalid JSON: … | the first schema error (+N more)>`, nothing written. A loaded file is logged once, `[studio] MCP settings policy: <path> (<n> rules)`, never on a silent boot. **`GET /api/mcp-settings`** (new, `viewer`, `no-store`, deployment-global; `server/routes/mcp-settings.mjs`) answers `{ ok, proxy, policy, configured }` — the document as the file holds it or `null`, never its path, and `proxy: true` only while `OBSERVOGRAM_MCP_ADMIN_PROXY=1` (read per request; the pass-through itself lands later in this batch). Nothing calls it at boot; the static bundle's shim answers `{ ok: true, proxy: false, policy: null, configured: false }`. Unset, nothing is read and no answer changes. `server/fixtures/serve-child.mjs` STRIP gains `MCP_SETTINGS_POLICY` and `MCP_ADMIN_PROXY`; `.env.example` and the README API Surface gain their rows; `server/route-table.mjs`, `server/test-authz.mjs` (`EXPECTED_CLASS`, `PROBES`, a case probe) and `server/test-tenancy.mjs` (deployment-global) the route; `tools/test-studio-bundle.mjs` (the shim's answer equals an unconfigured server's). `server/test-mcp-settings.mjs` (new, in `npm test`, 8 tests: the reader against plain env objects — unset, both spellings, the modern name winning —, each refusal's exact text, a BOM-led file, the unconfigured and configured answers, the one log line, the refused starts before the store, the switch's values, and the STRIP proof); `tools/fixtures/mcp-settings/policy.json` is the SPEC's example. Suite total 1299 → 1307 tests at this entry's commit.
- **`tools/lib/mcp-url-safety.mjs`** gains `parseOriginList(value)` → `{ any, origins: Set, rejected: [entry] }` — moved unchanged from `server/mcp-target-policy.mjs`, which re-exports the same function, so the server's `OBSERVOGRAM_MCP_ORIGINS` rule does not move; the static-bundle builder reads `--mcp-origins` with it (below). `server/test-mcp-url.mjs` pins the re-export.
- **`tools/build-studio-bundle.mjs`** — `--mcp-settings-policy <file.json>` and `--mcp-origins <origin>,…` (each at most once; twice or without a value exits 2 with the usage). The policy file is read with one leading BOM stripped, parsed and compiled with `compileSettingsPolicy` as `server/mcp-settings-policy.mjs` does, the refusals the server's texts behind the origin (`--mcp-settings-policy: <abs>: ENOENT …`, `…: invalid JSON: …`, `…: <first error> (+N more)`), and the document is appended to the config as `mcpSettingsPolicy`. The origin list is read by the server's rule (`parseOriginList`; `*` any origin), normalised and sorted, and appended as `mcpOrigins: { listed: true, origins: [origin] | null }` (null for `*`) — the shape `GET /api/mcp-endpoints` gives a server's studio; an entry that is not an http(s) origin, or a list with none, **fails the build** naming it (a server ignores such an entry; a bundle is distributed, so a silently shortened list would show only as a refusal in a reader's browser). Both keys come after `taxonomy`, only when baked — an unbaked config is byte-identical. When a flag is absent `OBSERVOGRAM_MCP_SETTINGS_POLICY` and `OBSERVOGRAM_MCP_ORIGINS` are honoured (legacy `TOMOGRAPH_` too; the per-org lists have no meaning in a bundle). The summary line gains ` · MCP settings policy: N rules` / ` · MCP origins: <origins | any (*)>` only when baked; `--json` gains `mcpSettingsPolicy: { source, file, rules } | null` and `mcpOrigins: { source, origins } | null`, always present — never the policy's contents. New exports `resolveSettingsPolicySource`, `loadSettingsPolicyFile`, `resolveMcpOrigins`, `checkBakedOrigins`; `buildStudioBundle({ …, mcpSettingsPolicy, mcpOrigins })` guards and returns them. **The shim** (`studio/static-backend.mjs`) serves the baked policy at `GET /api/mcp-settings` (`{ ok, proxy: false, policy, configured }`, the server's answer for the same file) and adds `mcpOrigins` only when baked. README ("Serve The Studio Without The Server") and DOWNSTREAM §9 (W6 row) and §10 document both flags. `tools/test-studio-bundle.mjs`: T3 (the `--json` keys, twice, no value), T3b (the four seams), T4b (the unbaked identity with the new keys null, the shim's unconfigured answer, a parent env with `OBSERVOGRAM_MCP_SETTINGS_POLICY` and `OBSERVOGRAM_MCP_ORIGINS` set bakes nothing, `main`'s env bakes both) and T8d (new: the shim equals a server started with the file; the list rule, `*`, the env fallback and the flag winning; the refusals; the config key order; the summary and `--json`; no operator path or rule text in any output). Suite total 1307 → 1308 tests at this entry's commit.
- **The MCP panel's Server settings** (**a declared default change**: the panel shows a **Server settings…** button, `#mcp-settings-btn`, which asks nothing until it is clicked) — `studio/mcp-settings-model.mjs` (new, pure), `studio/mcp-settings-api.mjs` (new, the loaders) and `studio/mcp-settings-view.mjs` (new, the renderer; every node by `createElement`, every string by `textContent`), the `.mss-*` zone in `app.css` under its own `==== MCP server settings` marker. The button opens a modal in the page (a scrim, `role="dialog"` `aria-modal="true"`, Esc, the scrim and `esc` close it and only it, the Tab trap, the focus returned) for a reader who may change an MCP endpoint — `GET /api/mcp-endpoints` `policy.register.allowed`: a session admin or owner, or the open posture's caller on a direct loopback request; anyone else sees it `aria-disabled` with the reason, as do the token posture, a policy that could not be read, a panel with no target and a static bundle served off this machine with no baked MCP origin list. Opening it reads `GET /api/mcp-settings`, checks the panel's target in the browser — `mcpUrlPolicy`, never the studio's own origin, a loopback MCP only from a loopback page, https unless loopback, a loopback or listed origin (`policy.typed.origins`; in the bundle the baked `--mcp-origins`) — and reads the server's settings description from `<MCP server root>/admin/schema` **from the browser**, with no header and no credential, no redirect followed, 10 s, 16 KiB. A description is rendered as its form; a 404/405/501/401/403 or an answer that is not a description gives the generic form, named, with its path, field names and API-key placement editable under "What the server expects" (prefilled from the policy's `generic` block, never kept); a description that breaks the contract is refused by name, with Try again and Use the generic form; a page origin the server's CORS does not name reads "unreachable", naming that origin. **Send to the server** and each declared action POST from the browser straight to the MCP server (`credentials: 'omit'`, `redirect: 'manual'`, no `authHeaders()`: no cookie, no CSRF header, no `X-Observogram-Org` go to another origin), the descriptor's `auth` field as `Authorization: Bearer`; every secret input is emptied as soon as the request is sent and every input on close; inputs carry `data-field`, never `name`, in no `<form>`, secrets `autocomplete="new-password"` with the password managers' ignore attributes. The answer is shown as the server returned it — the verdict only from `ok: true`, the checks as words, the body verbatim after the echoed secrets are hidden (`n value(s) the server echoed back were hidden`); a request sent whose answer cannot be read is "unknown", never "nothing happened", with **Test the connection**. After a verified configure the panel's connection test runs (`pingFromPanel` now returns what it drew; `openMcpTarget` returns its read) and the modal says what it showed from the read's outcome (`connected, and the read <tool> answered: …` / `connected, but the read failed: …`), then offers **Open the live panel** on the same target. A settings policy that cannot be read (or compiled in the browser) holds the send: `Could not read the settings policy, so its checks cannot run — close and reopen to try again.` The proxy is not used by this modal yet, and no sentence names it. Nothing modal-related enters `state` or storage.

### Rebadge batch 3, C0: a caller-supplied MCP URL is a privilege
- **`tools/lib/mcp-client.mjs`** (behaviour, no export changed — **a declared default change**, D10): the client never follows a redirect. Every request is sent with `redirect: 'manual'` (a transport hook's `fetchImpl` receives it as advice, like `signal`), and an MCP answering 3xx — or a browser's `opaqueredirect` — is refused with `MCP HTTP <status> on <method>: the MCP answered with a redirect to <origin> — Observogram does not follow redirects; register (or type) the URL it points at`, naming only the Location's origin, never its path or query. This holds for every caller — the server's routes, `npm run fetch-live`, the scheduled refresh workflow and journeys: an MCP behind a redirect (http → https, a trailing slash) is configured with the final URL. `tools/test-mcp-transport.mjs` +3.
- **`tools/lib/mcp-client.mjs`** (behaviour, no export changed — **a declared default change**): every MCP answer text is redacted by value, native `fetch` included — a non-OK body, a JSON-RPC or SSE error message, a tool's `isError` text and a fetcher's rejection have the bearer, the URL's userinfo and every credential-named query parameter value replaced by `<redacted>`, as a hook's `fetchImpl` answers already were; an upstream that repeats the Authorization header (a 401 page) no longer carries the token into a 502 body, a log line or a `mcp.probeErrors.*` annotation, and the CLI's error lines are redacted too. A body that is not JSON is reported as `MCP <method>: the answer is not valid JSON` (the parser's message quoted a cut of it). The old pin (`upstream says Bearer tok`) is flipped to `upstream says Bearer <redacted>`. A successful answer is redacted the same way — every string of the result `rpc` and `callTool` return (keys included, over JSON and SSE), so an MCP that repeats the request in a result (a version string, a description) cannot carry a server-held read token into a ping's `read.detail`, a snapshot, draft or refresh pack, or a recorded fixture; a result holding none comes back as it was. From a successful result the credential itself — the bearer (a server-held read token or the caller's `mcpAuth`) and the URL's userinfo — is redacted at any length, so a 7-character read token the MCP repeats never reaches an operator's ping; a value merely taken from a credential-named query parameter is redacted from it only from 12 characters, and a tool's JSON text is parsed before it is redacted: a short credential-named value (`sortkey=title`, `partitionkey=1`) is ordinary data, and replacing it renamed keys, cut ids and left a tool's JSON unparsed in packs, fixtures and the deploy's rollback snapshot. `tools/test-mcp-transport.mjs` +4 (an echoing MCP over native fetch: a 401 body, a JSON-RPC error and an SSE error frame, a tool's `isError` text, a body that is not JSON) and +6 (a tool's JSON and plain-text answers and a `tools/list` description, over JSON and SSE), `server/test-mcp-ping.mjs` +1 (an MCP repeating the endpoint's read token in its version: `read.detail` says `version Bearer <redacted>`): 1246 → 1247 tests.
- **The MCP routes redact the resolved credential** (`server/mcp-target-policy.mjs`, new: `redactTarget(text, target)`): draft-from-mcp and refresh-live, deploy, deploy-bulk, rollback and a journey run's live Pack B run every 502 body, deploy-record item error and stderr line through the target `resolveMcpTarget` answered — the token it resolved (a server-held variable's value or the caller's `mcpAuth`), the URL's userinfo and credential parameter values, then `redactCredentials` — a backstop behind the client's own redaction. `server/test-mcp-target-policy.mjs` (new, 5), `server/test-services-api.mjs` +1 (an MCP echoing the Authorization header: the draft, refresh and deploy 502 bodies, stderr and the rows hold `<redacted>`), `server/test-transport-hook.mjs` (a hook fault naming its own credentialed upstream is masked in the refresh-live, draft-from-mcp, deploy, deploy-bulk, rollback and journey-run 502s).
- **The MCP origin allowlist** (`server/mcp-target-policy.mjs` gains `mcpOriginList`, `mcpOriginDecision`, `parseOriginList`, `isLoopbackOrigin`, `originOf`, `credentialThatRides`; **a declared default change**, D2 (c)): every MCP target — a typed `mcpUrl`, a registered endpoint at each use (draft-from-mcp, refresh-live, deploy, deploy-bulk, rollback, through `resolveMcpTarget`) and an endpoint at registration (`POST /api/mcp-endpoints`, a `PATCH` of `url` or `readTokenEnv`, judged on the record after the change) — meets an origin rule whatever the caller's role. `OBSERVOGRAM_MCP_ORIGINS` (every org) ∪ `OBSERVOGRAM_ORG_<KEY>_MCP_ORIGINS` (one org, read only by the org owning the name): comma-separated origins, normalised by the URL parser; an entry with a path, query, fragment, credentials or `*` in its host is named once on stderr and ignored; a list without an accepted entry allows nothing; a lone `*` allows every origin. Loopback always passes. With a list set, every other origin must be in it. **With none set, no credential leaves for an unlisted origin but loopback** — an endpoint's server-held token, the caller's `mcpAuth`, a credential in a typed URL, or a loaded transport hook (it may attach its own); a typed URL without one reaches only an origin of the org's own endpoints; a token-less endpoint registers and fetches anywhere. Refusals: 403 `{ denied: 'origin' }` at a fetch, 400 at registration (the record and its row are not written), naming the origin only. **Migration:** an install that sends a credential to a remote MCP (deploys, authenticated reads, a `readTokenEnv` endpoint, a transport hook) sets `OBSERVOGRAM_MCP_ORIGINS` before upgrading. `server/fixtures/serve-child.mjs` STRIP gains `MCP_ORIGINS`. `server/test-mcp-target-policy.mjs` +5, `server/test-service-admin.mjs` +2, `server/test-services-api.mjs` +1, `server/test-tenancy.mjs` (each org's list is its own), `server/test-smoke.mjs` (mcpAuth by id to an unlisted origin → 403 before any wire call; an endpoint registered under `*` refused once the list changes).
- **A typed MCP URL needs the admin role** (`server/mcp-target-policy.mjs` gains `TYPED_MCP_URL_ROLE = 'admin'` — the one constant —, `typedMcpUrlDecision`, `mcpCallerOf`, `mcpTargetView`; **a declared default change**, R4): `POST /api/draft-from-mcp` and `POST /api/refresh-live` refuse a typed `mcpUrl` from an operator session, the bearer token (judged by rank, so flipping the constant to `operator` admits it) and the open postures' anonymous `local` caller (by kind, even on loopback): 403 `role` or `posture`, each sentence naming the way that works for its reader in its posture — a registered endpoint (`mcpEndpointId`), an admin registering one in Settings → MCP endpoints, and in the token posture arming sign-in (`npm run users -- add <login>`). A typed URL with `user:password` is a 400 (`send the token as mcpAuth`). `resolveMcpTarget(db, body, { forWrite, caller, typedRule })` takes the caller (none throws); the deploy, deploy-bulk and rollback routes passed `typedRule: 'any'` here, until the next entry. `GET /api/mcp-endpoints` gains `policy`: `{ typed, register }`, each `{ allowed, why, listed, origins }` — the reader's own org's origin list only. **Migration:** a script below admin that sends `mcpUrl` to the draft or the refresh registers the endpoint (an admin) and sends `mcpEndpointId`. The credential-hygiene, scheme and `OBSERVOGRAM_ALLOW_LOCAL_MCP=0` pins move from `server/test-smoke.mjs` (whose caller may no longer type) to `server/test-services-api.mjs` as the admin `ada`; smoke pins the refusal (nothing sent, no live pack, no row). `server/test-mcp-target-policy.mjs` +4, `server/test-service-admin.mjs` +1, `server/test-services-api.mjs` +1, `server/test-authz.mjs` +1; the GET's `policy` per principal in services-api, authz (open postures) and tenancy (never another org's list); `server/test-transport-hook.mjs` pins the bearer's refusal in the token posture.
- **The deploy routes take a typed MCP URL from an admin only** (**a declared default change**, D3): `POST /api/packs/:id/deploy/:target`, `POST /api/packs/:id/deploy-bulk` and `POST /api/deploys/:deployId/rollback` — which send the caller's write token to the target — follow the same rule as the draft and the refresh: a typed `mcpUrl` from an operator session, the bearer or a caller without sign-in is 403 `role` / `posture`, before anything is sent or recorded; `resolveMcpTarget`'s `typedRule` option is gone (every call site applies the rule). The single deploy's answer names the safe URL in `mcpUrl` (a credential parameter dropped), as its record already did. **Migration:** an operator's CI deploy (the bearer is an operator) registers its target once (an admin, Settings → MCP endpoints) and sends `mcpEndpointId`; the write token stays the request's `mcpAuth`. The deploy scheme pins move from `server/test-smoke.mjs` (403 `posture` there now) to `server/test-services-api.mjs` as `ada`. `server/test-services-api.mjs` +1, `server/test-auth-oidc.mjs` (an OIDC operator typing a URL at deploy → 403, no record).
- **Without sign-in only a loopback MCP or a listed origin may be registered** (**a declared default change**, D4): `POST /api/mcp-endpoints` and a `PATCH` of `url` or `readTokenEnv` from the open postures' anonymous `local` caller register a loopback MCP or an origin in `OBSERVOGRAM_MCP_ORIGINS` (or the org's list) only, with or without `readTokenEnv` — any other is 400 `on a server without sign-in, only a loopback MCP or an origin listed in OBSERVOGRAM_MCP_ORIGINS may be registered — list <origin> there, or sign in as an admin (npm run users -- add <login> arms sign-in)` (under `OBSERVOGRAM_AUTH=off`, the restart that arms it), the record and its row not written: registering first is no way around the typed-URL rule. A signed-in admin still registers a token-less endpoint anywhere when no list is set. `createMcpEndpointFromApi` / `updateMcpEndpointFromApi` take `{ caller }` (none throws). `GET /api/mcp-endpoints` `policy.register` gains `listedOnly`. **Migration:** a quick start against a remote demo MCP sets `OBSERVOGRAM_MCP_ORIGINS=<its origin>`, or adds a first user; a loopback MCP is unchanged. `server/test-mcp-target-policy.mjs` +1, `server/test-service-admin.mjs` +1, `server/test-smoke.mjs` (local registering an unlisted remote origin → 400, no row).

### Rebadge batch 3, C2: test the connection, then fetch
- **`tools/lib/contracts/mcp-capabilities.mjs`** gains `capabilityInventory(toolNames)` → `{ capabilities: { <capabilityId>: [advertised tool names] }, unmatched: <count> }`: an MCP's advertised tools mapped to the capabilities Observogram reads, in candidate order, a name advertised twice counted once, and every tool no capability calls only counted, never listed. The tool surface is unchanged. `tools/test-contract-guard.mjs` +2.
- **`pingMcp`** (`tools/fetch-live-pack.mjs`, with `PING_READS`, `PING_TIMEOUT_MS` = 5 s per request, `PING_DEADLINE_MS` = 10 s for the whole ping and `TOOLS_LIST_MAX_PAGES` = 10): `initialize` and `notifications/initialized`, the whole `tools/list` (its `nextCursor` followed up to ten pages; a cursor left over reads `more`) and **one** cheap read — the dashboards search asked for one item, else the Grafana health read (marked `credentialFree`: it answers without the MCP's backend credentials), else `system_health` — and nothing else. The verdict is read from where a failure surfaced: HTTP 401/403 on `initialize`, `tools/list` or `tools/call` → `auth-refused`; no answer or a 5xx → `unreachable`; a request's timeout or the deadline → `timeout` (the deadline aborts the request still in flight, through the client's `signal`); any other status (404), a body that is not a JSON-RPC result, a JSON-RPC error or a redirect → `not-mcp`; otherwise `connected`, with a read whose tool answered an `isError` text naming 401/403/unauthorized/forbidden kept as `backendAuthRefused` (the MCP's own credentials to its backend).
