- **Fixed on the way.** Bulk deploy sent selected rows a type filter hid; it now deploys exactly what the review shows.
- **`GET /api/packs/:id/conformance` carries `onPlaceholder`** for a pack with `library.todo.*` annotations (from `validationSummary`, for the requested environment), as `/api/validate` and `/api/library/register` already did — Conformance lists the clauses that pass only on a placeholder across reloads instead of hedging; the key is omitted, never `[]`, when the list is not known.
- **Recording-rule inference names both rules.** A good/total SLI inferred by the crawler (`tools/lib/sli-inference.mjs`) now reads *Inferred from recording rules `<svc>:<metric>:good_5m` and `<svc>:<metric>:total_5m`.* instead of the `…:good/total` shorthand; Discover links both rules and still resolves the shorthand in packs crawled before. No golden changed (none holds a good/total pair).
- **Removed** the Remediate compiler's unreachable inline deploy panel and Discover's never-produced *Missing* artefact paths (what a pack lacks is a failing rubric clause, which Conformance and Assessment report).
- **Tests.** `tools/test-ux-kit.mjs`, `tools/test-discover-rows.mjs`, and additions to the build, neuron, traceability, library and verify-deploy suites.

### The Build journey's step copy
The maintainer's copy for the three steps of building a pack. The header tabs (`BUILD_TABS` in `studio/build-model.mjs`) and each step's heading now read:

| Step | Question | Supporting text (the tab's tagline) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Define | What are we building for? | Choose a service, tier, and starting point |
| Compile | What will the pack include? | Build SLIs, alerts, dashboards, and checks |
| Verify | Can we use this pack? | Review coverage and resolve gaps |

Before, they read *What are we observing? · Service, tier & library*, *What should we watch? · Pack & deployable artifacts* and *Is it ready to use? · Conformance & placeholders*. The tab labels are in title case with articles and short prepositions lowercase, as the previous Build labels were (*Is It Ready to Use?*): *What Will the Pack Include?*. A tab's accessible name and title follow the tagline (*Define — Choose a service, tier, and starting point*). The README and `docs/BUILD_JOURNEY.md` follow. Only copy changed; the ledes under the headings are unchanged.

### The store, slice 2b: the offline operations — export, import --replace, rekey-issuer, purge-org
STORE_PLAN slice 2, second of two PRs (after 2a, below). It gives the store build a clean rollback and a way back: `packc store export`, `packc store import --replace`, `packc store rekey-issuer` and `packc store purge-org` (`server/store/ops.mjs`; the replace itself is boot step 3, `planReplace` / `applyReplace` in `server/store/import.mjs`). Every one runs with the server stopped — `assertNotInUse`, restore's probe, now shared, refuses while anything holds the database — except a directory export, which only reads. Each prints `store: <path>` first, refuses `:memory:` and a missing database (none creates one), and prints one line and exits 1 when refused. Slice 2 is complete once 2a and 2b merge; `develop` is held from promotion until then.
- **Roll back only after `packc store export`.** `packc store export <dir>` writes the `users.json` / `orgs.json` a pre-store build boots on: the enabled local users with their password records (only once stand-alone sign-in is armed; OIDC users never), and an `orgs.json` when the deployment had one, has more than one live org, or keeps the default org off the workspace root — live orgs, enabled members (OIDC ones by `sub`, under the recorded issuer), `operator` written as `member`. The report lists every viewer and operator (a pre-store build enforces no roles: they regain full write), each org an owner is no member of (a pre-store build has no owners: the owner loses it) and the cookie note (users revoked in the store stay signed in on a pre-store build until their cookies expire; rotating `OBSERVOGRAM_SESSION_SECRET` signs everyone out). **In place** (`<dir>` = the workspace, a store the server has started) it writes to the recorded users file and the workspace, records what it wrote (`legacy_hashes`, the marker `by 'export'`, one `store.export` row), so a store build starts on those files without refusing; a `users.json` / `orgs.json` it would overwrite that differs from those hashes (a pre-store build's edits, as on a second rollback) is refused naming `import --replace`, never overwritten. When it writes `orgs.json` while the default org is at the workspace root it makes the pre-store build's move itself — every check first (an existing `orgs/default/<entry>` refuses, naming each), then the renames, the default org's journey `file:` paths rewritten, the files, and the root `orgs/default` (`org.root`) in one `tx()`; it prints the CronJob change, and a failure part-way puts everything back and says so. An in-place export proceeds only into a workspace that is provably this store's: its marker names this store, or there is no marker, this store's database lives inside the workspace and no `*.db` directly in the workspace or its `db/` (each opened read-only, never migrated) holds another store (with the database outside the workspace, as in k8s, a missing marker refuses: one start on the workspace rewrites it; a workspace that does not exist refuses too) — another store's marker or database is refused naming both stores and the command that exports that workspace's own store, an unreadable `*.db` is refused naming it, and a corrupt marker is refused naming it with the way out (with the server stopped, move it aside, then export again). A directory export writes only into a directory that does not exist or is empty (a symlink is followed; what it names must be absent or empty): anything else is refused, naming the two ways out — an empty or new directory, or, for this store's own workspace, `OBSERVOGRAM_WORKSPACE=<it> packc store export <it>` with the server stopped; a directory inside the workspace or holding it is refused naming the in-place export. A replace that cannot remove an empty leftover after its commit warns once, naming it, and the start goes on.
- **Re-upgrade with `packc store import --replace`.** After a pre-store build changed users or orgs, the store build refuses to start (step 2 (d)); the refusal now also names `packc store import --replace`. The command records a request (`replace_requested`, only for the store the marker names, already imported; `packc store import` alone is a usage error), and the next start re-imports the files as they stand with the unit's own environment and prints a `[store] replaced from …` report: a local user absent from the users file is disabled, one in it updated and re-enabled, a new one created; an OIDC user is never disabled for being absent; the owner flag is never re-derived; orgs are created, renamed and removed (never the default) to match `orgs.json`, memberships follow the file exactly (a user disabled before and after keeps theirs, since the export leaves disabled users out of `orgs.json`), a removed org that reappears is skipped as a conflict; a file that is absent keeps what the store has. Every changed or disabled user's epoch is bumped (their cookies end). After a pre-store `npm run orgs -- create` and restart on a flat deployment, the replace moves the default org's root to `orgs/default` with that build's migration (journey paths rewritten, empty pre-store leftovers removed, the CronJob line printed); its memberships follow the file, whose `default` entry has none, so only owners reach it until members are added. It refuses, moving nothing and leaving the request pending, when a flat entry with data sits beside its `orgs/default/` twin, a rewritten journey would not parse, or no enabled owner who can sign in under the unit's mode would be left (and the store had one). A request made for another store, or with the marker missing, refuses at step 2 (c) with its ways out.
- **`packc store rekey-issuer --to <issuer> | --clear`.** The step-2 (b) refusal of a changed OIDC issuer now names both. `--to` (the same IdP at a new URL) rewrites every OIDC login to the new key, `oidc_issuer` and an `oidc:` `identity_mode` with it, refused when a rewritten login exists: users keep rows, roles, memberships and the owner flag; store sessions signed in under the old key end, and a fresh sign-in finds the same row. `--clear` (another IdP) disables every enabled OIDC user with an epoch bump — the owner rows keep their flag, disabled — and clears the issuer and an `oidc:` `identity_mode`; set `OBSERVOGRAM_BOOTSTRAP_ADMIN` before the next start to name the owner. One `issuer.rekey` row each; earlier audit rows and `deploys.jsonl` keep the old logins; a pending replace stays pending and runs under the new key.
- **`packc store purge-org <id>`** deletes the files of an org removed with `npm run orgs -- remove` (which now prints the command): only a removed, non-default org whose root is `orgs/<id>`; a symlinked root, a root outside `<workspace>/orgs` and a workspace whose marker names another store are refused. It drops that root's `legacy_hashes` keys, writes one `org.purge` row and rewrites the marker (`by 'purge-org'`); the org row stays, so its id is never reused. It cannot be undone and asks for no confirmation: the org's packs, `deploys.jsonl`, `snapshots/`, `runs/` and `journeys/` go, and no `store backup` holds them, so the docs say to copy the directory first.
- **`packc store restore` warns** on stderr, exit 0, when the workspace's `.store-imported` names another store than the one restored (or cannot be read): the next start refuses until they agree.
- **Docs.** README: the Security Posture's refusal bullet, and new sections Upgrade And Roll Back (the upgrade, the clean rollback, the re-upgrade and the replace's rules), Stale Import (the step-2 refusals and their ways out), Move The OIDC Issuer, Purge A Removed Org; `deploy/k8s/README.md` (the export from a one-off pod with the studio at 0, the image change, forward again with `import --replace`, `rekey-issuer` for an IdP move); the build status in `docs/STORE_PLAN.md`; `docs/HANDOVER.md`.
- **Tests.** `server/test-store-ops.mjs` (the Export gate through `server/fixtures/pre-store-build.mjs`, a frozen copy of the pre-store semantics; the Stale-import round trips export → pre-store edits → refuse → replace; rekey-issuer; purge-org; the restore warning; the CLI), and `tools/test-store-prestore-live.mjs` (`npm run test:store:prestore[:strict]`; the CI job `store-prestore` boots the real `v0.4.0` build over HTTP on exported workspaces). The step-2 (b) and (d) assertions in `server/test-store-import.mjs` / `server/test-store-boot.mjs` follow their new texts.

### The store, slice 2a: identity on the store — the boot order, the import, revocable sessions, tenancy always on
STORE_PLAN slice 2, first of two PRs. Users, orgs, memberships and the owner move from `users.json` / `orgs.json` into the embedded store (`observogram.db`), which the server now opens at every start (`server/boot.mjs`). The offline operations — `packc store export`, `import --replace`, `rekey-issuer`, `purge-org` — are the second PR (2b, above); `develop` is held from promotion until 2b merges, because `export` is the rollback path. Behaviour changes:
- **The import.** The first start of a store build imports `users.json` and `orgs.json` once, prints a `[store]` report on the boot log (also stored as `import_report`) and leaves both files in place, hashed (`legacy_hashes`, the `.store-imported` marker) and never read again. Names and fields the store cannot hold are dropped and listed in the report — never a refused start. `orgs.json` roles are mapped: `admin` / `owner` → `admin`, `viewer` / `read` / `readonly` / `read-only` → `viewer`, anything else (`member`, empty) → `operator`; the members mapped to `viewer` lose write power once roles are enforced (Stage 3). Data under `orgs/default/` with no `default` entry in `orgs.json` (a retired default org, or a migration that stopped before its `orgs.json` write) is never made an org: the report and every boot name it as left behind, with the ways out — with the server stopped, move its entries into the default org's root, or move the directory aside.
- **Edited legacy files refuse the start.** A `users.json` or `orgs.json` changed after the import (a pre-store build during a rollback, config management) refuses the next start with the imported SHA-256 and the ways out: put the file back, or move it aside and make the change with `npm run users` / `npm run orgs`. A marker that names another store refuses too; a vanished marker or file is repaired and logged.
- **Revocable sessions.** Each user row carries a session epoch: a password change or a disable signs that user out everywhere. Cookies issued before the upgrade stay valid. A disabled row cannot sign in.
- **Tenancy is always on.** Every `/api` request runs in an org and the response echoes it in `X-Observogram-Org`; a flat workspace is the default org, at the workspace root, and a created org's root is `orgs/<id>`, fixed at creation. The fixed flat-workspace migration (a legacy `orgs.json` deployment) never moves an empty directory and refuses a corrupt `orgs.json` naming its path instead of replacing it.
- **The header ORG chip** shows for a user in more than one org, or whose only org is not the default one (`orgChipModel` in `studio/api.mjs`, both `studio/app.mjs` sites). A user whose only org is the default org — including an `orgs.json` deployment's default org — no longer sees the static label.
- **One org without identity.** A one-org `orgs.json` with only a bearer (`OBSERVOGRAM_API_TOKEN`) now boots token-only, and a one-org `orgs.json` with neither a bearer nor identity now boots like a fresh install — on loopback it seeds `admin`/`admin`. Both refused before; two or more orgs without identity still refuse.
- **OIDC.** Users are recorded as `<issuerKey>#<sub>` (the issuer canonicalised: a bare origin's slash, the well-known suffix and a fragment folded; a path's trailing slash is kept, so `…/v2.0` and `…/v2.0/` are different keys); an OIDC deployment without `orgs.json` records `oidc_join_role = operator`, so every IdP user keeps today's access — set `OBSERVOGRAM_OIDC_JOIN_ROLE=none` before the first start to start closed; it is read at the first start only. `OBSERVOGRAM_BOOTSTRAP_ADMIN` (`<issuer>#<sub>`, or an email that counts only with `email_verified: true`) names the first owner, or `npm run users -- owner <login>` later. A store whose OIDC users were recorded under another issuer key refuses the start and names the spelling to set back.
- **The CLIs write the store.** `npm run users` and `npm run orgs` are thin entry points over `server/identity-admin.mjs`: `--role member` is refused (the roles are `viewer`, `operator`, `admin`; `member` is now `operator`), `users -- remove` disables instead of deleting (the audit references the row) and the new `users -- enable` undoes it (a still-seeded `admin` / `admin` row needs `passwd` first, and even disabled it refuses a start beyond loopback, naming `users -- passwd <login>` as the way out), the first local user becomes the owner (not while the server runs OIDC, where it is created without owner and the CLI says why) and arms stand-alone sign-in, which stays armed once armed (removing users never reopens a server; only `OBSERVOGRAM_AUTH=off` does); `users -- remove` keeps the last owner who can sign in under the server's mode — the issuer a shell's `OBSERVOGRAM_OIDC_ISSUER` names, else the mode the server's last start recorded (`identity_mode`, rewritten by a start whose mode changed once it listens — a start that fails to bind records nothing; on a store no start of this build booted, its local owners and those under the issuer it records, and no A-16 owner while it records an issuer); and `orgs -- remove` is soft (its row and files stay).
- **Journeys.** Over the API a journey loads by name only, and a studio-run journey never reads another org's part of the workspace — a `crawl:` root, a `file:` pack or an inventory site there is refused. The schedule snippets are per org: the k8s form points `OBSERVOGRAM_WORKSPACE` at the org's root and labels the CronJob with the org; the component CronJob's comment says one CronJob per org (`/workspace/<orgs.root>`), pinned by `tools/test-deploy-manifests.mjs`.
- **Docs.** README Security Posture and store section, `.env.example` (`OBSERVOGRAM_USERS_FILE` is an import source only; `OBSERVOGRAM_BOOTSTRAP_ADMIN`, `OBSERVOGRAM_OIDC_JOIN_ROLE`; tenancy has no knob), `deploy/k8s/README.md` (the first start imports; the `store restore` recipe from a one-off pod with the studio at 0; the per-org CronJob), plan statuses in `docs/PRODUCTIZATION_PLAN.md`.
- **`:memory:` over flat data.** An `OBSERVOGRAM_DB=:memory:` start over an `orgs.json` and a not-yet-moved flat workspace moves nothing and plans the default org at `.` (an `orgs.json` `default` entry too), so it serves the flat data in place; the report line says so, and no "left behind … merge it by hand" warning names the base. Over a half-migrated workspace (`orgs/default` already holds data) it plans the default org at `orgs/default`, as a file store does, instead of refusing with check E; the unmoved flat entries are named as left behind until a file-store start finishes the move.
- **Tests.** `server/test-store-import.mjs` (readers, hashes, marker, migration, the Import gate, the seed and check tables, `bootStore()` in-process) and `server/test-store-boot.mjs` (the Boot order gate through `start()` in child processes, stale import, the CLIs, arming, removed orgs, per-boot warnings, OIDC boot switches, `:memory:`); test-auth-local, test-tenancy (with a sweep that fails on an unclassified `/api` route), test-auth-oidc, test-smoke and test-workspace ported; every suite that imports server code clears the store's boot variables first. Guards: only the boot, the migration, the store's import / CLI modules and the suites import `server/store/legacy-files.mjs`; no `tools/lib` module imports from `server/`.

### Spec 1.3: `good_when` — a threshold SLI's bound has a direction
The maintainer's request of 2026-09-23: the MQ lab could not say that fewer connected channels or running listeners is bad — a floor had to be encoded as a ratio SLI or as an inverted "headroom" ceiling — and the burn-rate generator could only warn that a ratio-shaped threshold "suggests a floor, which the spec cannot express". RFC-0002 (upstream MoebiusX/otel-observability-pack, accepted the same day, merged on develop as 98be4ae) adds one optional field to `threshold` and `distribution` SLIs, `good_when: below | above`, default `below` — today's meaning: a sample is good at or below the bound — with `above` a floor; the schema refuses the field on `ratio` and `custom` SLIs through a `not` sub-schema, the form this repo's validator enforces. Absent means below, so every 1.2 pack validates and means what it meant. Observogram adopts it end to end:
- **Vendor.** `vendor/observability-pack-spec/v1.3/` holds the four upstream files byte for byte (each sha256 is the git blob's), `VERSIONS.json` records spec 1.3, ref `develop`, commit `98be4ae8…`; `v1.2/` is removed (1.3 validates every 1.2 pack unchanged; git history keeps it). `tools/sync-spec.mjs` reads the version directory from the fetched spec's own header (`| Spec version | 1.3 |`), fetches bodies untrimmed and defaults to the upstream default branch, develop. The studio’s links into the upstream repo (the footer’s “spec v1.3”, the Schema view’s schema and spec, the Conformance view’s maturity rubric) open that commit, not `main` — upstream main still serves 1.2 — and `tools/test-validator.mjs` holds them to `VERSIONS.json` `upstream.commit`.
- **Version.** `SPEC_VERSION = '1.3'` in `tools/lib/validator.mjs`, with `SPEC_DIR` and `SPEC_SCHEMA_PATH` beside it; every path that spelled `v1.2` (journey, crawler, gen-site, upconvert, the server's example catalogue, /healthz, a dozen tests), the compile banners, the CLI help, the studio's header, footer and texts, `package.json`, CI and the docs that state the current version follow the constant. `tools/test-validator.mjs` (new) pins the constants against the manifest and the one directory on disk, the 1.3 schema's acceptance of `good_when` on threshold and distribution SLIs and its refusal on ratio and custom ones (`$.spec.slis[N].good_when: matches forbidden 'not' schema`) and of a bad value, every 1.2-shaped pack of the repo validating unchanged, and a drift scan that fails on any other spec version in code, studio, workflows or current-version docs.
- **One helper.** `tools/lib/good-when.mjs`: `goodWhen(sli)` (absent means below — the one place that says so), `badComparator`, `boundGlyph`, `boundText` (`≤ 0.5 seconds`, `≥ 2 consumers`); every reader asks it, never the raw field. The studio cannot import `tools/lib`, so `studio/sli-direction.mjs` re-spells it and a test holds the two together input for input.
- **Burn-rate generator.** A floor's legs count the samples UNDER the bound — `sum_over_time((max(series) < bool t)[w:step])` in the error-ratio record, both burn legs and the two-bad-samples floor — a ceiling keeps `> bool t`; the comparison stays strict either way, so the bound itself is good. "Non-negative upper bound" became "a finite number" (a floor at −1 is a bound); the floor guess ("unit ratio suggests a floor the spec cannot express") became "looks like a floor — declare good_when: above (or good_when: below to state the ceiling)" and is raised only while the SLI declares no direction. Distribution SLIs keep no error-ratio form. `tools/lib/sli-inference.mjs` reads `< bool` back as `good_when: above`. compile.mjs builds no leg itself.
- **Boards.** A threshold tile colours `okAbove` (higher is worse) for a ceiling, `okBelow` (amber under the bound, red under half of it) for a floor (`thresholdSteps`); the dashed line sits at the bound either way; the descriptions name the side: `Good when ≤ 0.5 seconds.`, `The dashed line is the threshold — good when ≥ 2 consumers.` The reference boards and the dashboard goldens moved by those description strings alone. A bound of 0 has no amber band: the two steps coincided and Grafana (last step ≤ the sample) painted the good 0 of a ceiling red under a description that says `Good when ≤ 0 messages` — a ceiling at 0 is now green through 0 and red from `Number.MIN_VALUE` (5e-324 in the JSON), a floor at 0 red under 0 and green from 0; the only 1.2 tile this moves is a 0-bound one (the library's `dlq_depth`).
- **Cards.** The adapter gives a threshold or distribution SLI a `subtitle` — its bound with its glyph — which Discover's layers view and the Build stack draw under the title (`.card-sub`); the Discover drawer prints `bound ≤ 0.5 seconds` and `good when below`.
- **Library engine and CLI.** A template may declare `good_when` (threshold only); the index rows carry it normalised; instantiation copies the declaration — the override's, else the template's — and never synthesises one (a pack that says nothing stays 1.2-shaped byte for byte); `good_when` joins `OVERRIDE_FIELDS` and `CUSTOM_FIELDS`, refused on a ratio SLI and for any value but the two sides; the retired `comparison` now says "the direction of a threshold is good_when …, spec 1.3 — use good_when"; `packc init --override <sli>.good_when=above`. `DEFAULT_BURN_PROFILE` is unchanged.
- **The editor.** The Bound row gains a two-segment *good when: below · above* control in the tier control's idiom (`role=radiogroup`, arrow keys, a sliding thumb, focus keys per segment, `↺ library default` when overridden), for threshold SLIs in edit and create modes, committing live like every field — picking the library's own side clears the override; the Bound hint now reads "the bound in the SLI's unit; good when below (a ceiling: latency, lag) or above (a floor: replicas, consumers)"; the read-only face prints the side; the rolodex card, the DEFINE candidate ghost and the stack card print `≤` / `≥`; the create dialog's type hint names both sides. The stylesheet's rules use the tokens only and sit in the block the AA scan reads.
- **Conformance.** `L1.MUST.latency_slo` counts threshold and distribution SLIs by type; a floor satisfies it, as the upstream maturity model says; no rubric change (`docs/CONFORMANCE.md`).
- **Compatibility, measured.** A 1.2 pack compiled to Prometheus rules and to its unified board at the base commit and at HEAD differs in the banner comment line (`compiled from ObservabilityPack v1.2` → `v1.3`) and in the tile / trend descriptions that gained the glyph, and in nothing else; the compiled rules of every 1.2 fixture are byte-identical below the banner. The MQ lab (mq-observability-pack) re-vendors the lib next and can then express its own floors directly.

### The editor — one pop-up over an SLI and its SLO; the stack's cards open it; the sheet is live on Define
The maintainer, trying the copies of PR #104 on a test server: *the functionality is not there, I can't update an SLI. This looks half baked … updating should be easier — one has to scroll up and down to modify something that, if it's just 5 variables, maybe a pop-up would do.* The in-card Customise face and the '+ Custom SLI' form card are gone; one design replaces them (`docs/BUILD_JOURNEY.md` "The editor"):
- **One pop-up editor** (`studio/build-editor-view.mjs` over the pure `sliEditorModel` in `studio/build-copies-model.mjs`; `buildEditorModel` assembles it): a centered modal dialog — `role=dialog aria-modal=true` (the Tab trap covers it and leaves the non-modal sheet underneath alone), labelled by the SLI id, a scrim over everything, Esc leaves the field and closes, focus on the first field on open and back on the opener on close, one at a time, UI state only (`build.editor`). A title row (the id large, the product and its evidence, the type pill, the *customised* / *custom* / *from the tier-1 profile* / *was <key>* chips); a two-column grid — Id · Description (prefilled with the library default), Objective · Window, Bound · Unit for a threshold SLI, Metric · Type (fixed: a different shape is a new custom SLI) — then the PromQL as two-row textareas that grow, showing the RESOLVED expression (the parameters in, read from the instantiated pack; never `${…}`) with the line *parameters: job=orders-api, duration_metric=… — edit them in L2*; the evidence line; a status line (*applying…* → *applied · SLO availability_99_9 · 2 burn alerts · rule …*, the engine's error — also under its field — *not in the pack*, *as compiled* on Verify); per-field *↺ library default*, *Reset all*, *Done*, the SLI's add / remove switch in the footer. No scrolling at 1920×1080 or 1366×768 for a library SLI. Create mode is the same dialog over the custom form (Name → id, Type, the fields per type, *Add to the pack* from `canSubmit`; a success morphs it into edit mode over the new SLI); on Verify the same dialog opens read-only (values, provenance, no input).
- **Live apply**: every field commits on input (`setOverride` / `updateCustom` with `live: true` — the debounced instantiate, a keystroke is never a request of its own); the editor lives in a persistent host outside the re-rendered view; the same editor already mounted is redrawn in place (the dialog node, the scrim and the status live region stay, so the *applied · …* answer is a text change assistive technology announces, and the entrance never replays) and its render keeps the focused control's text, focus and caret across the pack's answer — three quick characters lose none, a value the engine rejects stays as typed beside its message; a field is found by its key, else by its name when a rename changed the key under it (a custom SLI's `cu:<id>:` keys), the footer controls by their own keys (`editor:done` …), and Esc closes the editor from `<body>` too (one document listener per host). The id field is the exception — a rename moves the SLO, the rule, the boards and the burn alerts — so it is pre-checked on every keystroke (`checkEditorId`, `existingSliIds`: the message under the field, the status *not applied — …* or *rename to <id> — Enter, Tab or Esc applies it*) and committed when the field is left; an invalid id stays in the field with its message, never sent, and keeps it across the pack's answer.
- **Rename and metric** (`tools/lib/library.mjs`): `id` and `semconv_metric` join `OVERRIDE_FIELDS`. A rename follows through the SLO id, the recording rule, the boards' bindings, the burn alerts, the chaos / remediation references and the provenance (under the new id, `customised: id`), keeps the library evidence (the expression is still the library's) and stays keyed by the id the library gives the SLI (`entryFragment` carries `key` beside `id`), so a renamed SLI stays attached to its library row; `checkSliIds` refuses a rename onto another SLI in the pack, onto an un-ticked library SLI (it would clash the moment it is ticked) and a custom id equal to a renamed id; `checkSloIds` spells its collision on the override's key; a rename to the id the pack already carries is no rename. `semconv_metric` is one bounded token; restated beside an edited expression it stays, left alone an edited expression still drops the template's claim; a custom SLI may state one. The index rows carry the library's metric; `packc init --override <sli>.id=` / `.semconv_metric=`; the API's shape message names both. No library SLI carries a percentile, so there is no percentile field.
- **Where it opens from**: every L1 SLI / SLO card on the stack is a real control (a focusable `role=button`, Enter / Space, `aria-haspopup=dialog`, a hover and a focus ring; an SLO card lands on the objective; the candidate ghosts on Define too; `stackCardActions`, a Build-only wrapper around the card body Discover shares), a rolodex card's *Edit* (*View* on Verify), the *+ Custom SLI* card, a product chip on the seed card (the L1 sheet on that product's first card). The sheet is live on **Define** — its pack is instantiated already — so the preview mode and *Compose in Compile →* are retired (`sheetModeFor`: edit on Define and Compile, verify on Verify). The header step tabs' accessible names read *Define — Service, tier & library* (and Compile, Verify): `BUILD_TABS` moved to `build-model.mjs`, `tabName` is the tab's `aria-label` and `title` (it read *Library — …*).
- **Tests**: `tools/test-build-editor.mjs` (new, 13: the model over a library ratio SLI, a threshold one with a unit, an above-tier, a customised, a renamed and a custom one, create mode, read-only, the helpers; headless renders per mode; the handlers incl. three quick characters and the id clash; the focus-caret-text preservation; the create form's live draft; the Tab trap with the sheet underneath; the modal rules), `tools/test-build-model.mjs` 59 → 58 (the four face / form tests retired; the rolodex's Edit and create card, a renamed id on the card and the ghost, the stack cards' action and wiring, the live Define sheet, the seed chips, the tab names), `tools/test-library.mjs` 89 → 91 (the rename everywhere, every clash, the metric, the CLI); `npm test` 265 → 279; lint 187 / 0. Driven live on 8013 as a user (the browser pane for real key and mouse input, headless Chrome for the pictures): a Define card opens the editor without seeding, `99.9` typed key by key loses nothing and the status names the SLO, a rename follows through the stack card, the SLO and the burn alerts, an edited good PromQL turns the evidence custom, Esc returns to the card, Tab → Enter → edit → Esc end to end, the threshold editor shows Bound and Unit without scrolling, a custom SLI lands on the stack, the edits survive a reload, Verify opens read-only, 1366×768 without scrolling; the console carried only the documented `/auth/me` 404 and the favicon.
- **After review** (each reproduced with real key events before the change, one commit each): the Id field committed every valid prefix of a rename typed key by key and left the SLI at `error_rat` when the final `error_rate` clashed, its message wiped by the next render — the id is pre-checked on every keystroke and committed when the field is left, after the browser's own focus move (a Tab computed from a field the synchronous render had replaced landed nowhere), and a typed id the model does not carry keeps its message across the pack's answer; typing the key back persisted `{ id: '<key>' }` (the studio said *customised: id*, the engine saw no rename) — the key clears the rename; renaming a custom SLI lost the focus after the first keystroke (its keys carry its id) and the pack answering while Done had the focus dropped it to `<body>`, after which Esc did nothing — the render keeps the focus by the field's name when its key changed and the footer controls carry `editor:*` keys, and one document listener closes the editor on Esc from `<body>`; the editor sat UNDER the sticky header (z 91 vs 100: at 1366×768 the title row of 35 of 56 library editors was hidden and the header took the clicks) — the scrim is at 150, the dialog at 151; the status said *applying…* forever when the text meant the committed value — `setOverride` / `updateCustom` answer whether anything changed; an older request answering while a newer edit waited on the debounce read *applied · SLO <stale id>* — `editorDirtyAfterAnswer`; the *applied · …* answer arrived by replacing the whole dialog, so the polite live region never announced it and the entrance replayed — the same editor is redrawn in place, the status node kept; the Verify card was named *Edit …* over a read-only dialog — *View … (as compiled)*; the DEFINE status still said the edits wait on Compile; two transitions missed the reduced-motion list; the fixed Type cell hand-wrote the read-only row (now `editFieldHtml` with `showHint`); the dead `.build-rolo-needs` rule (a sweep over every `.build-edit*` / `.build-editor*` / `.build-rolo*` class now runs in the test). `tools/test-build-editor.mjs` 13 → 14 (the debounce answer) with the handler, render and stylesheet tests extended; `npm test` 279 → 280; lint 187 / 0. Re-driven live on 8013: `error_rate` key by key sends nothing and keeps its message through another field's answer, Enter / Tab / Esc commit the rename (Tab lands on the next control), `99.90` after `99.9` sends nothing and the status stays applied, the same status and dialog nodes survive the answer, Done keeps the focus, Esc from `<body>` closes, a custom SLI renamed key by key keeps the focus on the next field, no dialog under the header at 1920×1080 or 1366×768, the Verify card reads *View … (as compiled)*.

### The tier is a seed, the library values are copies, the definition is a wizard stage
After trying the axis (PR #103) the maintainer found three constraints wrong: an SLI above the tier was disabled ("needs tier-1"), objectives and windows were read-only library values, and the definition column stayed a live form on every step — *selecting the tier is just a template, obviously users know what they need; we are constraining so much it's not useful … library entries need to be customisable once copied over into the current pack — they are not links, just a local copy.* Three principles (`docs/BUILD_JOURNEY.md` "The seed and the copies"):
- **The tier is a seed, not a constraint.** It decides what a pack starts with (`defaultToggles`, the scaffold) and which rubric grades it (`tierRequirements`); it never forbids an SLI. The engine's gate is gone: `instantiatePack` takes any SLI of the passed entries at any tier (the `sli-excluded` warning is retired), a value declared per tier resolves for an above-tier SLI by walking towards the stricter tiers to the first declared value (`perTier`; `validateLibraryEntry` lets a per-tier map leave out a tier below the SLI's `minTier`), an SLI's own tier features (forecast, chaos, remediation) keep their gating, and the rubric still grades at the chosen tier — an extra SLI simply counts. `packc init --slis` / the new repeatable `--sli` take any SLI of the entries. In the studio the rolodex never disables a card: an above-tier one carries an informational chip (*from the tier-1 profile*) and the objective it starts with, `addSliSelection` never refuses, `retargetSlis` keeps every SLI the user has across a tier change (with its overrides) and only refreshes the tier's defaults, the L1 count reads *N from a higher tier*, `provenance.slis[id].aboveTier` / `profileTier` say so on the result.
- **Copies, not links** — copy-on-write over the library's defaults, in the engine, the API, the studio (and the CLI for the scalars). `overrides: { [sliId]: { objective?, window?, threshold?, query?, good?, total?, description?, unit? } }`: validated as usage errors (400 / exit 2, never 500) — the objective a ratio in (0, 1) (the studio edits a percent), the window one of the schema's `7d | 28d | 30d | 90d`, the threshold a finite upper bound (`comparison` refused with the reason: spec v1.2 has no direction field), the PromQL non-empty within `MAX_PARAM_LENGTH` and free of `${…}`, keys `^[a-z][a-z0-9_]{0,63}$` with `__proto__` / `constructor` / `prototype` refused and nothing read through the prototype chain, an unknown field named; an override for an SLI not in the pack is a warning `{ kind: 'override' }`. An overridden objective flows into the SLO (its id follows, `sloIdFor`), the burn alerts and the boards; an overridden expression **replaces** the library's PromQL (the grammar check still runs) and **drops its evidence honestly** to `{ status: 'custom', source: 'edited in the studio', note: 'the library evidence no longer applies' }`; `provenance.slis[id].customised`, `provenance.overrides` and the annotations `library.customised.slis.<id>` / `library.overrides` say what was customised. `custom: [{ id, type, objective, window, good + total | query + threshold, description?, unit? }]`: SLIs written from scratch — a slug id unique among the pack's SLIs (a clash names both), an SLO, a recording rule, burn alerts from `DEFAULT_BURN_PROFILE` (availability for a ratio, latency for a threshold — the profile a template without `burn` takes), the overview board's bindings, evidence `custom`, `library.source: 'custom'`, counted by the rubric like any SLI. The three routes take both (at most 64 overrides and 16 custom SLIs per request); `/compile` and `/register` accept the instantiate inputs in place of `canonical`; the index rows carry the PromQL templates and the bound as the defaults the studio shows. `packc init --override <sli>.<objective|window|threshold>=<value>` (repeatable); the CLI takes no custom SLI in this slice.
- **The definition is a wizard stage.** DEFINE seeds: its primary action is *Seed the pack →* (`build.seeded`, persisted; *Continue to Compile →* once seeded); on COMPILE and VERIFY the column is a recessed, read-only **seed card** (`seedCardModel`: the service as a definition list, one tier chip *seeded at tier-2 · 15 MUST · 1 SHOULD*, the entries as muted chips, what the pack carries, *Change seed →*) with the conformance summary live beneath it; `buildStepReachability` opens COMPILE on a valid **and seeded** definition (a draft persisted before the seed on COMPILE / VERIFY counts as seeded; migrated on load); once seeded the DEFINE column says in one line that a tier change re-grades and refreshes the defaults while the customisations stay and a removed product drops its SLIs; overrides follow their SLIs when a composition re-keys the ids (`retargetOverrides`). Muted means `--ink-3` on the card — AA in both themes, the stylesheet scan covers the new rules.
- **The surface** (the L1 sheet; `studio/build-copies-model.mjs` pure): every selected card gains *Customise*, which expands it in place (one card in the snap track) into its edit face — the objective as a percent with the library default beside it, the window with a datalist, the bound and unit, the PromQL in monospace textareas, the description, a per-field *↺ library default*, a *customised* chip, the evidence badge turned *custom* with *edited — the library's evidence no longer applies* once an expression is edited, the engine's promql warning and its `override <sli>.<field>: …` error under the field; the last card *+ Custom SLI* (the id auto-slugged from the name, the fields per type, the engine's 400 inline, *Add to the pack* tried before it is kept); a custom SLI behaves like any selected card (`updateCustom`, its switch off `removeCustom`); on VERIFY the read-only faces with the *customised* / *custom* chips and the provenance line, and the stack's L1 cards carry *customised: …* in their foot. Actions: `seed`, `setOverride` / `clearOverride`, `addCustom` / `updateCustom` / `removeCustom`; focus keys `ov:`, `cu:`, `cf:`, `customise:`.
- **Tests**: `tools/test-library.mjs` 83 → 89 (above tier at tier-2 and tier-3 with the walk, overrides and the SLO id / window / threshold, the evidence drop and provenance, the usage errors and prototype-pollution keys, the `override` warning, a custom ratio and threshold SLI in every section and counted by the rubric, duplicate ids, the CLI's `--override` and `--sli`); `server/test-smoke.mjs` (instantiate with an above-tier SLI, overrides, an edited query, a custom SLI; the 400s incl. the caps; compile and register from the inputs); `tools/test-build-model.mjs` 50 → 59 (the rolodex never disabled, above-tier informational, `addSliSelection` above the tier, `retargetSlis` keeping and refreshing, `retargetOverrides`, `effectiveOverrides` and the body, the edit-face and custom-form models, `seedCardModel` per step, the reachability with the seed and the legacy migration, the persistence list, headless renders of the seed card, an expanded edit face, the custom card and the VERIFY read-only face, the handlers, the AA scan over the new rules).
- **After review** (each reproduced live before the fix): a custom SLI may not take a library SLI's id even un-ticked (`shadows the library SLI …`), and two SLIs may not share an SLO id (`sloIdFor` joins `<sli>_<pct>` with `_`: an overridden `broker_availability` at 0.9999 and a custom `broker_availability_99` at 0.99 collided silently — one SLO id, two burn alerts, schema valid, MUST 15/15); `packc init --override __proto__.…` reaches the engine instead of setting a plain object's prototype; toggling a library entry keeps the other entries' picks (`retargetSlisForEntries`; the list was nulled to the defaults); a legacy `generate` / `validate` draft resumes on COMPILE / VERIFY (`restoreBuildDraft` is a pure model function now); a non-numeric objective or bound stays text so the engine names it (`got "abc"`) instead of NaN → null; Esc inside a face or form field leaves the field first; a field committed by Enter or Tab keeps a focus (the commit waits for the focus move and hands the action the field's key); Customise lands the focus in the face's first field; the Customise button's `aria-controls` names an existing face (none when closed); one `editFieldHtml` atom for the face and the form, VERIFY's face as value spans, the datalist from `SLO_WINDOWS`; the label names the field only, the default / hint / error describe the input; the custom form keeps one live draft (a typed id sticks and is what Add sends); the id field's clash / not-a-slug / SLO-id message appears while typing and Add follows the model's `canSubmit` (the renderer's second rule is gone); a rejected customised value is named in the compile note, marked on its card with the face closed, and its face is opened when the error arrives. The docs commit lands last and its three files no longer carry the document body pasted into the regex literals (`$\`` in a replacement).

### The pack is the axis — the definition column, the stack as the main surface, a sheet per layer
The scan put the stack on every Build step but left the controls where slice 2 had them (a form over the silhouette on DEFINE, an SLI table and a toggle grid over the stack on COMPILE, the clauses in a rail on the right). The Build screen now has one axis, the pack, and two columns on all three steps (`docs/BUILD_JOURNEY.md` "The axis"):
- **The definition column** (left, ~320 px, sticky; `studio/build-definition-view.mjs`, `buildDefinitionModel`): the service (name, owners, environment), the tier as a **segmented control** with a sliding thumb (tier-3 · tier-2 · tier-1, each segment with its MUST · SHOULD counts, the chosen tier's blurb beneath; `role=radiogroup`, arrow keys), the library entries as **chips** (products, then archetypes: title, evidence dot, SLIs at this tier; a selected chip is filled), and the **conformance summary** — status, pass · on a placeholder · fail, the failing clauses named, todos, warnings and placeholders left — which replaces the rail entirely (`renderClauseRail` / `buildRailModel` retired). DEFINE's fields, tier cards and entry cards moved here; its main column is the silhouette alone.
- **The stack** is the main surface, full width; a slab head or its **`+`** opens **the layer's sheet** (`studio/build-sheet-view.mjs`, `buildSheetModel({ layerId, build, library, requirements, stack, checklist, mode })`): a non-modal side panel over the stack — `role=dialog`, labelled by its title, Esc closes, focus moves in and returns to the slab head, the stack dimmed under a scrim (the heads stay above it so a click on another head switches the sheet), one at a time, the open layer on the draft (`sheetOpen`, never persisted). A large title (`L1 · Contract`) and the layer's question (*What should we measure?* · *Where does the telemetry flow?* · *How do we see it?* · *What happens when it breaks?* · *How do we prove it?* · *Who owns it?*), the layer's clauses at the tier with their state (the shared clause row), then its options, always what the pack actually carries (read from `adapted`): **L1** the **SLI rolodex** — a scroll-snapping carousel of SLI cards from the selected entries (every product's behind *show every product*), each with its product and evidence, type, metrics, the objective and window at the current tier large and the other tiers muted, an add / remove switch, *needs tier-1* when above the tier (disabled with the reason); adding an SLI of a product not yet selected selects that product too (one action: `addSli(entryId, sliId)`, pure part `addSliSelection` — the rest of the selection kept and re-keyed for the new composition); then the SLOs switch (*off is expected to drop 4 clauses of the tier: …*; once off, the engine's failing set — *off — 4 clauses fail with it: …*). **L2** the scrape jobs, receivers, backends, exporters and storage with the params they take (targets, endpoints, versions), editable. **L3** the Dashboards switch, the boards, views and recording rules. **L4** the Policy switch with the burn windows per SLO, the Routes switch with the channel params, the remediation templates. **L5** the Validation switch, the probes and chaos experiments with their target params, the baselines. **GOV** the owners and imports, read-only. A section switch names the clauses it drops — an expectation while on (`sectionClauses`), the engine's failing set once off (`sectionDrops`, from the checklist; the test instantiates with each section off and asserts both equal the engine's), and a slab that fails because of a section off elsewhere says why (L5 with SLOs off: *no SLO to test*); every param is edited on one sheet (`paramLayer`). COMPILE lost the SLI rows table and the SECTIONS grid (they live in the sheets); a slab whose section is off carries an *off* chip on its head.
- **One component on the three steps**: on COMPILE editable; on DEFINE a preview (every switch disabled, params read-only) with *Compose in Compile →* which switches step and reopens the same layer; on VERIFY read-only with the layer's pinned todos and their inline params (keyed by sheet and todo path, so a filled todo takes only its own inputs away). The sheet writes through the existing actions (`setSli` / `addSli`, `setToggle`, `setParam`) and re-instantiation redraws the stack, the sheet and the column; the sheet body's and the rolodex's scroll offsets survive the redraw, a focused card is re-centred, and a vanished sheet input hands focus to the sheet's next input, then its close control.
- **The language**: 24–32 px between groups, 14–16 px radii, a translucent sheet (`backdrop-filter: blur(20px) saturate(140%)` over the card colour, solid where unsupported), soft layered shadows, hairlines, real switches with a sliding knob, the segmented control's sliding thumb, one accent per layer (the layer token) used sparingly, sans for controls and mono for ids, 200 ms ease-out motion with `prefers-reduced-motion` respected, visible focus everywhere, both themes through the tokens — no 3D, no parallax, no new gradients. Honest gaps stay: *pass on a placeholder* is never plain green, a switch off says what it drops, a rejected param is marked on its sheet row.
- **Tests** (`tools/test-build-model.mjs`, 46 passing for the axis, 50 with the review fixes below): `buildDefinitionModel` and its headless render (ARIA, the summary in its five states, the wiring), `rolodexItems`, `addSliSelection` (the IBM MQ scenario, the one-to-two re-keying, the defaults collapsing, above-tier refused, purity), `paramLayer` partitioning every param, `sectionClauses` = the engine's dashboards-off failures, `sectionSwitch`, `buildSheetModel` per layer and per mode, the sheet headless per mode incl. escaping, its handlers through fake elements, the slab head's `aria-haspopup` / `+`, `stackExpanded`, the sheet focus fallbacks, one clause row for the summary and the sheet, and the stylesheet (the translucent surface with its fallback, the accent per layer, the motion, the snap, the reduced-motion block, no colour literal beyond the tokens). Driven live on 8013 (DEFINE → the L1 preview → *Compose in Compile* → the rolodex: remove one, an above-tier one disabled, *show every product*, an IBM MQ SLI added and the IBM MQ chip selected → L3 dashboards off / on → the L4 channel params → Esc returning focus to the slab head → VERIFY's L2 sheet with its ten todos, one filled → *Continue with visible gaps*), both themes, reduced motion emulated once; the console carried only the documented `/auth/me` 404 and the favicon.

### The axis, after review — the sheet stays non-modal, the switches say what the engine fails, AA on the sheet and the column
Two review passes (run-it; conventions · a11y · tests · commits) against the axis, each finding reproduced live before its fix (`docs/BUILD_JOURNEY.md` "The axis" carries the contracts):
- **The Tab trap skips the non-modal sheet.** `installDialogFocusTrap` caught every open `role=dialog`, so the sheet — `aria-modal=false` by design — was keyboard-modal: Tab cycled inside it and a Tab from `#build-name` was pulled into it (Owners unreachable). The trap now covers `[role="dialog"]:not([hidden]):not([aria-modal="false"])` (`TRAPPED_DIALOGS`) and takes the document as an argument, so a headless fake document drives it: Tab from the column walks on while a sheet is open, a modal (the About card) still traps and wraps.
- **A section switch states the engine's consequence.** With SLOs off the line named the burn alerts (which the engine keeps passing — quantified per SLO, the clause holds with none) and missed `L5.MUST.tier2_chaos_staging` (which it fails: the chaos experiments' steady-state hypothesis is an SLO). `sectionClauses` is now the section's scope minus the vacuous clauses (*off is expected to drop …* while on); once off, `sectionDrops` reads the checklist (*off — 4 clauses fail with it: …*), so the line never disagrees with the stack; `sectionNotes` puts *no SLO to test* on the L5 head and in its sheet. The test instantiates with each section off at tier-2 (SLOs at every tier) and asserts both readings equal the engine's failing set.
- **Focus survives the re-render** of a tier segment (arrow keys) and a library chip (Space): both carry a focus key (`tier:<id>`, `entry:<id>`) and `focusFallbackSelectors` knows them; before, focus fell to `<body>` and a second key did nothing.
- **A newly opened sheet starts at its top**: the body's and the rolodex's scroll keys are per layer (`sheet:L3`, `rolodex:L1`), so L3 no longer inherits L1's offset.
- **The rolodex moves where a smooth scroll is cancelled** (the desktop app's embedded pane): if the track has not moved 250 ms after a smooth scroll, `wireRolodex` sets the offset instantly (scroll-behavior forced to auto and restored) and the snap settles; a move to the current offset is not issued.
- **The entrance plays on opening only**: a one-shot (`buildSheetEntering`, set by `openSheet` for a different layer) draws that render's scrim and panel with `is-entering`, where the animations now live; every re-render while the sheet stays open (a switch, a param, a settled keystroke) rebuilds it without the class. Measured before: the panel slid in six times per SLI switch flip.
- **WCAG AA on the sheet and the column, both themes**: text at or below 12 px is `--ink-3` (`--ink-4` read 4.27:1 on the dark card), `--ink-5` is no longer a text colour there (the other tiers on a card, the chip kind's sub, the clause id — now 11 px), the accent as text (the eyebrow, the item ids, the type pill, a selected card's state, a todo's card chip) is `--accent-text: color-mix(in srgb, var(--accent) 65%, var(--ink))` (the raw light L1 amber read 3.3:1), the chosen segment's name takes the same mix of its criticality colour, the Scaffold chip drops its literal grey. A test scans every rule of the axis block and computes each text colour against its surface from the tokens (113 offenders on the previous stylesheet, none now).
- **The status is announced once**: the summary block is rebuilt on every re-render, so its `aria-live` announced nothing or everything; it is gone, and `renderBuildView` writes the settled status as one line (`buildStatusLine`) to a persistent visually-hidden `role=status` node outside the view (`#build-status`), only when it changed.
- **One param-row atom** (`paramRowHtml(p, { readOnly: true })`, the label block shared through `paramLabelHtml`; the sheet's `paramReadHtml` copy had already lost the flag's title) and **one evidence vocabulary** (the chip model drops its copy of the atom's words). The module table no longer lists the retired `buildRailModel`.
- **Commits**: the branch was rewritten before the fixes so each commit stands alone — the docs land first (the source comments cite "The axis"), the feat commit no longer imports symbols only the test commit uses (208 → 187 lint warnings at that commit), the tests add their own imports.

### Build is a live scan — the layer stack on every Build step (foundation slice)
Build produced a pack in Discover's language and never showed it: forms plus a checklist rail, while Discover draws every pack as L1 … GOV slabs and artefact cards. The centre stage of every Build step is now the layer stack of the pack being compiled (`docs/BUILD_JOURNEY.md`, "The scan: Build renders the layer model" — the principles and the three slices; this is the first):
- **The artefacts are the adapter's.** `POST /api/library/instantiate` also returns `adapted` — `adapt()` of the env-overlaid canonical, exactly as `POST /api/validate` computes it (the smoke test asserts the two byte-identical for the same canonical; the captured fixture carries the field and its stability test compares it against a fresh in-process adapt). The stack draws that projection with the same `.card` markup Discover uses: the card body is one shared helper, `studio/card-html.mjs` `artefactCardHtml`, that Discover's `renderCard` and the stack both call (the stateful shell — active card, unresolved references, the benchmark action — stayed in `renderCard`; only the pure HTML moved), so what Build shows on COMPILE is the artefact list Discover shows after the hand-off, id for id. A Scaffold card is dashed in both views.
- **The model** (`studio/build-model.mjs` `buildStackModel({ adapted, checklist, requirements, candidates, todos, params, mode, toggles, expanded })`, pure, under `node:test`): the slabs in `LAYER_DEFS` order (L2X only with an artefact or a clause, GOV neutral, L4 with its policy · alerting · self-healing subgroups), each slab's edge state from the clause checklist of its dimension (fail > pending > placeholder > pass, with the clause and why), its maturity as clause counts with pass-on-placeholder its own segment, ghost cards for the rubric (every clause of the tier on DEFINE — the silhouette; a clause still unmet afterwards, as *Missing*, Discover's word), the selection's SLI and SLO candidates on L1, the todos pinned to the slab of their path family and to the Scaffold card the adapter minted for them (the engine's symbols and the adapter's ids agree: 21 todos, 21 Scaffold cards, the same set), a section switched off dimming the slab it feeds, and the detail artefacts Discover folds (panels, queries) folded here too.
- **The view** (`studio/build-stack-view.mjs` `renderBuildStack` / `buildStackHtml` + `wireBuildStack`): Discover's `.section` / `.section-head` / `.section-grid` markup and layer tokens, a verdict edge per slab, a click on the slab head opens the layer's clauses, the theme tokens only so light and dark both follow. **DEFINE** draws the silhouette under the library entries, reshaping live with the tier and the entries, the edges lighting up as soon as the selection compiles. **COMPILE**'s canvas is the live stack — the SLI rows and the section toggles stay as the control area above it, the warnings and the schema verdict between, the YAML as a collapsible below; untick an SLI and L1 loses its card and its SLO, switch dashboards off and L3 dims, its clauses turn red on the edge and come back as ghost cards. **VERIFY** draws the stack with the todos on their slabs (the inline parameter inputs unchanged; a pin on the card each names scrolls to it) and adds the per-layer maturity bars to the conformance card; the verdict cards, the artefacts strip and *Ready to continue?* are otherwise unchanged. The clause rail is a compact summary — status, the three counts, the failing clauses always listed, how many pass only on a placeholder, the full list under "all clauses" — since the per-layer clauses now live on the slabs. Two never-persisted UI fields on the draft (`stackOpen`, `railOpen`) keep an opened layer open across the debounced re-instantiation.
- **Tests**: `tools/test-build-model.mjs` (slab order and L2X gating, edge states incl. the dashboards-off red edge and the cold all-pending silhouette, the silhouette across the three tiers with the rubric's labels, the L1 candidates, todo pinning per family and per card, L4 subgroups, maturity counts, dimming, a headless render of the stack and of the compact rail, `artefactCardHtml` as the one card body, the artefact list deep-equal to the adapter's for the same canonical); `server/test-smoke.mjs` (the `adapted` field and its identity with `/api/validate`).
- **After review**: a card's *todo* pin on COMPILE jumps to Verify on that todo (the todos are drawn there; the reveal — scroll, flash, caret — is one atom the stack and the controller share); focus survives a filled todo — a todo's inputs key by slab and todo path, and when the input is gone focus moves to the same slab's next input or its edge instead of `<body>`; a reload on VERIFY resumes on VERIFY once the pack is back (`wantedStep` on the draft, never persisted, honoured once); COMPILE's stale note reads "marked on its row on Define and Verify"; the rail and the slabs draw one clause row (`studio/build-atoms.mjs` `clauseRowHtml`, the placeholder and failing rows naming their todos in both); a Scaffold card keeps its focus ring in Discover; the maturity number prints the pass share and the pass-on-placeholder share separately ("0% +100% ◐", never one green number) and the bar carries the counts as its label; the slab verdict is a pill on the card surface, WCAG AA in both themes (the test computes the ratios from the theme tokens); the dead slab accent (`--slab`, `DISCO_SLAB_ACCENT`) is gone — a slab's colour is its layer token.
- **Planned, written into the design doc, not built**: slice 2 (L1 SLO gauges, the L2 flow strip, L3 dashboard wireframes from the compiled Grafana JSON, the L4 chain, L5 cards) and slice 3 (the isometric slab stack with an acquisition animation, the landings' miniature stacks).

### The BUILD journey, slice 2: the studio journey and the API
The three steps the maintainer asked for — select service/tier, generate the pack, validate it and generate its artefacts — as a second journey beside Discover · Diagnose · Remediate, over the slice-1 engine (`docs/BUILD_JOURNEY.md`, "Slice 2"):
- **The API** (`server/index.mjs`, behind the same auth/tenancy posture as `POST /api/validate`): `GET /api/library` (the index plus the scaffold params), `GET /api/library/requirements/:tier` (the rubric filtered by `minTier`; 400 names the known tiers), `GET /api/library/:id` (index row + full SLI templates; 404 names the known entries), `POST /api/library/instantiate` (the `instantiatePack` inputs → `canonical`, `canonicalYaml`, `todos`, `provenance`, `warnings`, `schemaErrors`, `summary`, `conformance` of the env-overlaid canonical; Node passes the Lezer PromQL grammar like `packc init`; an engine usage error is 400, never 500), `POST /api/library/compile` (`{ canonical, target }` → one artefact through `compile.mjs`, nothing registered), `POST /api/library/register` (`registerUploadedPack` exactly as `/api/validate`, source hint `library:<entries>@<tier>`). `POST /api/validate` attaches `summary.onPlaceholder` whenever a pack carries `library.todo.*` annotations — the engine gained `todosFromAnnotations` / `hasLibraryTodos` so the todos are recoverable from a pack that arrives as YAML (asserted equal to a fresh instantiation for every entry at every tier and for a composed pack in `tools/test-library.mjs`), and `libraryIndex` reports the SLO window per tier beside the objective.
- **The studio**: `state.mode 'build'`, `BUILD_TABS` (Define · What Are We Observing? · Compile · What Should We Watch? · Verify · Is It Ready to Use?) rendered by the same header renderer as the analysis cards with the same three accents — a step card is reachable when the previous step's inputs are valid, locked otherwise; entry points on the home hero, the service gate and the upload popover ("Build from the library…"); the logo still returns home. `studio/build-model.mjs` (pure models: the select / generate / validate models, the clause checklist's three states — pass, pass on a placeholder, fail — step reachability, the todo grouping by artefact), `studio/build-api.mjs` (loaders, `fetchFn` injectable), one renderer module per step. DEFINE: name, owners, environment, the three tiers with their MUST / SHOULD counts and the clauses each adds, the entries as Products / Archetypes cards with evidence badge and SLIs per tier, the selection's params with placeholders flagged. COMPILE: per-entry SLI toggles (an SLI above the tier disabled with the tier it needs), the objective and window at the tier (read-only), the section toggles, the pack YAML with download; every change re-instantiates through the API (debounced) and the right rail — shared by the three steps — fills in the tier's clauses live. VERIFY: the conformance verdict at the tier with the three states, the schema verdict, the warnings by kind, the todos grouped by artefact with the param that fills each one editable inline (a filled todo disappears), one artefact card per compile target with preview and download, "Download pack YAML", and "Open in Discover", which registers the pack the way an upload is registered and switches to Discover with it selected, saying how many placeholders remain. The draft survives a reload as inputs only (the canonical is re-instantiated, never stored).
- **Tests**: `tools/test-build-model.mjs` (the models over an instantiate response captured from the running server into `tools/fixtures/build/`, asserted stable against a fresh instantiation), the six routes and the identity posture in `server/test-smoke.mjs` / `server/test-auth-local.mjs`; `npm run lint:build`, `npm run test:build`.
- **The first decision is about the pack**: both landings (the signed-in service gate and the local hero) open with "What would you like to do?" — *Check an existing service or pack* (the service picker or an import) or *Build a new pack* (Define · Compile · Verify); VERIFY ends on *Ready to continue?* with two exits, *Resolve or adjust* (back to Define) and *Continue with visible gaps* (*Continue to Discover* when no placeholder remains); both paths join at "Pack available in Discover" with the placeholders still visible, so Diagnose grades them as gaps (`docs/BUILD_JOURNEY.md` "Where it starts").
- **After review**: a param value the engine refuses keeps the previous pack on screen — marked stale, the reason on the row that carries the value on Define and under its todo on Verify; Verify stays reachable and the hand-off is blocked until it is fixed — instead of dropping the pack and bouncing the user to Generate, the one step without parameter inputs; an explicit SLI list follows the tier (a key the new tier does not reach is dropped, a key it unlocks comes in ticked), so the `sli-excluded` warning can be cleared; DEFINE counts the placeholders the engine wrote (`provenance.placeholders`, the rail's number) rather than every flagged param, and a param input hints the resolved default (`#orders-api-oncall`, not the raw template); the service name is bounded at 45 characters once slugged — what the schema's 64-character ids allow after the longest derived suffix, `-deployment-overlay` — and VERIFY's schema footer no longer blames a toggle; the logo returns home from build mode and every mode transition repaints the header cards (leaving the journey through its exit bar once left the build cards over the hero); the DEFINE status line escapes the typed name; the artefact buttons carry accessible names and a disabled SLI row's title says the tier it needs; `POST /api/library/register` labels only a library-built pack `library:<entries>@<tier>` (a plain pack gets `metadata.name`, as `/api/validate`); `loadRequirements` shares one request per tier in flight; the engine bounds a param value at `MAX_PARAM_LENGTH` (4096 characters) and echoes at most ten unknown keys; the build fixtures are pretty-printed like the repo's other JSON fixtures.

### The BUILD journey, slice 1: the pack library, the tier scaffold and `packc init`
A second journey beside Discover · Diagnose · Remediate, for teams that have no pack yet — Define (what are we observing?) · Compile (what should we watch?) · Verify (is it ready to use?), handing the produced pack to Discover. This slice is the engine with no UI (`docs/BUILD_JOURNEY.md` is the contract slice 2's studio and API build on):
- **The library is data** (`library/products/*.library.yaml`, `library/archetypes/*.library.yaml`, format `library: v1`; `library/README.md`): one versioned entry per product or archetype — SLI templates with metric names, PromQL, `minTier`, per-tier SLO objectives and a burn profile, params (`${id}`), scrape jobs, product boards, derived views, synthetic probes, chaos and remediation templates, and an evidence block per entry and per SLI (`recorded-live | reference-pack | upstream-docs | semconv`, sources, notes, gaps). Ten entries: kafka, prometheus, grafana (derived from the reference packs, measured live 2026-09-22), ibm-mq (the certified pack of mq-observability-pack, 2026-09-16), alertmanager and otel-collector (the stack self-metric alias table, pinned stack 2026-09-07), loki and tempo (`up` of the lab's scrape job plus the collector / promtail rows — no `loki_*` / `tempo_*` request metric is in the evidence, so none is named), http-service and queue-consumer (OTel semconv v1.27.0 in Prometheus spelling, marked not verified live). `validateLibraryEntry` refuses an undeclared `${param}`, a listed metric name that does not appear in the query, and an entry without a ratio SLI at tier-3 or a threshold SLI by tier-2.
- **One tier scaffold** (`tools/lib/library.mjs` `tierScaffold`, shared by every entry) produces the structural sections a tier needs — otel, backends, pipelines, storage, recording rules, the overview / burn / deployment-overlay / customer-impact boards, burn-rate policy from named profiles (severities demoted at tier-3), routes, remediation, baselines, probes, chaos — sized so that every MUST clause of `tools/lib/conformance.mjs` applying at the tier passes with every toggle on; `tierRequirements(tier)` is that rubric filtered by `minTier`, not a second rubric. Toggles (`slos`, `policy`, `routes`, `dashboards`, `validation`, the SLI list) leave honest gaps: a section off is absent and both the schema and the rubric say so. A remediation is triggered by its SLI's fast burn alert under the compiler's name (`alert:<slo>_burn_<factor>x_<short>_<long>`), the alerts a library pack compiles — the entries carry no `trigger` (the reference packs' symptom-alert names resolved only where those repos ship rule files; in a library pack 0 of 21 did) and the suite asserts every trigger and every chaos `expected_alert` against the compiled rules.
- **Placeholders and provenance.** Values the library cannot know are params flagged `placeholder: true`; left at their default they land in the pack as a plausible value AND as a todo `{ path, fields, what, clauses, params }` and the annotation `library.todo.<artefact symbol>` — the crawler's `crawler.scaffold.<symbol>` convention — and `tools/lib/adapter.mjs` now lists `library.todo.` among its scaffold prefixes, so the studio parks a placeholder artefact as Scaffold like a crawler stub. The backend versions the pack declares (`telemetry.backends[].version.declared`, `storage.<signal>.version`) are placeholder params too — `prometheus_version`, `loki_version`, `tempo_version` — with `min` the scaffold's floor, so a team is told to state what it runs before tier-1's `gating: enforce` applies. Every produced pack carries `library.source = <entry>@<version>`, `library.tier`, `library.toggles`, `library.slis`, `library.params`, per-SLI `library.evidence.slis.<id>` and `metadata.labels.source: library`.
- **Engine API** (pure, browser-safe, served at `/lib`): `parseLibraryEntry`, `validateLibraryEntry`, `libraryIndex`, `tierRequirements`, `defaultToggles`, `instantiatePack(entry | entries, { name, tier, environment, owners, params, toggles })` → `{ canonical, todos, provenance, warnings }` (several entries compose into one pack, ids prefixed, params namespaced `<entry>.<param>`; an unknown param key or a non-scalar value is an error that lists the known keys, never a silent drop; a selected SLI above the tier is excluded with a `sli-excluded` warning, not fatal, so a tier change after the SLIs were ticked keeps the rest), `validationSummary`, `symbolOf`. Node side: `server/library.mjs` `loadLibrary({ root })` → `{ entries, errors }` (a missing root is an error naming it, not an empty library), and `library/` is in the package's `files` so an installed `packc init` has its entries.
- **`packc init`** (`tools/pack-init.mjs`): `--list`, `--show <entry>`, `--entry <id>[,<id>] --tier <tier> --name <svc> [--env] [--owner]... [--param k=v]... [--slis a,b] [--no-<section>] [--out] [--json]`; YAML to stdout, todos, warnings and the conformance line to stderr; exit 0 · 1 (the pack does not validate against the schema or fails a MUST clause of its tier, so a CI caller can tell MUST 14/15 from 15/15) · 2 (usage), the convention of validate-pack and journey. A `--param` value carrying a double quote, a backslash or a control character is refused (it is spliced verbatim into label matchers), and every SLI expression is parsed with the Lezer grammar once the values are in — a failure is `warning [promql]` and exit 1; `instantiatePack` takes the parser as `promql` (the engine stays browser-safe) and returns `warnings: [{ kind, message, sli?, field? }]`, which also carries the burn-rule generator's own warnings on the produced policy (kind `burn-rules`, compiled once at build time) — and the prometheus entry's four subtracted good legs now carry the `(… or vector(0))` guard the generator asks for, with `== bool` on the up comparison, stated in its evidence notes (the reference pack's forms are unguarded; upstream fix pending).
- **Proof** (`tools/test-library.mjs`, in `npm test`; `npm run lint:library`): every entry × every tier with default toggles validates against the v1.2 schema, compiles through every `compile.mjs` target, builds its boards with `checkBindings` green, passes every applicable MUST (the failing set is asserted ⊆ the clauses the todos name, and empty) and every SHOULD at tier-1, carries the provenance annotations, and its todos are exactly the `library.todo.*` annotations; dashboards off fails exactly the dashboard clauses and policy off exactly the burn-rate clause; the adapter projects the placeholders as Scaffold; composition, SLI selection, param overrides and the CLI's exit codes are pinned; three goldens (`tools/fixtures/library/kafka.tier-2`, `ibm-mq.tier-1`, `http-service.tier-3`) are byte-stable (`node tools/test-library.mjs --update`).
### Build number: the commit the studio runs from, in the footer, the CLI and /api/version
"v0.4.0" in the footer said nothing about *which* 0.4.0 was running. There is no build step, so the identity of a process is its commit, read once by one module.
- **`server/build-info.mjs`** — `buildInfo({ root, refresh })` → `{ version, build, commit, branch, dirty, date, shallow, source }`: version from package.json; from git, `build` = `rev-list --count HEAD` (an integer that climbs with every commit on the branch — 975 on develop the day this landed), `commit` = the short sha (what makes the number unique), `branch` (`null` on a detached HEAD — a CI checkout of a tag or a PR merge ref — never the literal `HEAD`), `dirty` = `status --porcelain` non-empty (uncommitted **or untracked** files), `date` = the commit's ISO date, `shallow` = `rev-parse --is-shallow-repository`; `source: 'git'`. A shallow clone (`git clone --depth 1`, actions/checkout's default) has no history to count, so `build` is `null` there rather than the clone depth. Four git spawns per read (rev-parse answers the top-level directory, the shallow flag and the branch at once; `log -1 --format=%h%n%cI` the sha and the date), every one guarded (no binary, no repo, or a git-less copy sitting inside somebody else's repo — `--show-toplevel` must be this tree), falling back to a `build.json` stamp at the root (`source: 'file'`; a stamped build number must be a non-negative safe integer, anything else reads `null`), then to package.json alone (`build: null`, `source: 'package'`). Memoised per root; `refresh: true` re-reads. `buildLabel(info)` → `v0.4.0 · build 975 · 9c4f827 · develop` (`· dirty` appended; `build unknown` followed by whatever is known — `v0.4.0 · build unknown · 9c4f827 · develop · shallow` on a shallow clone, `v0.4.0 · build unknown` from package.json alone), `buildShortLabel` → `v0.4.0 · build 975`. Node-only (child_process), so it lives under `server/`: `tools/lib/` is served to the browser at `/lib` and must stay free of `node:*` imports; the CLI and the stamp tool import it as `../server/build-info.mjs`.
- **`npm run build:stamp`** (`tools/stamp-build.mjs`) writes the git-ignored `build.json` for a tree that will be copied without `.git` — a tarball, a container. A checkout with history is stamped (exit 0); a shallow clone is refused (exit 2, naming the fix: `fetch-depth: 0`, `git fetch --unshallow`) and a `build.json` lying there is removed rather than copied into an image; a git-less copy that already carries a readable stamp keeps it (exit 0, `kept existing build.json: …` — the file is the copy's identity); a git-less copy without one has nothing to stamp (exit 2; an unreadable `build.json` is removed). `--root` needs a directory with a package.json, and unknown arguments are refused (exit 3), so a typo never stamps the wrong tree. The Dockerfile copies `build.json*` next to package.json and says to stamp before `docker build`; its `OBSERVOGRAM_BUILD` ARG no longer defaults to `container` and now overrides `/healthz`'s composite string only. The image also creates `/app/.observogram` for the `node` user it runs as — the documented `docker run` had died with `EACCES` while seeding the admin user, since before this branch — and the run line carries the `OBSERVOGRAM_ADMIN_PASSWORD` the server needs to start off loopback; the k8s README and the kustomization comment gained the stamp step.
- **`GET /api/version`** — the fields plus `label`, public (registered before the auth and tenancy middlewares: the footer fills before anyone signs in, and it holds nothing secret; the README's Security Posture names it as one of the two routes readable without a session), `Cache-Control: no-store` so a proxy never pins an old build to a new process. `/healthz` keeps its `build: "975.9c4f827+dirty"` composite, now derived from the same reader (`server/version.mjs`, resolved on first use rather than at import), so a worktree no longer reads `untracked`.
- **Studio** — the footer's `<span id="build-label">` ships package.json's version as its fallback and `studio/build-label.mjs` fills it at boot following docs/UI_CONVENTIONS.md: loaders `loadBuildInfo` and `loadHealth` (fetchFn injectable) → `buildLabelModel` (pure) → `renderBuildLabel` (the span) and `renderVersionChrome` (the span, the Advanced menu's About entry, the header subtitle in the short form, the brand tooltip); `loadVersion()` in app.mjs is only their composition. The title is the ISO date + `source: git | file | package` (+ `shallow clone: no history to count`); on a failed fetch the fallback stays. `loadVersion()` is called from `boot()` again — the call was lost in the merge that landed About (1b63b35), which is why the About entry always read "version unknown" — and the About modal now shows commit · branch · dirty, the commit date, the source and, on a shallow clone, that there is no count. `app.css`'s header drops its stale `v0.3`.
- **`packc --version`** prints the label (the version stays the first token; `--version --json` prints the fields), and the help banner carries it on its first line.
- **Tests** — `tools/test-build-info.mjs` (a throwaway repo with three commits reads build 3 with git's sha and branch, a fourth uncommitted file flips dirty, a nested git-less copy borrows nothing, a `--depth 1` clone reads shallow with `build: null` and is refused by the stamp, a detached HEAD reads no branch, the stamp round-trips through a copy without `.git` and survives a re-run there, a stamped build number outside the non-negative safe integers reads `null`, `--root` without a value / without a package.json / with an unknown argument exits 3, package-only reads `build: null`, the labels, the memo and `refresh`, the studio loaders, model and both renderers headlessly, the footer fallback === package.json version); the smoke suite covers `/api/version` (200, JSON, no-store, the fields, agreement with `/healthz` — on a shallow checkout by the sha alone — and the served shell's span) and clears `OBSERVOGRAM_BUILD` so a shell export cannot change what it asserts; the identity suite asserts `/api/version` answers 200 without a session in both the stand-alone and the seeded posture.

### Dashboards: symmetric generated boards — full rows, equal heights, balanced tiles, bound-SLO bar gauges
