`flow()` still lays panels out in reading order, but what the generator hands it now always closes every row: on the committed boards a source board with one bound SLI put a w4 tile, the w12 bar gauge and one w6 burn curve on a row, wrapped the second curve alone underneath and left a hole under the tile (`kafka-consumer-lag`, `grafana-alerting-health`); with two SLIs the tiles and the bar gauge filled 20 columns and both curves wrapped to a half row (`grafana-plugins-and-auth`, `prometheus-query-engine`, `prometheus-tsdb-health`); one derived view rendered at w12; a pack with 5 or 7 SLIs left a 4- or 3-column hole in the unified tile row; a burn-template board's w4 tiles wrapped 6 + 2 for 8 SLOs; and the bar gauge on a bound board listed every SLO of the pack. Every visual row of every board the generator emits is now 24 columns wide with one height (`tools/lib/dashboards/lib.mjs`, `generic.mjs`):
- **Tiles** (`splitWidths(n)`, `tileRows(n)` in lib): a row of n tiles takes widths that sum to 24 and differ by at most one, the remainder on the first tiles — 5 → 5,5,5,5,4; 7 → 4,4,4,3,3,3,3; 6 → 4×6; 8 → 3×8. More than eight take the fewest rows of at most eight, the larger rows first (9 → 5,5,5,5,4 over 6,6,6,6; 13 → 7 + 6; 25 → 7 + 6 + 6 + 6), so no tile is ever narrower than w3 (a single row of 12 would have been twelve w2 stats). A lone tile is not stretched to w24 (a banner-wide number with a sparkline): it sits w6 h8 beside its own trend — `derivedSliTrend`, the SLI over time with the objective or threshold as a dashed line — or, on a burn-template board, beside the alert timeline. Two tiles share the row at w12. The unified board's SLI tiles, a source board's and a burn-template board's SLO tiles (`burnTiles`, 8 SLOs → 8 × w3 on one row) all follow this; `derivedSliTiles` takes `widths` (per tile) and `h` besides `w`. A pack module's `sliTiles(pack, ids, { widths, h })` receives the same hint; tiles that ignore it are laid out as the module returns them, on their own row above the standard bar gauge and curves (never inside the one- or two-tile shapes that assume w6 h8 tiles).
- **The contract block** — the unified board's (every SLI and SLO) and a source board's (what it binds, each id once, only ids the pack declares) — by the number of SLIs N and SLOs M it holds (`contractBlock`): M = 0 the tiles only; N = 0 the bar gauge w12 with the two burn curves w6; N = 1, M = 1 the tile, the SLO's burn tile and the two curves, all w6 h8 on one row (a one-bar gauge has nothing to compare with and sat mostly empty in an h8 panel); N = 1, M ≥ 2 the tile w6 h8 beside the bar gauge w18, the curves w12 on the next row; N = 2 both tiles w6 h8 beside the bar gauge w12, the curves w12 below; N ≥ 3 tile rows of their own, then the bar gauge w12 with the curves w6. A burn-template board with a single SLO shows its burn tile w6 h8 beside the alert timeline w18 and no bar gauge.
- **`burnBars(binds, w, h, sloIds)`** filters the series to the SLOs a board holds (`{slo=~"a|b"}`); when `sloIds` names every SLO of the pack, matches none of them (a burn board with `params.slos: []`, a source board whose only SLO binding is a typo) or is not given, the expression is the bare series as before, so no board ever carries the empty selector `{slo=~""}`. The overrides (label and thresholds per SLO) already followed the bound list; only the expression changed. The unified boards' bar gauge is unchanged and the MQ pack's module-built boards (`burnBars(null)`, `burnBars(all, 12, 8)`) are byte-identical.
- **Derived views** (`viewWidths(n)`, `derivedViewPanel(..., { w })`): 1 → 24; 2 → 12,12; 3 → 8,8,8; four or more in pairs of 12, an odd count closing with a trio of 8. On the unified Signals row and a source board's §5 row the views that render as graphs come first; a note (an undeclared view, or one with neither a metric nor a declared SLI, such as the golden-signals views) is w24 on a row of its own below them. The per-resource template board renders its view at w24. A view on a trio row is w8, below the width at which `ts()` switches a many-series graph to the right-hand table legend with last/max, so it gets the bottom list legend; on a full or half row the table legend as before.
- **Bar gauges with few bars pin their text** (`tools/lib/dashboards/lib.mjs` `bargauge` takes `text`; `burnBars` sets `{ titleSize: 16, valueSize: 28 }` when it shows three bars or fewer): Grafana scales a bar gauge's name and value with the panel height, and the two-SLO gauge of a bound board rendered 60 px truncated names once it stood alone on an 8-high row. A gauge of every SLO is unchanged.
- **`checkBindings`** now reports a source-board binding or a burn board's `params.slos` entry that names no SLI or SLO of the pack (`binding slos.nope names no SLO of the pack`) instead of letting it shape the block or filter the bar gauge to nothing.
- **Goldens regenerated** with the generator's own commands (`node tools/gen-dashboards.mjs --pack reference-packs/<p>.pack.yaml`, `node tools/test-golden-compile.mjs --update`), no hand edits. Panel by panel the diff of the fifteen `reference-packs/dashboards/*.json` against the previous generator is `gridPos` only, plus the bar-gauge expression on the bound boards (the filter), the golden-signals note moving below the two graphs on the three unified boards (three panel ids renumber) and, on the two boards that bind one SLI and one SLO (`grafana-alerting-health`, `kafka-consumer-lag`), the one-bar gauge replaced by that SLO's burn tile; no other expression, title, description or binding moved. The five payment-service `grafana-dashboard` compile goldens (`payment-overview`: 3 SLIs + 1 SLO → a tile row 8,8,8 over the filtered bar gauge and the curves) moved by `gridPos` and the bar-gauge expression; edge-hostile-names' `hostile-overview` binds nothing and did not move.
- **Test** (`tools/test-gen-pack.mjs`): every board of every reference pack, and of synthetic packs cloned from the kafka pack with 1, 2, 3, 5, 7, 9, 12, 13 and 25 SLIs (one SLO each) × 1, 3, 4 and 5 derived views (boards rebuilt for every contract shape — all, first, pair, one SLI with two SLOs, a tile and a trend bound to the same SLI, SLOs only, SLIs only, burn boards of every SLO, of the first and of none, a per-resource board — `checkBindings` green), is grouped by `gridPos.y` and every row must sum to 24, tile from x = 0 without a gap and share one height, with no stat narrower than w3 or wider than w12 with a sparkline and no `{slo=~""}` selector; the contract-block shapes, the tile rows, the lone tile's trend, the note-under-graphs order, the legend by width, the 8 × w3 burn tiles, the bar-gauge filter (present on a bound board, absent on the unified board and on a burn board of every SLO or none), the unknown-binding report and a pack module's tiles (honouring the hint, and ignoring it) are pinned.

### Reference packs: the names the lab exposes (prometheus, grafana, kafka) and an honest certification row
Measured on the mq-observability-pack lab on 2026-09-22 — Prometheus 3.14.0, Grafana 12.4.11 OSS, `apache/kafka:3.9.2` (the lab's image pin; KRaft) with `jmx_prometheus_javaagent` 1.6.0 + the Strimzi `kafka-metrics.yaml` rule set and `kafka_exporter` 1.10.0, all scraped by that Prometheus. With the packs at 54223dd loaded, seven recording rules never produced a sample (`prometheus:scrape_duration:p99_5m`, `prometheus:query_latency:p99_5m`, `grafana:database_query:p99_5m`, `grafana:login_success:ratio_5m`, `kafka:produce_latency:p99_5m`, `kafka:fetch_latency:p99_5m`, `kafka:controller_elections:rate_1h`, plus their error ratios) and the kafka "Per topic throughput" panel was empty, because the SLIs named metric families that do not exist. Every replacement expression below was executed live before it was written; the proofs are in the packs' evidence documents (`docs/catalogue-evidence/*.md`, "Measured live — 2026-09-22"). SLI ids, SLO ids, recording-rule names and dashboard uids are unchanged.
- **prometheus** (`reference-packs/prometheus.pack.yaml`): `scrape_duration_p99` = `max(quantile_over_time(0.99, scrape_duration_seconds[5m]))` — `scrape_duration_seconds` is one of the series Prometheus generates per scrape, a per-target gauge with no histogram form (0 `_bucket` series on 3.14.0); `query_latency_p99` = `histogram_quantile(0.99, sum by (le)(rate(prometheus_http_request_duration_seconds_bucket{handler=~"/api/v1/query|/api/v1/query_range"}[5m])))` — `prometheus_engine_query_duration_seconds` is a summary; the 3.x-only `prometheus_engine_query_duration_histogram_seconds_bucket` is recorded in the evidence as the engine-side alternative and not used. On a healthy server the HTTP histogram reads 0.099 s: 0.99 × its first bucket bound, every request in `le="0.1"`, i.e. "p99 ≤ 0.1 s" rather than a measured latency; the 1 s objective sits on the `le="1.0"` bound. The histogram's presence on 2.x is cited from the v2.55.1 source (`web/web.go`), not measured. `target_versions` extended to 3.x. The "documented, not fixed" notes in `tools/lib/contracts/stack-self-metrics.mjs` and `docs/MCP_INTEGRATION.md` now say the pack was fixed (the rows keep their aliases).
- **grafana** (`reference-packs/grafana.pack.yaml`): `database_query_latency_p99` reads `grafana_database_queries_duration_seconds_bucket` (plural; the singular family never existed) and the pack declares the prerequisite `[database] instrument_queries = true` (off by default) in `metadata.annotations.prerequisites`; `login_success_ratio` is `POST /login` requests answered 200 over all `POST /login` requests from `grafana_http_request_duration_seconds_count`, the total leg kept only where its rate is positive (`sum(rate(…) > 0)`) so a window without a login reads no data instead of NaN (measured: one NaN sample per 5-minute canary cycle without the guard; the 14×/5m alert needs two bad logins in 5 minutes, which that canary never produces). `grafana_api_login_post_total` counts successes only (per `pkg/api/login.go`) and `grafana_user_login_errors_total` does not exist; Grafana's own `grafana_authn_authn_{successful,failed}_login_total{client}` pair is defined at 12.4.11 (the failed one has no series until a login fails) and would also cover OAuth / SAML clients, but a failed login was not exercised, so the pack keeps the HTTP pair it executed end to end and says so; `alerting_rule_evaluation_success_ratio` guards the subtracted failures counter with `or vector(0)` (on 12.4.11 both counters appear with the first rule, the guard keeps the ratio defined wherever the failures counter is lazy). `target_versions` extended to 12.x. `rules/grafana.burn.yml` regenerated for the two changed ratio legs.
- **kafka** (`reference-packs/kafka.pack.yaml`): the JMX-side names follow the Strimzi rule set and were read from the live exposition — `produce_latency_p99` = `max(kafka_network_requestmetrics_totaltimems{request="Produce",quantile="0.99"}) / 1000` (what a producer waits for, replication included); `fetch_latency_p99` = `max(kafka_network_requestmetrics_localtimems{request="FetchConsumer",quantile="0.99"}) / 1000` (TotalTimeMs is the consumer's long-poll wait for the next record: p99 102 ms on the lab's 10 msg/s topic and `fetch.max.wait.ms` — 500 ms, seen as the 503 ms p999 — on an idle partition, both above the 50 ms objective with no fault); `controller_election_rate` = `max(rate(kafka_controller_kafkacontroller_newactivecontrollerscount[1h])) * 3600` (KRaft; the count is per controller node — every node counts each election it observes, `QuorumController.handleLeaderChange` — so `max`, never `sum`, which reads N per election on an N-controller quorum; the `ControllerStats` election meter is ZooKeeper-era and absent); `partition_replica_health` compares with `== bool` (the filter form summed ISR counts: 3 on a replication-factor-3 cluster, indistinguishable on the lab's RF 1; the burn rules already had the bool form); `per_topic_throughput` over `kafka_server_brokertopicmetrics_messagesin_total{topic!=""}` (the generator rates it by topic; the selector drops the broker-wide series, which has no topic label and rendered as a fourth topic `{}` equal to the sum of the others). Kafka request histograms come out of the JMX exporter as per-quantile gauges plus `_count_total`: no `_bucket` family exists for any request metric. `docs/catalogue-evidence/kafka.md` rewritten around the measured section (families, labels, provenance, what was wrong). `rules/kafka.burn.yml` unchanged: threshold SLIs are read through their recording rules.
- **Dashboards** (`tools/lib/dashboards/generic.mjs`): the §10 "Validation — MTTD, MTTR and certification" row was emitted for every pack with chaos experiments while its tiles select `{job="certification", pack="<name>"}`, so on the three reference packs the whole row was empty. It is now rendered only when the pack declares a scrape job named `certification` (the MQ pack does); a pack with chaos experiments and no feed gets the row header and one text panel saying exactly that. The pack matcher in `certSel` stays. The three unified boards regenerated (uids unchanged); `reference-packs/dashboards/README.md` documents the rule. `derivedViewPanel` accepts a label selector on a counter (`x_total{…}` is still rated, unit ops). `tools/test-gen-pack.mjs` pins the certification branch — a pack with a `certification` job gets the 14 tiles and only them, the committed packs the note, `certification-x` is not the feed — and the derived-view shapes.
- **Burn-rate generator** (`tools/lib/burn-rules.mjs`): the "add `or vector(0)` to the subtracted leg" warning no longer fires for a leg that already carries the guard (it did for the grafana alerting SLI above); the emitted rules are unchanged.

### Inventory coverage: is the right number of things being monitored?
The site inventory becomes something the monitor of the monitors can check against, in three moves.
- **The generic core stops calling instances queue managers** (`tools/lib/site/`). An inventory has hosts and *instances*; the module says what one is — `module.instances = { key, kind, label, title, schema }`: the collection key (the IBM MQ module keeps `queue_managers`), the kind name the expected sets and the inventory series use (`qmgr`, `broker`, `cluster` …), the live label, the title in messages, a schema fragment merged into `Instance` (required keys such as `shape` and its enum). The merged model and every environment carry `instances` plus the same array under the module's key; `ctx.instances`, `ctx.instanceKind`, `perInstance(ctx, instance)` (with `ctx.qmgrs` and `perQmgr` kept as legacy names). The MQ semantics that sat in the core (rdqm-ha hosts and address, `native_port` under dual + non-container, `client_port` per exporter host, `native_port` per host) move to `module.checkInventory`; the core keeps what every inventory shares. The schema's `queue_managers` becomes `instances` with `shape` a free string the module constrains. `tools/test-gen-site.mjs` 30/30; `docs/gen-site.md` updated.
- **Every partition publishes its expected sets** (`tools/lib/site/expected.mjs`): `site.json.expected` carries, per kind, the inventoried names (the instance kind always; `host` when the module lists it in `expectedKinds`, because hosts are observable only where a scrape job labels `up` with `host` — the MQ module does not), the recording series `<svc>:inventory:<kind>`, the scrape jobs whose `up` series carry the label (`module.expectedKinds`), and counted kinds with a PromQL count per parent and floors (queues per queue manager). The core emits `prometheus/rules/<name>.inventory.yml` — one `vector(1)` record per name — unless a module template already did (the MQ module does, with its Silent alert).
- **A journey checks it** (`tools/lib/inventory-coverage.mjs`, `tools/fetch-live-pack.mjs` `observeInventory`, `tools/lib/journey.mjs`): `inventory: { site, kinds? }` names the partition; the run asks the MCP's metrics query tool — resolved from the capability registry, gated on `tools/list` like the stack sampler — once per kind for `max by (<label>) (up{job=~…})` or the site's count query, and records `inventory: { status checked | partial | not-attempted | failed, reason, site, environment, checkedAt, kinds }` with per enumerated kind *up*, *down*, *silent*, *unexpected* and a coverage percentage, per counted kind the count per parent against the floors. A file-sourced Pack B is not-attempted (no live series), an MCP without the tool not-attempted with the tier reason, an unreadable site or one without an `expected` block failed with the reason, a `kinds` entry the site does not declare failed naming it (no wire call), and a kind whose query failed is `failed` with no numbers — a failed query is never an outage of every name. `gate.inventory`: `requireChecked` (default true), `maxSilent` / `maxDown` / `maxUnexpected` / `minCoveragePct` per enumerated kind, floors always, `kinds` to narrow. Surfaces: the markdown report's line and per-kind table, `packc journey list`'s `inventory 11/12 qmgr · 3/3 host · 40 queues` segment, `GET /api/journeys` `lastRun.inventory`, and Advanced → Neuron: an *inventory coverage* fleet tile (the largest kind as up / inventoried, the other kinds and counted totals, the unchecked journeys named), per-kind inventoried-vs-up lines per run for the journey in focus and the newest record's table. Coverage never touches the grade or the alignment. Suites: `tools/test-inventory-coverage.mjs`, `tools/test-journey.mjs` (a fake MCP with and without the metrics tool), `tools/test-neuron-model.mjs`.

### Advanced → Neuron: the monitor-of-monitors surface
The Journeys item under Advanced becomes **Neuron** — one page that reads every saved journey as one instrument, with the stats graphs the plain list never had. `studio/neuron-view.mjs` (loader `loadNeuronData` → `tools/lib/neuron-model.mjs` `buildNeuronModel`; renderer `renderNeuron(container, { data, ui }, host)`; dispatcher entry `renderNeuronView`): a toolbar (window of the newest 20 / 50 / 100 / 200 runs per journey, trend metric alignment · grade, the journey in focus, **run all** in sequence, refresh); nine fleet tiles (journeys with scheduled / notify / stack-gated / broken-definition counts, last outcomes with a pass · gate-failed · vantage-lost bar, fleet alignment and grade as the mean of each journey's last value with a *paired* delta against the run before, requirement chains intact / declared with the four ladder buckets and the scored vs ladder integrity means, journeys whose chains got worse, the widest exposure across the fleet, delivery sent / failed / skipped, journeys whose last run carried a nonzero lower-is-comfortable stack sample); six fleet panels — alignment or grade over time per journey (time axis when the records carry times, a gap where a vantage was lost), an outcome heatmap newest-right, breached criteria and candidate-cause kinds over every run in the window, **blind-spot exposure over time** (the SLOs the widest degraded artefact would blind, per journey) and **the widest exposures across the fleet** (the newest records' degraded nodes that would blind an SLO or an alert, as stacked bars SLOs · alerts · other consumers, with their journey); then the journey in focus (chosen, or `defaultFocus`: a chain getting worse, then gate-failed, then vantage-lost, then the lowest alignment, then the name) — alignment + grade per run, the ladder buckets per run as stacked bars in a neutral ramp, scored vs ladder integrity, run duration, **the blast radius of the newest record** (every degraded node of a declared chain once — nodes of undeclared chains are live-only inventory, as `chainSummary` treats them — with every chain it degrades, widest first: SLOs, alerts, total; one stacked bar per node, structural exposure and never a claim that anything is blind) with the widest node's exposure per run, one small step chart per stack self-metric row in a single ink colour with a ring on `nonzero` samples, a hollow marker where the probe did not answer and the posture-budget note under gated rows, and the newest record opened up: requirement chains with their degraded nodes and blast radius, the ranked candidate causes with the vantage beside them, the transition since the run before, the gate and its breaches, drift / grade / conformance / freshness, the stack evidence table with the Alertmanager and Grafana status, the vantage, backend versions, delivery (`null` = no notify block, absent = written before delivery), and the schedule with the cron / schtasks / GitHub Actions / CronJob snippets loaded on demand. The saved-journey cards (capture, run-now, chips, lines, history) close the page unchanged — `journeys-view.mjs` now exports `loadJourneysData`, `renderCaptureBar` and `renderJourneyCards` for it, and a card's run-now refreshes the whole neuron. Honesty rules kept from the records: a vantage-lost run is a gap, never 0; stack samples are signals (one colour, worded as such); ladder buckets are counts; causes say "not a verdict"; means are over the journeys that have the number and an empty set is `null`; the paired delta ignores a journey that only gained a first run. The view renders without a pack loaded; the `journeys` view id stays a route alias and a persisted `view: journeys` migrates to `neuron`. State: `neuronJourney`, `neuronWindow`, `neuronMetric` (persisted).
- **`tools/lib/svg-charts.mjs`** — zero-dependency SVG builders: `lineChart` (multi-series, gaps break the line and leave a hollow marker at the baseline, time axis or run order — `xMode` says which), `stackedBarChart`, `barChartH`, `stackedBarH`, `stepChart`, `niceTicks`, `timeTicks`, `fmtTime`, `legendHtml`, the Okabe–Ito `PALETTE`; colours are CSS variables with fallbacks so light / dark follow the theme, every text is escaped. **`tools/lib/neuron-model.mjs`** — the pure model (`buildNeuronModel({ journeys, runsByName, window })`, `buildJourneyDetail`, `metricPoints`, `ladderSeries`, `stackRowSeries`, `blastRadiusNodes`, `exposureSeries`, `defaultFocus`), browser-safe and served at `/lib`.
- **`GET /api/journeys/:name/schedule`** — the parsed `schedule:` and the four snippets, built with the same inputs and emitters as `packc journey schedule --json` (env var names only, never a value; `placeholder: true` and the placeholder cadence without a `schedule:`; 404 for an unknown or unloadable journey).
- Styles: the `.nrn-*` zone in `app.css` with the `--NRN` accent (light / dark); the fleet tiles reuse verdict-ui's `.mc-tile` atoms.
- Suites: `tools/test-neuron-model.mjs` (sorting, gaps, paired deltas, fleet counts, heatmap padding and window, breach / cause counts, focus order, notify null vs absent), `tools/test-svg-charts.mjs` (ticks, time labels, segment / gap-marker counts, axis mode, bar arithmetic, truncation, escaping); `server/test-smoke.mjs` covers the schedule route; `tools/test-studio-graph.mjs` links the new module.

### One dashboard engine: the compiler emits the generator's boards
`compileGrafanaDashboard` — the `grafana-dashboard` target, the studio's Dashboards group under Fix The Gaps, `packc compile … grafana-dashboard` — now builds its boards with `tools/lib/dashboards/generic.mjs`, the generator behind `gen-dashboards.mjs`, the committed reference-pack boards and the MQ lab's certified boards, instead of the thin per-binding timeseries panels it emitted before. Every pack gets the **Unified Observability** board `<name>-unified` (the whole pack in its own section order: SLI tiles and error-budget burn, MTTD / MTTR / certification and synthetic checks when declared, policy and alerting, remediation, derived views, pipelines, logs and traces), then one board per `spec.dashboards[]` entry as the generator shapes it (a `source:` entry gets what its `panel_bindings` declare, a `slo-burn-template` becomes a burn board, a `per-resource-template` a rollup); `checkBindings` refuses a `source:` board whose declared binding no panel honours — a pack error, never a thinner board (a `template:` board's bindings are not checked, as in the generator). On top of the generator the compiler keeps the platform contract: the Grafana version profile applied as a rewrite of the generated board (the `schemaVersion` floor per band unless the pack pins one; the bare-uid datasource form and no per-target datasource below Grafana 10; above it targets inherit the panel's datasource, the form the lab measured on Grafana 12.4), the datasource placeholders `${DS_PROMETHEUS}` / `${DS_LOKI}` / `${DS_TEMPO}` for a gateway to map — the in-repo `grafana-mcp-bridge.mjs` now maps them in dashboards as it always did in alert rules: Prometheus to its configured datasource, Loki / Tempo only when given a uid for that type, otherwise they reach Grafana as they are; `opts.datasourceUids` pins real uids at compile time and `compileArtifact` forwards it, `onWarning`, `module` and `repoUrl` to the dashboard compiler — and the tags `observability-pack`, `<service>`, `obs-pack-id:<id>`. **The uid is now the dashboard id** — the generator's contract, the boards link to each other at `/d/<id>`; capped the Grafana way, with a compile warning, only past 40 characters — instead of the hashed `obs-pack-<service>-<id>`: the deploy path's pre-state capture by `dashboardId` and the board it writes finally agree, and a board a live fetch reads back by uid carries the id the pack declares. A Grafana that already holds old-uid boards keeps them as separate dashboards until they are deleted. The compile catalog's Dashboards group now exists for every pack and lists the unified board first (`dash:<name>-unified`, flagged `generated`) when the pack does not declare it: compilable and downloadable, not deployable from the studio — deployed, a board the pack does not declare would come back as undeclared drift and never verify; declare it in `spec.dashboards[]` to deploy and verify it. The bundle starts with it. `compile(pack, 'grafana-dashboard')` without a dashboard id compiles the first declared board or, for a pack without `dashboards[]`, the unified board (it used to throw `dashboard not found: undefined`), and `packc compile <pack> grafana-dashboard <id>` forwards the id.
- Goldens: the ten `grafana-dashboard` goldens (payment-service and edge-hostile-names × Grafana 9.5 / 10 / 11 / 12 / 13) are now the generator's boards — 11 and 4 panels instead of 2 and 1, uid = id, five tags, `schemaVersion` 37 / 39 / 40 / 41 / 42 for the unpinned pack and the pinned 39 for payment-service on every band, a bare-uid datasource on 9.5. No other golden moved.
- Tests: `tools/test-gen-pack.mjs` pins the one-engine claim — with the lab's datasource uids (`prom` / `loki` / `tempo`) every committed `reference-packs/dashboards/*.json` equals the compiler's board (tags aside, which the compiler only adds), and without pinned uids the placeholders replace them; `tools/test-compile.mjs` asserts uid = id and the `obs-pack-id` tag, the generated catalog item, the bundle order, the id-less default on a pack without dashboards and the opts forwarding; `tools/test-profiles.mjs` reads the datasource form from the first panel that carries one (the boards open with a text header); `tools/test-backend-live.mjs` reads deployed dashboards back by uid (= id) instead of title.

## 0.4.0 — 2026-09-20

**The monitor-of-monitors release.** Observogram assures that the artefacts which monitor a system keep doing their job: the live fetcher stops asserting what it never observed (steps 0-1), the stack samples its own self-metrics through the MCP (step 2), journeys keep stack-health evidence per run (step 3), and the requirement graph shows what would go blind when an artefact dies, a per-node ladder and candidate causes (step 4). The pack generators (boards including a Unified Observability board for every pack, burn-rate rules with the live-measured PromQL, gen-site for a fleet of environments from one pack and an inventory) became one vendorable library and the compiler emits the same PromQL. Also: stage 2 tenancy, first-boot admin and account menu, Diagnose views on the behavioural engine, the Tomograph to Observogram rebrand, legacy pack format support.

### Blind-spot blast radius on the graph (step 4)
Step 3 kept the stack's own self-metrics per journey run; step 4 puts the **monitor-of-monitors question on the requirement graph itself**: what would go blind if an artefact died (structural exposure), whether a present artefact is doing its job or the vantage could not look at all (the per-node ladder), what changed since the previous run, and which candidate causes the evidence can offer. Three things are true of everything in this block, by contract and pinned by the suites: **nothing scored changed** — `branch.integrity` / `verdict` / `counts`, every node `status`, `rollup.integrityMean` and the grade's Drift-free criterion are byte-identical with and without the new annotations, everything new is an additive field beside them, and the switch to the ladder is a proposal (`docs/SCORING_PROPOSAL_LADDER_INTEGRITY.md`), not applied; **the honesty vocabulary is kept** — a blast radius says what WOULD go blind, never that something IS blind, `unobserved` means the vantage could not look (a probe family failed or is not exposed by the tier), never "absent", and `present_unhealthy` means on the wire but not doing its job; and **causes are candidate causes ranked by evidence — not a root-cause verdict**, with a change of the vantage itself reported separately and never as a cause. No new MCP tool name, no new dependency, no build step; the two new reasoning modules are zero-import and vendorable.
- **Blast radius (reverse index).** New zero-import `tools/lib/blast-radius.mjs`: `CONSUMER_SIDE` (per edge type, which side consumes the other's assurance — `from` for `sli_of`, `sources`, `exported_by`, `produced_by`, `protects`, `forecasts`, `visualises`, `contains`, `routes`, `remediates`, `validates`; `to` for `materialises`, where the SLI consumes the rule's series), `PROTECTION_SIDE` (the `to` side loses when the `from` side dies: an SLO loses protection with its alert, an alert loses delivery with its route and remediation with its remediation — transitive, a dead route means an undelivered alert means an unprotected SLO), `normalizeGraphShape` (nodes as an array, a Map or a keyed object; edges without `from` / `to` / `type` dropped; edge types outside the table carry no known direction and are ignored), `blastRadiusOf(shape, key, { weights })` → `{ key, kind, label, blinded: { total, weight, byKind, nodes: [{ key, kind, label, hop }] }, unprotected: { slos, alerts }, summary: { slos, alerts, panels, dashboards, routes, remediations, total } }` (`null` for an unknown key; breadth-first over the reverse adjacency, cycle-safe; `blinded.nodes` sorted hop → `KIND_ORDER` → label and capped at `BLINDED_NODES_CAP` = 64 while `total` / `byKind` stay uncapped; protection losers already blinded are not listed twice; `weight` sums `DEFAULT_WEIGHTS`, a copy of the graph's limb weights, unknown kind 0.5) and `blastRadiusIndex(shape)` → `Map key → summary` in sorted key order. Scaffold placeholders (a `scaffold` flag or `artefact.source === 'Scaffold'`) never blind anything, are never traversed and are never listed. `traceability-graph.mjs` exports `graphShape(graph)` — the artefact-free projection `{ nodes: [{ key, identityKey, kind, layer, label, virtual, scaffold }], edges: [{ key, from, to, type, provenance }] }` — and `compareBranches` computes the A-graph and B-graph indexes once per comparison, attaching `blastRadius: summary | null` to every node verdict (A's index for aligned / drifted / declared-only / unverifiable nodes, B's for live-only ones).
- **Per-node ladder (additive, unscored) + the scoring proposal.** Every node verdict gains `ladder: { rung, status, detail }` — rungs `unobserved < absent < exists < alive < healthy`, status `present_unhealthy` | `present_stale` | `unobserved` | `null` — read from what the live fetcher wrote into Pack B: `mcp.observed.scrape_targets` / `recording_rules` / `alert_rules`, the `mcp.discovered.scrape_jobs_down` / `recording_rules_unhealthy` / `alert_rules_unhealthy` / `slis_unhealthy` lists, `mcp.probesFailed` / `mcp.probesUnsupported` with `mcp.probeErrors.<family>`, `mcp.versions.<product>` for backends and `mcp.refreshedAt` as "now" (a timestamp older than 2× the artefact's interval reads `present_stale`; no interval or no timestamp means staleness is not judged). Burn-rate alerts link to live alert rules by the compiler's `<slo>_burn_<factor>x_<short>_<long>` convention, narrowed to the declared windows. A `declared_only` artefact the fetcher observed but withheld from Pack B (a scrape job whose every target is down) reads `present_unhealthy` — on the wire beats absent; a kind whose probe family failed or is not exposed reads `unobserved`, never absent; an `unverifiable` kind reads rung `unobserved` with status `null`; without any `mcp.` annotation every present node is `exists`. Per branch: `ladderVerdict` (`broken` > `degraded` > `unobserved` > `healthy`; `undeclared` mirrors the scored fields) and `ladderIntegrity` / `ladderIntegrityPct` on the same weights — unhealthy / stale credit 0.25, absent 0, unobserved nodes leave the denominator; `rollup.ladder = { healthy, degraded, broken, unobserved, integrityMean, integrityPct }` over the declared branches. `docs/TRACEABILITY_GRAPH_COMPARISON_SPEC.md` §5b carries the table and evidence rules; `docs/SCORING_PROPOSAL_LADDER_INTEGRITY.md` proposes that Drift-free read `rollup.ladder.integrityMean` under `gradeSchema` 3, with the four worked rows on the test fixture — a proposal through the PHASE_1 acceptance path, **not applied**.
- **Per-run persistence: chains, versions, transition, live pack.** New zero-import `tools/lib/chain-history.mjs`: `branchRecordsFromGraph(traceabilityGraph)` keeps per branch `{ rootKey, title, rootKind, verdict, ladderVerdict, integrityPct, ladderIntegrityPct, confidence, missingRoles, degraded, truncated? }` with the degraded nodes (`declared_only` / `drifted` / `live_only`, or ladder status `present_unhealthy` / `present_stale` / `unobserved`; aligned-and-healthy and `unverifiable` nodes are not recorded) worst first — absent, unhealthy, stale, drifted, unobserved, live-only, then widest blast radius — as `{ key, kind, label, status, ladder, blastRadius: summary, deltaFields }` (caps 64 branches × 16 nodes × 6 delta fields, a cut list marked `truncated`); `chainSummary(record)` → `{ declaredTotal, intact, partial, broken, undeclared, ladder: { healthy, degraded, broken, unobserved }, integrityPct, ladderIntegrityPct, degradedNodes, topExposure: { label, kind, slos, alerts } | null }` (means are `null` with no declared chain — an empty set is not 100 % healthy; `null` when the record carries no `branches`); `diffRunBranches(previous, current)` → `{ since, changed: [{ rootKey, title, from: { verdict, ladderVerdict }, to, direction, nodes: { newlyDegraded, recovered } }], appeared, disappeared, any }` with `direction` from the rank tables `intact < partial < broken` and `healthy < degraded < unobserved < broken` (`worse` when nothing improved and something got worse, `better` the mirror, `changed` otherwise or when either side is `undeclared` / unknown; `null` when either record carries no chains). The run record gains `branches`, `chains`, `versions` (`mcp.versions.<product>` → value, bare product keys only, `null` when none — an absence, never "unchanged"), `transition` (against the newest record read *before* this run is written; `null` on the first run or after a record without chains, e.g. a vantage loss) and `livePack: { kept, path, bytes?, reason }`. The definition key `keepLivePack: transitions | always | never` (validated by `loadJourneyDef`; default `transitions`) decides whether Pack B's canonical JSON is written to `runs/<journey>/live/<record stem>.json` beside the record: `transitions` keeps the first run, a run whose chains moved, a gate failure, the run after a vantage loss and the run after a record that cannot be compared; a file-sourced Pack B is never snapshotted (the file is the snapshot), whatever the policy. The pure `livePackDecision` is exported; a snapshot write failure lands on the record as `historyError`, never thrown. Retention is unchanged (`JOURNEY_RUN_FILE_RE` untouched, `readJourneyRuns` keeps ignoring `live/`) and now also removes orphaned snapshots — after the record prune, `pruneLiveSnapshots(recordFiles, liveFiles)` (pure) names the run-shaped `live/` files whose record is gone, never a survivor's, never a non-run file; `readLivePack(name, record)` reads a snapshot back (`null` when none was kept, the path is not the `live/<stem>.json` shape, or the file is gone — a pruned snapshot reads as absent, never as an error). `renderJourneyMarkdown` adds a `Requirement chains` table (`chain | verdict | ladder | integrity | ladder integrity | worst node`, cap 24, only when the record carries chains — no table means none were declared or recorded, never that every chain is intact; the worst-node cell carries the ladder detail and `blinds N SLOs`) and a `Transitions since previous run` section (`title — intact/healthy → broken/broken (worse); newly degraded: …; recovered: …`, `appeared` / `disappeared`, then the `live pack: kept (…) — reason` / `not kept — reason` line).
- **Candidate-cause ranker.** `chain-history.mjs` gains `rankCauses({ previous, current, deploys })` → `{ transitions, causes: [{ rank, kind, score, evidence, chains, nodes }], vantage: { changed, from, to, detail } | null, note }` with `note` = `candidate causes ranked by evidence — not a root-cause verdict`. Only chains whose transition reads `worse` (plus chains that appeared already partial / broken / degraded) are considered, and only their nodes that moved (new on the current side, or the same identity with a different status / ladder status; every degraded node when the record cannot say); nodes whose ladder status is `unobserved` are dropped last, so a chain whose only movement is the vantage looking away yields no cause. Four kinds, scored by fixed `CAUSE_SCORES` so a rank is explainable by reading the table: `observogram-deploy` — a deploy inside the window whose item `artifact` names a moved node 0.9 (an artefact id on the node — `aId` / `bId` — matches exactly, otherwise the artefact must be contained in the node's label or key case-insensitively with a `dash:` prefix dropped; only that direction, so a rule deploy cannot blame every SLI whose short name it embeds; stubs under three characters and the wildcard `all` name nothing; the persisted degraded-node record carries no ids today, so on a real run the match is by label / key), else a deploy that wrote the journey's pack (by `pack.id` / `pack.name` against the record's `packA`) 0.6; `config-drift` — a moved node that drifted on a decision-bearing field 0.8, on cosmetic or unrecorded fields 0.4; `backend-version` — a product both records reported with a different version, 0.6 when a moved node is a backend / metric / recording rule / burn-rate alert, 0.3 otherwise; `stack-self-metric` — a current-record stack row that answered data and reads as a signal (`nonzero` hint, `lower` above 0, or a `higher` ratio below 1) in the family feeding a moved node's kind per `FAMILY_FOR_KIND` (scrape → `scrape_job`; ruler → `recording_rule` / `burn_rate` / `sli` / `slo`; notify → `alert_route`; tsdb → `backend` / `storage_metrics`; collector → `pipeline_*` / `otel`; dashboards → `panel` / `dashboard`; synthetic → `synthetic`) 0.5. One cause per distinct evidence, aggregating every chain and node it explains; ordering score desc → kind order → evidence; a verify-type record or a dry run is skipped; malformed input never throws. `deploysInWindow(deploys, since, until)` windows Observogram's own audit lines to `(previous.startedAt, current.startedAt]` on `at` and merges the latest verify onto its deploy so the evidence can name the outcome (`deploy dep_x by carlos at … (upsert) touched <artifact>; verify: pending`); `journey.mjs` reads `deploys.jsonl` beside `runs/` under the same workspace root without importing server code (missing file → nothing, a torn line skipped). `vantage` reports what changed about the vantage itself — the vantage word, a probe family that newly failed (with its error), is no longer exposed, no longer probed, answers again or is now exposed, and the exposed tool count — beside the causes, never among them. The record carries `causes` (`null` on the first run: nothing to explain yet); `topCause(record)` reads the rank-1 cause. Markdown: `### Candidate causes — ranked by evidence, not a root-cause verdict` (`_no previous run_` / `_no chain got worse since …_`, `N. [kind] score — evidence (chains: titles)`, then `vantage changed: …` / `vantage: unchanged`).
- **Surfaces.** `packc journey list` appends `chainStatusLine(record)` after the stack segment — `chains 8/10 intact · ladder 7 healthy · 2 degraded` (zero ladder buckets other than healthy omitted; `chains none` for a record without declared chains) — and, only when a chain got worse or a cause was ranked (`transitionGotWorse`), `causeLine(record)` (`top cause: [kind] evidence` / `no candidate causes`); a vantage-lost line carries neither. `GET /api/journeys` `lastRun` gains `chains` (`chainSummary(lastRun)`, `null` without chains), `transition: { any, changed, worse } | null` (counts, not the full diff), `topCause` (the rank-1 cause object or `null`) and `vantageChanged` (`true` / `false` / `null` without a causes block); `GET /api/journeys/:name/runs` hands the record through unchanged. The Journeys view prints one plain-text chains line per card — `requirement chains: N/M intact · ladder: h healthy · d degraded · b broken · u unobserved · top exposure: <label> (<kind>) blinds N SLOs` with a muted `changed since previous run (W worse)` marker — and one cause line — `candidate cause: [kind] evidence — not a verdict` with a muted `vantage changed` marker; counts and markers, no colours, no new CSS. In the Diagnose chain cards (`compare-view.mjs`) `evidenceFor` names `present but unhealthy`, `present but stale` and `unobserved — <detail>` nodes (the ladder reading replaces `missing live` / `aligned` for that node) and appends `· blinds N SLO(s)` to `declared_only` / `drifted` nodes with `blastRadius.slos > 0`; the card meta shows `ladder: <ladderVerdict>` and the section rollup appends `· ladder NN%`.
- **Suites.** New `tools/test-blast-radius.mjs` (`npm run test:blast-radius`, 58 assertions — the vendoring guard, the tables, hop distances along the full chain, the protection relation, scaffold exclusion, cycle safety, determinism, the listing cap, the accepted input shapes, no input mutation) and `tools/test-chain-history.mjs` (`npm run test:chain-history`, 122 assertions — the guard, the tables and caps, which nodes are recorded and in what order, the truncated markers, the summary and its top exposure, transition directions, appeared / disappeared and node lists, the deploy window and verify merge, every cause kind and score, the one-way artefact match, the moved-node rule, the vantage block including a vantage-lost previous record and file-sourced sides, determinism, malformed input); both in the aggregate (34 suites). `tools/test-traceability-graph.mjs` adds the `graphShape` projection, the `blastRadius` pins per node kind, the ladder block (healthy / unhealthy / stale rules, an unhealthy burn rule linked by name, a down target, unsupported and failed probe families, absent for real, withheld from Pack B) and the byte-identity pin on `integrity` / `verdict` / `counts` / node `status` / `rollup.integrityMean` with and without the annotations. `tools/test-journey.mjs` drives a fake MCP through the snapshot policy (every branch of `livePackDecision`, the files under `live/`, pruning, `readLivePack`), the record shape and its round-trip through the history file, the markdown sections, the CLI lines and the ranker against a seeded `deploys.jsonl` (a deploy inside the window, one outside, a torn line, the vantage block). `server/test-smoke.mjs` seeds a record with `branches` and `causes` and pins the `lastRun` shape.
- **Review corrections (step 4).** Two fix passes after the step-4 reviews; nothing scored changed on either side (`integrity`, `verdict`, `counts`, node `status`, `rollup.integrityMean`, the grade — byte-identical, still pinned). *Graph side.* The fetcher stamps its own clock: `mcp.fetchStartedAt` (the instant the fetch began) and `mcp.observedAt.<family>` (the instant each probe family answered, data or empty; `dashboards` re-stamped after detail enrichment), and the ladder's "now" is the family's `observedAt`, else `fetchStartedAt`, else the caller's `mcp.refreshedAt` (older packs — named in the detail only on that fallback), so a `refreshedAt` trailing a fresh observation by a minute no longer reads stale. A scrape job is `present_unhealthy` only when EVERY target is down (the fetcher's `scrape_jobs_down` rule); some targets down reads `alive` with `n/m targets down: <instances>`, and a down target's `lastError` is not the job's. Fractional burn factors (`_burn_14_4x_` ↔ 14.4) parse in the graph and the fetcher; alert observation entries carry `interval` (the group evaluation interval — the alert adapter keeps it now, and burn-rate freshness uses the longest linked one) and `labels: { slo, burn_rate, window_short, window_long }` (present keys only, omitted when none), and the ladder links a live rule to its declared window by labels first, name second; the `alert_rules_unhealthy` list is narrowed to the declared windows like the rule set and the detail names the matched / failing rule(s). An unknown interval reads `healthy` / `alive` only within a 1 h ceiling ("interval unknown; 1 h ceiling applied"), older reads `present_stale`; a timestamp more than 1 s past "now" reads `alive` with "timestamp <n>s in the future (clock skew); freshness not judged", never `healthy`; a reported health of `unknown` stays `present_unhealthy`; `ladderVerdict` ignores `live_only` nodes as the scored verdict does; "no fetch timestamp on the wire (mcp.refreshedAt missing); staleness not judged" when the observation has a timestamp and the pack has no clock. `compare-view`'s `evidenceFor` orders the evidence line before the 5-cap — declared_only load-bearing, present_unhealthy, present_stale, drifted, declared_only (other), unverifiable, unobserved, live_only — and a `declared_only` node whose live twin sits under another chain reads "on the wire and in Pack B under another chain: …" (or "in Pack B under another chain; not reachable from this branch"); "withheld from Pack B" only when no twin exists. The golden `alert_rules.json` gains the two `interval` lines; the largest example pair's payload grows +88 % (spec §5c). *Journey side.* **Deploy attribution is exact.** `runJourney` resolves every windowed deploy item's artifact selector against Pack A (`resolveDeployArtifact`, exported: `declared:<i>` → the i-th declared recording rule's name, `slo:<id>` → the SLO id, its SLI base and the SLI the pack binds it to, `dash:<id>` → the id, a bare name → itself, `all` → nothing; the item carries `resolved` beside `artifact`) and the ranker matches a resolved name exactly — against the node's `aId` / `bId` (now persisted on every degraded node), its label, or an identity handle of its `kind::{json}` key (`id`, `name`, `record`, `slo`, `job`, `uid`) — never by substring, and only when the item's group can write the node's kind (`DEPLOY_GROUP_KINDS`: dashboards / restore / delete → dashboard, panel; rules → recording_rule, burn_rate, sli, slo; alerts → burn_rate, alert_route; alertmanager → alert_route; pipelines → pipeline_*, otel; an unknown group constrains nothing); the `< 3` stub rule is gone; the evidence reads `touched declared:0 → payment:api_availability:ratio_5m` and, for a wildcard-only deploy of the journey's pack, `wrote pack X (all rules) — no item names a moved artefact` (0.6 — needs the audit line's `pack.id`, the registry id = the pack file's stem, or `pack.name` to equal the record's `packA.name`). Without a caller's resolution the ranker knows the pack-free part (`deployArtifactNames`). **Versions.** A flip to or from the literal `live` (the fetcher's word for "answered without a number") is a vantage matter — reported in `vantage.detail` as `mcp.versions.<product> changed to/from live`, never a cause — and `backend-version` scores 0.6 (`versionFeedsMovedKind`) only when the changed product feeds a moved node's family (`PRODUCT_FAMILIES`: prometheus / victoriametrics / thanos / mimir → scrape, ruler, tsdb; grafana → dashboards; alertmanager → notify; otel, otelcol → collector; loki, promtail → logs; jaeger, tempo → traces; unknown → none), else 0.3 — a Grafana-only change is no longer 0.6 on a Prometheus rule. **Baseline.** The chain diff, the versions and the deploy window are compared against the newest record that CARRIES chains (`BASELINE_SCAN_LIMIT` 25 back), the vantage against the immediately previous record (`rankCauses` gains `baseline`); `transition` is never null on a new record: `{ reason, since: null, changed: [], appeared: [], disappeared: [], any: false, skipped }` with `reason` `first run` | `previous run lost its vantage` | `previous runs carry no chain record` when nothing could be compared, else the diff with `reason: null` and `skipped: [{ startedAt, outcome }]` naming the records passed over (cap 8); the report says "previous run <ts> lost its vantage — comparing against <baseline ts>" in both sections and never "no previous run" when one exists. **No broadening.** A node whose status and ladder status are identical on both sides is never blamed; when nothing recorded moved, the changed transition entry carries `note` — `declared side: missingRoles none → action`, `degraded list truncated (cap 16) — a node that moved may be unrecorded` — and the chain yields no cause. **Escaping.** Every value the transitions, causes and breaches sections interpolate goes through `mdCell` (line breaks collapsed, `|` escaped, a line-leading `#` / `-` / `*` / `+` / `>` / `1.` neutralised) and the ranker's evidence collapses whitespace; a label carrying `\n\n### Gate breaches` cannot forge a section. **Also:** `readDeployLog` (exported) reads only the trailing 8 MB of `deploys.jsonl` (`DEPLOY_LOG_TAIL_BYTES`, the cut first line dropped); `readJourneyRuns` reads only run-shaped files (a stray `notes.json` is never the previous run); an unparseable baseline start gives an EMPTY deploy window, never all of history; a snapshot write failure reads `livePack: { kept: false, reason: 'snapshot write failed: …' }` beside `historyError`; `packc journey list` appends `vantage changed: <detail>` (`vantageLine`) whenever the vantage block says so, with or without a top cause; `pruneLiveSnapshots` names only run-shaped `live/` files OLDER than the oldest surviving record (retention victims), never a newer stem whose record a concurrent writer has not landed yet; `stackSignalRows` reads direction + value only, never the stored `nonzero` hint; the `causes` block no longer carries a second copy of the transition (`{ causes, vantage, note }`); `chainStatusLine` distinguishes `chains none (pre-step-4 record)` / `chains none (vantage lost)` / `chains 0 declared`; `keepLivePack: never` writes none from now on (earlier snapshots age out with their records); `rankCauses` accepts only `type: 'deploy'` (as `deploysInWindow` did). `cause.chains` carries the branch titles and `rootKeys` the identity keys; `chainSummary.degradedNodes` / `topExposure` count and rank the declared chains only, and `undeclaredNodes` counts the live-only nodes of undeclared chains — the Journeys view appends `N live-only in undeclared chains` only when > 0. Pinned: the server's real selector strings (`declared:0`, `slo:checkout`, `all`, a rollback ref under `restore`), `dash:payment` blaming neither `scrape_job payment` nor the rule embedding it, a three-letter dashboard id, wrong groups, `branchRecordsFromGraph` output satisfying the id match, the `live` flips, a Grafana-only change on a Prometheus rule, the stale hint, missing-role / truncation notes, baseline vs previous, the wording after a vantage-lost run (and with no earlier chains, and after a pre-step-4 record), `notes.json`, the 8 MB tail with a cut first line, the empty window, markdown forgery through labels / actor / evidence / breaches / vantage / live-pack reason, the CLI vantage segment, the smoke seed made consistent and the `vantageChanged: null` API case; the fake-MCP e2e deploy now carries `declared:0` end to end.

### Stack-health evidence per journey run (step 3)
Step 2 sampled the observability stack's own self-metrics on every live fetch as point-in-time signals; step 3 turns those samples into **evidence over time** by keeping them per journey run, so the run history becomes the time series the journeys surface reads. Nothing in this block creates a `Verified` stamp, touches the grade, or turns a sample into an SLO verdict.
- **Run-history retention (prerequisite).** A journey on a cron cadence used to grow `runs/<journey>/` without bound. `writeRunRecord` now prunes the directory to the newest `OBSERVOGRAM_JOURNEY_RUN_RETENTION` files after every write (default `1000`; `0` = unlimited; a value that is not a non-negative integer falls back to the default; legacy `TOMOGRAPH_*` spelling honoured via `brandEnv`). The policy is the pure, exported `pruneRunFiles(files, keep)` (names to delete, oldest first — filenames are ISO start times so lexical order is chronological; non-`.json` entries are never candidates) plus `parseRunRetention` / `journeyRunRetention()` (read at write time). A deletion failure lands on the record as `historyError`, never thrown; `readJourneyRuns` is unchanged. Scheduling stays external by decision (VALUE_BACKLOG item 11).
- **`stackEvidence` on the run record.** `liveEvidenceFacts(canonicalB)` parses `mcp.observed.stack_metrics`, `mcp.observed.alertmanager` and `mcp.observed.grafana.*` into `stackEvidence = { status, reason, rows: [{ id, family, product, value, unit, direction, outcome, hint, at, referenceSli, reason? }], alertmanager: { version, clusterStatus, silencesActive, error } | null, grafana: { datasources, unhealthyDatasources, contactPoints, error } | null }` — `null` when Pack B carries no `mcp.stack.status` (a file-sourced B is honest absence), rows enriched with the contracts table's `referenceSli` and display-only `hint` (a row the table no longer declares keeps `referenceSli: null`), `expr` dropped, cap 64, malformed JSON degrading to no rows / `null` surfaces rather than a fabricated panel. The step-2 `stack` counts block is unchanged for compatibility. Suite: `tools/test-journey.mjs` (retention policy + effect, env parsing, evidence from a fixture-built annotated Pack B, null for file-sourced B, retired row ids, malformed JSON, advertised-but-failing surfaces, round-trip through history).

- **Gate key `stack` — thresholds on the samples.** `gate.stack: { requireSampled?, rows?: { <rowId>: { min?, max? } } }` turns the samples into an early warning for a business owner, never an SLO verdict: `requireSampled` breaches `stack` when `stackEvidence` is `null` (`Pack B is not a live draft`), the panel was `not-attempted` (with the reason), or no row answered `data`; `rows.<id>` breaches `stack.<id>` with `no sample for <id> (<outcome>[: reason]) — threshold cannot be checked` when the row is absent or did not answer data (a file-sourced B with a declared threshold breaches honestly instead of passing by absence), and with `<id> = <value> <unit> outside [min … max] — point-in-time sample, not an SLO verdict` when the number falls outside the band (equality passes; an `info` row may carry a threshold). `loadJourneyDef` validates the block through the exported `validateGateStack(stack, name)` — unknown row ids (case-sensitive against `STACK_SELF_METRIC_PROBES`) throw with the known ids listed, `min` / `max` must be finite numbers, an entry with neither (or `min > max`, or a non-boolean `requireSampled`) is refused. Pure `formatStackValue(value, unit)` renders `83.3%` / `0.004/s` / `0.0/h` / `7.4s` / `1`. `renderJourneyMarkdown` adds a `Stack self-metrics — point-in-time samples` table (`id | family | value unit | outcome | hint | reference SLI`, cap 24, only when rows exist) and lists stack breaches with the others; `packc journey list` appends `stackStatusLine(record)` (`stack sampled N` / `stack not attempted` / `stack none`). The capture default gate and every existing key are unchanged; nothing touches the grade. Pinned in `tools/test-journey.mjs` (every branch of the gate, the validation errors, the formatter, the table and cap, the CLI line).
- **History helpers, listing summary and the journeys chips.** New browser-safe `tools/lib/stack-evidence.mjs` (imports only the contracts table; vendorable, listed in `docs/VENDORING.md`): `stackSeries(runs, rowId)` (oldest → newest per row, gaps skipped never interpolated, non-data outcomes kept with `value: null`), `latestByFamily(record)` (the data row per family, ties by table order, retired rows last), `stackSummary(record)` (`{ status, reason, sampled, families }` or `null` without evidence), `nonzeroRuns(series)`, `stackPostureBudget(series, { objective, cadenceMs, windowMs, isBad? })` (the cadence heuristic — a sampled posture is only `measurable` when the window allows ≥ 10 bad samples; every note reads "signal, not verdict") and the shared `formatStackValue` / `stackOutcomeLabel` (journey.mjs now re-exports the formatter from here — one vocabulary for CLI, report and studio). `GET /api/journeys` adds `lastRun.stack = stackSummary(lastRun)` (`null` for a file-sourced B); the Journeys view loads the module from `/lib/stack-evidence.mjs` at call time and renders a `stack self-metrics — point-in-time samples` line per card — one chip per family, the `nonzero` hint as a muted marker, id + reference SLI in the title, `nonzero in N of last M runs` for lower-is-comfortable rows over the fetched history, one muted chip with the reason when not attempted; no chip carries an ok/error colour. New suite `tools/test-stack-evidence.mjs` (`npm run test:stack-evidence`, in the aggregate); `server/test-smoke.mjs` seeds a record with `stackEvidence` and checks the listing shape.
- **Docs reconciled with the branch.** README's journeys section carries the gate example with the `stack:` block, the retention env var and the studio chips, and its API table / repository map now list the journey routes and modules (`tools/lib/journey.mjs`, `tools/lib/stack-evidence.mjs`, `studio/journeys-view.mjs`); `docs/MCP_INTEGRATION.md`'s journey section documents the record fields, the gate keys with their breach table and honesty rules, retention, and the history helpers with the cadence heuristic; `docs/VENDORING.md` lists `stack-evidence.mjs` + the contracts table as a vendorable pair; `docs/VALUE_BACKLOG.md` item 11 gains a status note (drift over time now includes the stack samples; scheduling stays external).
- **Review corrections (step 3).** Reproduced against a seeded workspace and the fake MCP: `latestByFamily` ranked data rows by contracts-table order alone, so whenever a family's first table row is `higher` / `info` (`scrape_success_ratio`, `tsdb_active_series`) and answered data, the family's lower-is-better rows — and with them the `nonzero` marker and the `nonzero in N of last M runs` history the chips exist for — never reached the surface: the card read `scrape 95.0%` while the run breached on `scrape_targets_down`. Data rows now rank by `nonzero` hint first, then declared-in-table before retired, then `lower` before `higher` / `info`, then table order (`tools/test-stack-evidence.mjs` pins each rung; the old "ties by table order" assertion pinned the hiding). Also from the review: a `rows.<id>` threshold on a row the fetcher never wrote names why — `(not-attempted: <tier reason>)` on a not-attempted panel, `(not attempted by the sampler — call budget exhausted or row not observed)` on a sampled one — instead of a bare "absent from this run" that read like a fetch hole; `evaluateStackGate` no longer trusts an unvalidated gate object (a bound that is not a finite number, an entry with neither bound, `min > max` or a non-mapping entry breaches `stack.<id>` as `threshold invalid (…) — cannot be checked`, never passes by silence); a breach whose display rounding equals its bound appends the raw number (`0.000/s (raw 0.0004)`); an outcome the contracts do not declare is kept verbatim (missing → `unknown`) rather than relabelled `failed`; `POST /api/journeys/capture` runs `validateGateStack` before saving (400) and `GET /api/journeys` / `packc journey list` / the studio card carry `loadError` for a definition that cannot load; `pruneRunFiles` only treats the run-record filename shape (`JOURNEY_RUN_FILE_RE`) as a run, so a hand-dropped `notes.json` neither displaces a record nor is deleted, and the deletion-failure branch (`historyError`, never thrown) is now under test. README and `docs/MCP_INTEGRATION.md` reworded accordingly (`requireSampled`'s third branch, "the only *gate* reader", the raw-number fallback of the chips without the helper module).

### Stack self-metrics (step 2)
Observogram monitors the artefacts that monitor the system; step 2 of the roadmap starts **acquiring the observability stack's own health signals** through the MCP so later steps can build stack-health evidence and early warning. Three things are true of everything in this block, by contract and pinned by the suites: **every sampled number is a point-in-time signal, not a verdict** — no `Verified` stamp, no `Scaffold` marker, no SLO verdict and no grade criterion or grade input changed anywhere; **a restricted MCP tier reads `not-attempted` with the reason**, never "absent"; and **the alias table reads only names a real product exposed** — it was documentation-grounded when written (every metric name following the product's upstream docs, named on the row) and now carries three live evidence sources: the pinned Docker stack (`npm run test:stack:live`, every alias stamped with the image it was verified on) and the public (2026-09-07) and authenticated (2026-09-08) Krystaline tiers (`npm run record-fixtures`); a product a given stack does not run reads an honest `not-in-inventory`.
- **Alias table (contracts).** New data-only `tools/lib/contracts/stack-self-metrics.mjs`: `STACK_SELF_METRIC_PROBES` — 24 rows over nine families (scrape, ruler, notify, tsdb, collector, dashboards, synthetic, logs, traces), each with its signal, unit, display-only direction, the reference-pack SLI whose vocabulary it follows, ordered product aliases (`{ product, expr, requires }`) and the upstream docs the names come from — plus lookup resolvers (`probeRows`, `rowsForFamily`, `eligibleAliases`, `productPreferenceOrder`, `displayHint`, `bestOutcome`) and the allowed sets (`STACK_FAMILIES`, `STACK_UNITS`, `STACK_DIRECTIONS`, `STACK_OUTCOMES`). One known discrepancy is documented on the row rather than fixed: the reference pack's `scrape_duration_p99` names `scrape_duration_seconds_bucket`, which Prometheus never exposes (the metric is a per-target gauge), so `scrape_duration_max` samples `max(scrape_duration_seconds)`.
- **Registry rows + shapes.** `mcp-capabilities.mjs` gains `stack_self_metrics` (carrier for the table, rides `metrics_query`), `alertmanager_status`, `alertmanager_silences`, `grafana_datasources`, `grafana_datasource_health` (enrich) and `grafana_contact_points`; `build_info_versions` now declares its `instant-vector` shape. `response-shapes.mjs` adds `instant-vector` (`{ result }` and `{ data: { result } }`), `silences`, `datasources`, `contact-points`, and a new **object** shape kind (`object: true`, `objectAt`, `anyOfKeys`) for `status-object` and `health-object` — `validateResponseShape` locates the first candidate object carrying every key group, tolerant of extras and of a `{ data: {…} }` envelope. Five tool names join the pinned wire surface.
- **Sampler (fetcher).** `tools/fetch-live-pack.mjs` exports `sampleStackSelfMetrics({ callTool, quiet, metricsQueryTool, inventory, seenProducts, discoveredToolNames, hasToolsList, refreshedAt, maxCalls })` → `{ status, reason, rows, callsMade }`, run by `fetchMcp` after the version probes. Policy: attempt only when `metrics_query` is advertised (a server with no `tools/list` is attempted), otherwise `not-attempted` with the reason `metrics_query not exposed by this MCP (restricted tier)` and zero calls; with a `metric_names` inventory an alias is eligible only when every `requires` name is present (rows with none are `not-in-inventory`, no call); eligible aliases ordered generic-first then products already seen; at most 2 calls per row, 48 per panel (`call budget exhausted` beyond it); every call through `quiet()` under the family `stack_self_metrics`; instant vectors parsed from `{ result }` or `{ data: { result } }` by `sampleFromInstantVector` (exported — the recorder reads answers the same way), `NaN`/`±Inf`/missing samples are `empty`. Outcomes are exactly `data | empty | failed | not-in-inventory | not-attempted` — never "ok".
- **Alertmanager / Grafana status rows.** `observeAlertmanager` (`alertmanager_status` → version / uptime / clusterStatus; `alertmanager_silences` → active + total) and `observeGrafana` (`grafana_datasources` → `[{uid, name, type}]`, `grafana_datasource_health` per uid ≤ 10 → `ok | error | unknown` + a message trimmed to 200 chars, `grafana_contact_points` → count + ≤ 32 names), each tool guarded by `tools/list` when present, tolerant parsing through the response shapes. `fetchMcp` returns `stackSamples`, `alertmanagerObserved`, `grafanaObserved`; answered tools leave `unmatchedTools`.
- **Annotations.** `buildCanonicalPack` (new params `stackSamples`, `alertmanagerObserved`, `grafanaObserved`, all `null`-safe — a caller that predates step 2 writes nothing) emits `mcp.stack.status`, `mcp.stack.reason` (only when not attempted), `mcp.stack.sampled` / `empty` / `failed` / `notInInventory` / `notAttempted`, `mcp.stack.families` (`<family>:<best outcome>`), `mcp.observed.stack_metrics` (attempted + not-in-inventory rows, cap 64), `mcp.observed.alertmanager`, `mcp.observed.grafana.datasources`, `mcp.observed.grafana.contact_points`, and lists the answering tools in `mcp.toolsCalled`. No `mcp.verified.*` or `mcp.scaffold.*` key is touched by any of it (pinned by the suite).
- **Surfaces.** `POST /api/draft-from-mcp` answers `summary.stack` (`status`, `reason`, the five counts as numbers, `families: { family: best outcome }`, `rows: [{ id, family, product, value, unit, direction, outcome, hint, reason? }]` — `hint` is the contracts' display-only `displayHint`, computed on read and never stored), `summary.alertmanager` and `summary.grafana` (each `null` when not exposed), and warns `Stack self-metrics not attempted — metrics_query not exposed by this MCP tier.` on a restricted tier; `GET /api/live-status` adds `stackStatus` and `stackSampled`. The studio draft summary gains a "stack self-metrics — point-in-time sample, signal not verdict" block: one row per family with the best row's formatted value (or `— empty` / `— probe failed` / `— not in inventory`), a single `— not attempted` row on a restricted tier, then the Alertmanager version + active silences, the Grafana datasource count with the unhealthy names, and the contact-point count. `liveEvidenceFacts` adds `stack { status, reason, sampled, empty, failed, notAttempted }`, carried on the journey run record and printed as one `Stack self-metrics` line in the markdown report — no gate key reads it.
- **Fixtures: synthetic now, recorded later.** `tools/fixtures/mcp/synthetic/` holds hand-written samples for each new shape (an otel-mcp-server and a Prometheus-API instant vector, Alertmanager status and silences, Grafana datasources, datasource health, contact points), each carrying a `_synthetic` marker the shape check tolerates and `tools/test-contract-shapes.mjs` asserts; they pin shape only. The fixtures README now carries a **provenance table** (recorded 2026-06-12 from the Krystaline otel-mcp-server vs synthetic 2026-09-07; the recorded metric-name list is trimmed to 25 names and proves none of the alias table's `requires`), the recorder procedure, and what to re-run afterwards. A recorded `<tool>.json` at the fixtures top level takes precedence over its synthetic copy in the shapes suite (same assertions, plus "no `_synthetic` marker"), and every file in `recorded-stack/` must be a valid instant vector for a declared row.
- **Recorder.** `tools/record-mcp-fixtures.mjs` (`npm run record-fixtures`, `MCP_URL` + optional `MCP_AUTH`) reuses the fetcher's JSON-RPC client and the contract registry for every tool name (the guard suite scans it for literals), is read-only against the MCP, and never prints or stores the token (every string that leaves the process is redacted). Default mode **reports**: the `tools/list` surface with its drift against `allKnownToolNames()`, the metric inventory, and for every alias of every row whether its `requires` are all present plus the value read with `sampleFromInstantVector` (`data` / `empty` / `FAILED` / `not-in-inventory (missing …)` / `not-attempted (restricted tier)`), then the status tools and probe families with their shape verdicts. `-- --write` records: the full inventory to the git-ignored `.tmp-mcp-metric-names.json`, the trimmed inventory (every required name and the `*_build_info` metrics kept, plus the first 25 others, in the server's order, replaced where the adapter found the list), scrape targets / the winning rule tool(s) / dashboard search with every list capped at 6 and structure verbatim, one `metrics_query` instant vector per family under `recorded-stack/<row id>.json` with `_recorded` provenance, and the status tools when advertised; `--out <dir>` redirects everything. New scripts `lint:recorder`, `test:recorder`.
- **First live recording (2026-09-07, public Krystaline tier, `https://www.krystaline.io/mcp/public`, no credentials).** The recorder's first real run replaced the guesswork for part of the table: that tier advertises 43 tools including `metrics_query`, `metrics_label_values` (2,682 names), `alertmanager_status` and `alertmanager_silences`, so `metrics_label_values.json` is now a 40-name recording that evidences `up`, `scrape_duration_seconds`, `vm_*`, `alertmanager_*`, `otelcol_exporter_queue_*`, `grafana_http_request_duration_seconds_count` and `promtail_dropped_entries_total`; `alertmanager_status.json` is recorded (its `config` field, the full Alertmanager configuration, omitted by hand); and `recorded-stack/` holds six real `metrics_query` instant vectors (otel-mcp-server answers a flat `{ status, resultType, result }` envelope). Ten aliases read `data`, one `empty`, twenty-six `not-in-inventory` — the stack runs VictoriaMetrics, not Prometheus, and does not scrape vmalert (its ruler's own health is invisible there: exactly the blind spot the sampler exists to name). Every Grafana-backed tool except `grafana_health` answers `HTTP 401` on that tier (no Grafana credentials), so the Grafana status fixtures stay synthetic until the authenticated tier is recorded; the silences recording was empty and pins nothing, so its synthetic copy stays too. The 2026-06-12 `metrics_targets.json` / `vmalert_rules.json` / `grafana_dashboards_search.json` recordings were kept on purpose (today's targets are all `up`; the `down` Alertmanager target is the liveness case the adapters are pinned against). Contract fix found by the run: `grafana_dashboard_get` was listed as a **search** candidate of the `dashboards` probe, so a failed search fell through to a uid-less call whose `-32602 Invalid arguments` error masked the real search error; it is now only the `dashboard_detail` enrichment tool, and the family reports the 401 it actually got. Suites replay a recorded status fixture in preference to its synthetic copy and read expected values from the fixture they replayed, and the recorder suite's fake inventory is frozen to the 2026-06-12 names so re-recording never moves its expectations.
- **Authenticated-tier recording (2026-09-08, `https://www.krystaline.io/mcp`, `MCP_AUTH` bearer).** The recorder's first run against the authenticated Krystaline tier re-recorded `vmalert_rules.json` (the 2026-06-12 recording predated most of today's rule groups; adapted goldens re-pinned; its annotations carry rule descriptions and runbook URLs into the private krystalinex-core repo — references, not credentials, and the identical payload the credential-less public tier serves), `alertmanager_status.json` (`_recorded.scrubbed` names the hand-removed `config` — receivers, SMTP settings, internal hostnames — same trim as the 2026-09-07 file it replaces) and the six `recorded-stack/` instant vectors (same rows and outcomes as the public tier: 14 aliases `data` · 0 `failed`; the trimmed `metrics_label_values.json` recording came back byte-identical to the committed public-tier file, and both tiers report a 2,682-name inventory — the full lists were not compared). The intended goal — replacing the synthetic Grafana fixtures — was not reachable: the tier advertises **no Grafana-backed tools with any issued key** (its otel-mcp-server deployment carries no Grafana integration; the public tier advertises them but answers HTTP 401), so `synthetic/grafana_*.json` and the 2026-06-12 `grafana_dashboards_search.json` stay, `metrics_targets.json` keeps its 2026-06-12 `down`-target recording (the 2026-09-08 payload is all-`up`), and the empty silences recording again pins nothing. The tier also advertises logs and k8s tools the registry has no rows for (noted in the fixtures README; no registry rows added).
- **Local-stack recording of the Grafana-backed tools (2026-09-08, `docker/stack.compose.yaml` fronted by otel-mcp-server 1.8.0).** The fourth evidence source, and today the only place `grafana_datasources`, `grafana_datasource_health` and `grafana_contact_points` answer: the pinned stack (Grafana 12.4.4, admin-seeded with a `VictoriaMetrics (stack)` datasource, a `Loki (absent)` datasource pointing at a service the stack does not run, the `webhook-oncall` contact point and one dashboard) behind a local otel-mcp-server started with `GRAFANA_URL` + `GRAFANA_AUTH_BASIC` and no MCP auth keys, recorded through `MCP_URL=http://127.0.0.1:3011/mcp npm run record-fixtures -- --write --out .tmp-recorded-local`. The three `synthetic/grafana_*.json` files are gone, replaced by recordings whose `_recorded` carries `scrubbed: []` with what was reviewed (`jsonData` / `secureJsonFields` empty; compose hostnames and the loopback Grafana URL inside a failed check's error kept verbatim) and the server line, added by hand. Two shapes the hand-written files had wrong: **`grafana_datasource_health`** answers `{ datasource, health }` — `health` is `{ supported: true, status: 'OK' | 'ERROR', message, details }` when Grafana's endpoint answered 2xx and `{ supported: false, error: 'HTTP 400: Bad Request — <url>' }` when it did not (Grafana 12.4.4 answers a check that ran and failed, backend unreachable, with `HTTP 400`; an unknown uid with `500`) — so the `health-object` contract now locates `['health', 'data', '']` and accepts a verdict key or the `supported` / `error` pair (one key group: the checker requires every group and the two forms share no key), and `observeGrafana` reads `OK` → `ok`, `ERROR` → `error`, `supported: false` + `HTTP 400` → `error` with the error text as message, any other `supported: false` → `unknown` (not checked, never "not unhealthy"), `supported: true` without a status → `unknown`; and **`grafana_contact_points`** answers the receivers API (`{ count, contactPoints: [{ name, active, integrations: [{ name, sendResolved, lastNotifyAttempt, lastNotifyAttemptDuration }] }] }` — no `uid`, `type` or `settings`, so no webhook URL or secret ever rides in it), not the provisioning shape. The recorder keeps both datasource-health verdicts when several uids answer (the first uid's answer as `grafana_datasource_health.json`; the first answer of the other case as `.ok.json` / `.error.json` with its uid and case, skipped when identical to the primary file). Suites replay the recording with expected values read from the files: `test-contract-shapes` pins both health cases and the envelope location, `test-fetch-live` drives `observeGrafana` with the recorded uids and asserts the unhealthy datasources name exactly `Loki (absent)` (plus the non-400 and no-status `unknown` cases), the smoke and end-to-end fakes answer health per uid, and the recorder suite's host guard is the fake's own host:port. `grafana_dashboards_search.json` keeps its richer 2026-06-12 recording (the stack answers the same `{ count, results }` shape).
- **Live validation tier (2026-09-07, the real products in Docker).** The public MCP tier could only evidence what that stack happens to scrape; `docker/stack.compose.yaml` (`name: observogram-stack`, loopback-only ports 19090 / 19093 / 18428 / 18880 / 18888+14318 / 13030 / 19115 / 19080 / 14269, disjoint from the validate stack) now runs every product the table names at an exactly pinned version — Prometheus v2.55.1 scraping all of them plus a blackbox probe job, Alertmanager v0.27.0 with a dead webhook receiver, VictoriaMetrics v1.113.0 scraping itself and a dead target, vmalert v1.113.0, otel-collector-contrib 0.115.1 with a `debug` exporter beside an OTLP exporter to a dead endpoint (queue + bounded retries), Grafana 12.4.4 with a provisioned Prometheus datasource and one provisioned always-firing alert rule, blackbox-exporter v0.25.0, promtail 3.3.2 tailing a sample file into a dead Loki, and Jaeger all-in-one 1.62.0 — every ruler carrying one recording rule and one always-firing alert so the failure and notification counters move on a fresh stack (`up -d --wait` returns in ~10s). `tools/test-stack-live.mjs` (`npm run test:stack:live[:strict]`, NOT in `npm test` — it needs Docker and skips loudly without it) brings the stack up, recreates the collector and Grafana so neither carries a previous run's stimulus, waits for every scrape job to be up and for the rate windows to hold two samples, then for **every alias of every row**: (a) asserts each `requires` name is a metric family on the product's own exposition (`/metrics`; the Prometheus TSDB name inventory for the scrape-synthesised `up` / `scrape_duration_seconds`, which never appear on Prometheus' own `/metrics`; the blackbox `/probe` output for `probe_success`; the vmalert service for `vmalert_*`), recording separately the presence of any name the `expr` reads that `requires` does not demand (the lazily-registered-counter policy's shape); (b) evaluates the `expr` on the real Prometheus and reads the answer through the fetcher's own `sampleFromInstantVector` (`data` / `empty`; a PromQL error is a FAIL naming the error); (c) records the collector's name set at startup, sends one OTLP/HTTP request per signal kind (and one query through Grafana's datasource proxy), and records the name set again.
